# kuna history

The condensed project history. This file replaces four retired doc trees —
`docs/history/` (frozen design docs + campaign logs), `docs/rust-port/` (port plan, ADRs,
losses ledger, verification protocol), `docs/divergences.md` (DIV registry), and
`docs/UPSTREAM.md` (vendored-tree provenance) — all deleted 2026-07-28; their full text
lives in git history before that date. Code comments citing `decompiler/cpp/<file>.{cc,hh}`
refer to the retired vendored C++ tree (recoverable from the pinned upstream commit below);
citations of `LOSS-NNN`, `UB-N`, or paths under `docs/rust-port/` and `docs/history/`
(in code comments or `phases.toml` prose) refer to the retired
ledgers and design docs summarized here.

## The Rust port: how it was verified

The port's central rule: the vendored C++ tree stayed **byte-for-byte untouched as the
oracle** the entire time — every claim of correctness was differential against an unmoved
reference, never self-graded. The Rust side reproduced the exact `decomp_dbg` /
`decomp_test_dbg` console command surface, so the existing XML datatests and harness
verified the port *unchanged* via an `--engine {cpp,rust}` switch.

**Structure.** 91 port items + 91 paired verify items + 18 infra items (182,926 LOC of
scope), each pinned to the C++ source with `git hash-object` blob shas. Items were grouped
into waves W0–W11, each wave fanned out to parallel agents in isolated git worktrees and
serially integrated under a wave gate; a gate passed only when every item's verify was
ACCEPTed. Bodies were line-faithful transliterations (same algorithms, iteration order,
comparators, registration order); only the shell (ownership, `Result`, crate layout) was
idiomatic. Determinism was enforced structurally: `BTreeMap`/`BTreeSet` only (`HashMap`
banned workspace-wide via clippy `disallowed_types`), stable sorts, mandatory wrapping
helpers, explicit widths.

**The porter/verifier split.** Every port item got an adversarial verifier that was
structurally independent: it received only the C++ source at the pinned blob sha, the Rust
diff, fresh gate output, and the checklist entry — never the porter's reasoning. Each
verdict had to check off a mandatory hunt list (signedness, integer widths/promotions,
wrapping, comparator totality, iteration-order provenance for every loop, do-while/
`lower_bound` boundaries, erase-while-iterating equivalence, exception→`Result`
partial-state parity) and write **≥3 new adversarial tests** targeting the fragile spots —
tests landed regardless of verdict. Verdicts were ACCEPT / ACCEPT-WITH-LOSSES (every
divergence cited in the losses ledger) / REJECT (required a failing test or a concrete
divergence trace against the C++ oracle; three REJECTs blocked the item for human
decision).

**How bugs were found.** Layered differential evidence, finest to coarsest:
golden vectors for opbehavior/float/comparators/XML; per-instruction SLEIGH lift-diff over
the corpus; stage-boundary snapshots B0–B5 (pipeline registration, lift, post-flow CFG,
post-heritage SSA, final IR, printed C) pinned byte-equal to C++; the 207 upstream unit
tests transcribed 1:1; and the 675 datatest assertions with **monotonicity** enforced —
a per-assertion passing-set diff at every wave meant no previously-passing assertion ever
regressed. The W10 "parity grind" root-caused each remaining divergence with dual-engine
traces; the ledger records wrong theses corrected by tracing (the dual-AddrSpaceManager
keystone, LOSS-132, made every analysis pass see zero varnodes) and one **vacuous parity
claim REJECTED** by a verifier (19/675 where all 19 were empty-negative assertions). The
SLEIGH compiler had its own oracle: `sleigh_opt` is deterministic, so 148/148 `.sla`
outputs were compared as decompressed element streams, then all specs were rebuilt with
the Rust compiler and the full suite re-run. Only after all gates held was the C++ tree
deleted.

**Cost.** The port-and-verification campaign ran roughly **two weeks of near-constant LLM
time** (2026-06-05 vendoring → 06-20 C++ removal, port proper 06-10 → 06-19) and cost
approximately **$8k** in API spend.

**Ledgers.** `losses.md` was the append-only accountability spine: ~250 numbered entries
(LOSS-001–252) recording every deliberate departure at decision time — deferrals (81),
faithful-to-C++-UB divergences (58), seams (24), dependency substitutions (9: zlib→flate2,
libbfd→`object`, bison/flex→hand parsers, std::regex→`regex`), and diagnosis journals.
Every parity-blocking entry was closed en route to 675/675; what stayed open is permanent
by design (deterministic iteration order replacing C++ heap-pointer order; debug-panics
where C++ relies on x86 shift-masking UB; content-equality rather than byte-equality for
deflate). The port also surfaced **six latent bugs in upstream Ghidra** (UB-1..UB-5):
`opcode_name[]` OOB read at `CPUI_MAX`, SIGFPE on `INT64_MIN / -1` in IntSdiv/IntSrem,
signed overflow in XML `convertCharRef`, a dangling-record read in `rangemap::erase`, a
`MemoryBank` page-copy overrun, and a sort-order violation in the pcode-snippet keyword
table making `||`/`abs` unlexable — all reproduced faithfully or fail-closed in Rust,
pinned by tests.

**Architecture decisions (ADR one-liners).** 0001: IR in slotmap generational arenas
owned by `Funcdata` (no `Rc<RefCell>`). 0002: ordered containers only, C++ comparators
transcribed field-by-field. 0003: `uintb→u64`/`intb→i64` with mandatory wrapping helpers.
0004: `Result<KunaError>` mirroring `LowlevelError`, caught at the same frames. 0005:
`universalAction` as a declarative SchedNode table, observably identical to C++ `clone()`.
0006: stage tables in `phases.toml` with build.rs codegen, byte-compatible catalog JSON.
0007: typed P0 store + per-stage artifact versioning for rerunability
(`GhidraExact` restart semantics by default).

## Divergences from upstream Ghidra (DIV registry)

A DIV entry records every **intentional change to default behavior** vs upstream (bug
fixes or evidence-backed default flips), with its measured effect on the 675 upstream
assertions. Byte-identical option-gated exposures need no DIV. Entries are append-only;
new default flips add a row here (full original entries with evidence: git history of
`docs/divergences.md`). Pre-rename entries say "stage"/S-codes for phase/P-codes.

| DIV | Option/area | What changed | Evidence/effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| DIV-1 | parenthesization (no flag) | `-(-x)` not `--x` (re-parses as pre-decrement) | 0/675 |
| DIV-2 | `compareform original`, `arraynotation`, `thumbfuncptr`, `inferfuncentry`, `booleanmask`, `addcarrychain`, `ovlesssimplify`, `memsetrecover` | 8 issue-fix options default-on (GH-558/8471/6930/1282/7190/8913/9230/1537) | 22/675 re-pinned (15 files) |
| DIV-3 | `flagcompare`, `stackprobeloop`, `dynamichashmax`, `arraystride`, `condexeplace`, `inputvarnodeadjust` | 6 more issue-fix options default-on | 0/675 |
| DIV-4 | `loweredswitch` | angr LoweredSwitchSimplifier: comparison cascades → `switch` | 0/675 (binary-search guard) |
| DIV-5 | `namestyle angr` | angr-style naming (`v1`/`a0`/`dat_`/`sub_`/`label_`) + storage decl comments | 185/675 re-pinned; `namestyle ghidra` restores |
| DIV-6 | `realtypes` | `xunknownN`/`undefinedN` render as real C types | print-only; 0/675 |
| DIV-7 | `dedupvardecls` | duplicate scalar local decls collapsed | 0/675; +0.14% speed |
| DIV-8 | `relocobjects` | ELF `ET_REL` `.o` loading (relocations + extern binding); extended by DIV-70 to COFF `.obj` | new capability; 0/675 |
| DIV-9 | `i386_pie_plt` | i386-PIE PLT stub decode → named imports, no-return works | 0/675; `usage` −69% time |
| DIV-10 | `loopbreak_recovery` | loop-exit gotos become `break;` (converges toward upstream) | 0/675 |
| DIV-11 | libc proto table (no flag) | `setlocale` prototype added | 0/675 |
| DIV-12 | `regionstructure` | angr Phoenix/SAILR region structurer is the default structuring path | 0/675 (CollapseStructure fallback) |
| DIV-13 | `noreturn_externmatch` | name-matched no-return externs in `ET_REL` | 0/675; ~23% faster on chcon.o |
| DIV-14 | `gotoreduce`, `crossjumprevert`, `taildup`, `dedupitetail`, `regionlooprefine`, `ifelseflatten`, `switchmultipred`, `tailcalljump`, `foldcallret`, `branchflip`, `stackguard`, `noreturn_extern`, `noreturn_propagate`, `switchsharedcase` | 14-flag angr SAILR structuring/readability sweep default-on (3 flags remove code) | 0/675 via per-test opt-outs in 12 files |

| DIV-18 | `returndup` — **REVERTED** 2026-07-05, **SUPERSEDED by DIV-54** | early-return duplication was default-on; decbench measured −976 aggregate GED regression | opt-in runtime choice until DIV-54; **the −976 is superseded** — it was measured on the *unselective* pass, #137's const-return gate reversed it, and a 2026-08-02 isolating ablation over 52,862 optimized functions measures +417 GED-perfect / −7,756 aggregate GED (`docs/decbench/returndup-regression-triage.md`) |
| DIV-19 | `noreturn_reach` | CFG-reachability no-return discovery (+ 2026-07-08 hot/cold-split fix) | Listing-gated |

| DIV-33 | executable batch targets (bug fix, no flag) | unfiltered `decompile-all`/`decompile-project`/wasm runs skip callable symbols in data sections while preserving them for naming, inventory, and explicit address selection | 0/675 (analysis tier is parity-isolated); private PE `bc4c15d8…3ae1b` 693→351 targets and 63.58→7.78 s; all 351 executable artifacts byte-identical |
| DIV-34 | `braceformat function next` (upstream option, new default) | no blank line between a function prototype and its `{` (upstream skip_line renders `)\n\n{`); `option braceformat function skip` restores | 0/675; print-only |
| DIV-35 | `nullprinting on` (upstream option, new default) | zero pointer constants render as `NULL` instead of `(type *)0x0` (upstream option_NULL default off); `option nullprinting off` restores | 675/675 via 1 datatest opt-out (forloop_loaditer); 2 stage asserts re-worded; print-only |
| DIV-36 | `inplaceops on` (upstream option, new default + ported consumer) | `out = out OP y` statements render as `out OP= y` (the upstream `emitInplaceOp` consumer of option_inplace_ops was never wired; kuna ports it), `x += -c` folds to `x -= c`; comma contexts (for-headers) exempt; `option inplaceops off` restores | 675/675 via 9 datatest opt-outs; 1 stage assert re-worded; print-only |
| DIV-37 | `truthycond` (new kuna option, default-on) | boolean-context zero comparisons render truthy (`if (x != 0)` → `if (x)`, `if (p == NULL)` → `if (!p)`) in if/while/for/ternary conditions and `&&`/`\|\|`/`!` operands; float/enum/equate zeros and value uses keep the explicit form; `option truthycond off` restores | 675/675 via 7 datatest opt-outs; 9 stage asserts re-worded (1 opt-out: branchflip polarity test); print-only |
| DIV-38 | `braceelide` (new kuna option, default-on) | a single-statement if body drops its braces (statement indented on the next line); Copy-leaf bodies only (labels/comments/multi-statement/else arms keep braces, dangling-else impossible); `option braceelide off` restores | 675/675 via 4 datatest opt-outs (copytrim, ifswitch + boolless/condconst oracle pins); 1 stage assert re-worded; print-only |
| DIV-39 | `warnstyle` (new kuna option, default `inline`) | analysis warnings render as terse `// slug` end-of-line comments on the line they describe (`usage(1); // no-return`, `if (v8) { // branch-flip`, prototype-line `// early-return x3`) instead of full `/* WARNING: ... */` banner lines; unknown texts keep full info behind `warn:`; `option warnstyle banner` restores | 675/675 via 3 datatest opt-outs (inline, inlinetarget, switchreturn); 14 stage asserts re-worded to slug forms; print-only |
| DIV-40 | file front-ends default to `mode auto` | raw inputs `<500 KiB` select `aggressive`, `500 KiB–<2 MiB` select `reliable`, and `>=2 MiB` select `fast`; explicit `--mode reliable\|aggressive\|fast` restores a fixed policy | 0/675 (datatest console has no file-policy default); private PE `bc4c15d8…3ae1b` resolves to `fast`: 3.23 s median / 351 of 351 functions versus default-analysis control incomplete after 618.60 s; web and native share exact boundary tests |
| DIV-41 | `fast` and `aggressive` presets: `fast_funcdisc on` | correct the metadata-only fast inventory with a rooted recursive Listing walk plus conservatively validated absolute pointer-table roots (4096-candidate ceiling on non-ARM); full prologue patterns, AIF gap scanning, and ordinary Listing consumers remain off in `fast`; `aggressive` includes the option through its all-safe-off-options contract; address-selected commands retain their exact scope and skip the preset-provided walk | 0/675 (analysis tier parity-isolated); private PE `bc4c15d826aaebeace3fec6360eb687e5662cba8745605093254931dcdb3ae1b` previously exported 350 import veneers plus only its entry and omitted real `0x402d80` code; corrected `--max-fn-seconds 10` export: 462.28 s / 1,475 MiB peak RSS / 3,140 bodies + 13 isolated failures (3,153 definitions); public `pdb_prog.exe` recovers direct callee `0x140001000`, and `aif_gap_x86_64` recovers indirect-only target `0x13ae`, both with nonempty C bodies |
| DIV-42 | unfiltered `fast` whole-binary watchdog defaults to 10 seconds per function | native `decompile-all`/`decompile-project` and WASM whole-binary decompile/project isolate probed pathological functions after 10 s instead of inheriting the native 120 s default; native selectors retain 120 s and explicit `--max-fn-seconds` wins, including `0` | 0/675 (driver policy only); private PE `bc4c15d8…3ae1b` no-flag confirmation: 445.06 s / 1,511,288 KiB peak RSS / 3,140 bodies + 13 isolated failures (3,153 definitions); public native/WASM policy matrices plus Node-WASI and browser-shim fast-project coverage |
| DIV-43 | `aggressive` preset drops `dwarf_lines` (bug fix, no new flag) | the `.debug_line` pass annotates rather than recovers, so blanket-enabling it under `aggressive` — which `auto` selects below 500 KiB (DIV-40) — made `/* src.c:NNN */` per-instruction comments the DEFAULT rendering of every small `-g` binary; it joins `v850indirectbranch` as a manual `--option dwarf_lines on` opt-in, which still wins over the preset | 0/675 (mode presets are not a datatest surface); coreutils `fmt` (219 KiB, resolves to `aggressive`) `main` 321 → 179 lines with 140 source-line comments gone, `decompile-all` 7,106 → 5,299 lines with 1,740 gone; non-comment output identical apart from the `braceelide` single-statement bodies the comments had been suppressing; runtime unchanged within noise (4.36 s vs 4.80 s median of 3) |
| DIV-44 | Cortex-M vector-table oracle scans program-header-executable sections (bug fix, no flag) | the vector table is DATA the CPU reads, but the oracle only looked at `SHF_EXECINSTR` sections, so a firmware whose `.isr_vector` is flagged `WA` inside an `RWE` `PT_LOAD` — every FreeRTOS demo image — never matched, never region-painted `TMode=1`, and lost its whole Thumb call tree; the candidate set now also covers allocated sections contained in a `PF_X` `PT_LOAD` (`phdr_executable_sections`), with the acceptance test (SRAM initial SP, `word[1] == e_entry`, odd Thumb handlers landing in executable memory) unchanged | 0/675; decbench freertos `RTOSDemo.out` 8 → 146 (O0), 8 → 118 (O2), 9 → 147 (O2-noinline) functions, byte-identical to the control experiment that hand-flipped `.isr_vector` to `AX`; unchanged on coreutils `ls` (617), betaflight STM32F405 (5,798 `aggressive`), crazyflie (2,790), cleanflight (2,840), and on relocatable objects (no program headers) |
| DIV-45 | `kuna decompile` exits non-zero on a per-function pipeline abort (bug fix, no new flag) | three compounding defects made a failed function look like a success: the caught panic payload was passed as `&Box<dyn Any>` (so both downcasts failed and every `LOSS-131` record read "panic with non-string payload"), the CLI surfaced the console's stderr only when the emitted C was *empty* (an abort still renders the un-decompiled shell, so it never was), and the shell's comment blamed structuring. Now the payload round-trips, the emitted comment names the real reason (`/* WARNING: decompilation failed: … */`; the generic "structuring declined" text stays for a genuine no-`sblocks` render), and `kuna decompile` prints `error: decompilation failed for <fn> in <binary>: <reason>` plus the forwarded `decomp_dbg` stderr and exits 1 — still printing the recovered shell on stdout. `decompile-all`/`decompile-project`/wasm are unchanged (their per-function `error` field already isolated it, and now carries the real text) | 0/675 and stages PARITY OK (no datatest function aborts, so the console arm is inert); coreutils `comm` `sub_3994` @ 0x3994 before: exit 0, empty stderr, `/* structuring declined at a stub */`; after: exit 1, the real reason ("called Option::unwrap() on a None value") on stderr with the `jumptable.rs:1811` panic site forwarded |

| DIV-46 | unnamed spacebase references render as stack locations (bug fix, no new flag) | a `PTRSUB(spacebase, off)` whose offset P6 bound no Symbol to fell out of the P9 SPACEBASE arm into the *functional* render, emitting the internal p-code operator and a raw machine register into the C body (`*(void *)&PTRSUB(ESP,8)[v2] = 0;` — neither `PTRSUB` nor `ESP` is declared anywhere, so the function is not C); it now takes the upstream `PrintC::pushUnnamedLocation` leaf the arm had been declining, naming the storage in kuna's existing unnamed-location convention (`&Stack00000008`, the analogue of Ghidra's `&stack0x00000008`, undeclared extern-like by design). The symbol-bound path is untouched | 0/675 (no datatest reaches a symbol-less spacebase PTRSUB) and 305/305 stages; 217 of 13,759 functions across 12 decbench binaries emitted `PTRSUB(` before, 0 after (mydoom 4/161, betaflight 109/6,388, cleanflight 72/3,118, mirai 12/181, the rest 1–4 each); interleaved medians `decompile-all --mode reliable`: mydoom 1,553 → 1,563 ms (+0.66%), gzip 2,939 → 2,952 ms (+0.44%), cleanflight 24,453 → 24,339 ms (-0.47%); `tests/stages/ghdec-spacebase-unnamed.xml` (0/6 pre-fix, 6/6 post-fix) |
| DIV-47 | P6 Cover extension restored (bug fix, no new flag) | two dropped cover-extension steps in the merge port each emitted C that computes a **different value** than the binary. (a) `Funcdata::build_copy_pair_range` omitted `Cover::addRefPoint` (`merge.cc:1121`), so `Merge::checkCopyPair`'s dominance range was a point, no intervening write was ever found inside it, and `markRedundantCopies` silenced load-bearing restores — a `-O0` guard-clause function returned a failed call's NULL instead of its parameter, and `for` loops lost their initializer. (b) `ActionMarkImplied` dropped `Merge::markImplied`'s operand cover-dirtying (`merge.cc:1595-1605`) and a Varnode `coverdirty` never reached its HighVariable (`Varnode::setFlags`, `varnode.cc:377-378`), so Covers were never regrown through implied consumers and the speculative merges folded two values live at the same printed expression into one variable | 675/675 PARITY OK and 305/305 stages with no re-pin or opt-out; both directions are upstream-faithful restorations, not new policy; `verify_cover_miscompile` (fixture `covercopy_x86_64`) pins the value-carrying statement for each; `verify_declared_vars`' sanity floor drops 5→4 because (b) lets the size phi coalesce with `strlen` as upstream does |


| DIV-48 | the `realtypes` relabel keeps an unknown pointee's size (bug fix, no new flag) | `realtype_unknown_base` short-circuited to `void` for EVERY size under a pointer, so `undefined1 *`, `undefined4 *` and `undefined8 *` all rendered `void *` — but only the *declaration* was relabelled: `opLoad`/`opPtradd` still build index and cast expressions from the ORIGINAL pointee size, so the declaration contradicted its own body (`void *a3` alongside `a3[1]`, which means byte offset 1 under GCC's void-arithmetic extension where the expression was built for offset 8) and a store cast down to `*(void *)` lost its access width entirely. The same size table a scalar uses now applies to the pointee (`undefined8 *` → `unsigned long *`, `undefined4 *` → `unsigned int *`), and `void` survives only as the **fallback** for residual sizes with no natural single C type (0, 3, 5, 6, 7, 10, …). P5 never mis-typed anything — `option realtypes off` already produced the stride-consistent `xunknown8 *a3` — and a genuine `TYPE_VOID` pointee is not a residual unknown, so `free`/`malloc`/`memcpy`/`realloc` keep their opaque `void *` | 0/675 and 321/321 stages; libacl `set_acl_fd` (the case that surfaced it) before: `void *a3` with `(*(code *)a3[1])(a3,a0)` — `gcc -std=gnu11 -fsyntax-only` gives 12 "invalid use of void expression" errors + 12 "dereferencing 'void *' pointer" warnings; after: `unsigned long *a3`, same `a3[1]`, 0 errors 0 warnings, matching the source (`struct error_context` is three function pointers at +0/+8/+16). Across 5 whole-binary `decompile-all --mode reliable` runs (libacl, gzip, mydoom, mirai, coreutils `fmt`): `*(void *)` cast sites 737 → 1, `void *` occurrences 1,585 → 198, emitted line counts byte-for-byte unchanged; the single residual is a 10-byte x87-`long double` pointee, where `void` is the designed fallback. Least-contended-run timings (`decompile-all --mode reliable`, 7 interleaved pairs on a loaded box): gzip 2,924 → 2,939 ms (+0.51%), libacl 792 → 793 ms (+0.13%), mirai 4,651 → 4,582 ms (-1.48%); `tests/stages/ghdec-realtypes-pointee.xml` (3/7 pre-fix, 7/7 post-fix) |
| DIV-49 | structured components print in final order (bug fix, no new flag) | `ActionFinalStructure` never called `BlockGraph::orderBlocks` (`block.hh:437`, the first line of `blockaction.cc:2192`), so whenever structuring left more than one top-level component the printer walked the root list in **collapse order** — whichever component the collapse happened to append first. The function then opened in the middle of its body and reached its real entry only after an unconditional `goto`, i.e. emitted the entry as unreachable dead code (`libopencm3` `sdram` `main` opened on the `'p'`/`'P'` command handler and printed `clock_setup(); console_setup(); sdram_init();` after `goto label_8000792;`). The producer of the ordering key was already ported (`BlockGraph::add_block` keeps a composite `index` at the minimum over its members); only the sort was missing. It now runs, keyed on `FlowBlock::compareFinalOrder` (`block.cc:709`): entry component (`index == 0`) first, RETURN-terminated components last, everything else by ascending index, root graph only (upstream does not recurse), stable so the RETURN-terminated components upstream's comparator reports as equal keep a deterministic order. Applies to both structuring engines — `CollapseStructure` and the default-on region structurer hand `ActionFinalStructure` the same root list | 675/675 PARITY OK and 324/324 stages with no re-pin and no per-test opt-out (the stages delta is purely the 6 new keys); across 12 decbench binaries / 3,434 functions the "unconditional `goto` followed by a statement" signature goes 25 -> 0 and "body opens with a label" 29 -> 3 (the 3 residual labels sit at the entry block's own address, a legitimate back-edge target); blast radius 30 of 3,434 functions (0.87%) change text at all, and the whole changed set keeps its goto count and line count exactly (1,172 gotos / 16,123 lines before and after) — the emitted statements are a permutation; interleaved medians, `decompile-all --mode reliable`: O0 diffutils `diff` (526 functions) 7.38 s -> 7.07 s, O2 `bzip2` (113 functions) 15.06 s -> 15.18 s (+0.8%); `tests/stages/ghdec-finalorder-entryfirst.xml` (4 of 6 assertions fail pre-fix, 6/6 post-fix) |
| DIV-50 | renaming reconciles a full-size input with `guardInput`'s leftover pieces (bug fix, no new flag) | gnulib's `rpl_fcntl` at `-O2` produced **no body at all** in 48 of 267 optimized x86-64 binaries (`rename_recurse: set_input_varnode (empty stack): Lowlevel { explain: "Overlapping input varnodes" }`), one function per binary. `Heritage::renameRecurse` materializes an input Varnode for a read with no reaching definition; here that storage is already tiled by the *write-masked* input pieces a previous `Heritage::guardInput` left behind (it tiles a partially-input range, write-masks each piece so `Heritage::collect` stops seeing them, and represents the range by a PIECE concatenation that the rule pools later fold away), so `Funcdata::setInputVarnode` raised `LowlevelError("Overlapping input varnodes")` and the whole function was abandoned. This is **not** a port defect: Ghidra 12.1 headless raises the identical `Low-level Error: Overlapping input varnodes` on the same address of the same binary, which is why it is a DIV and not a Convergence. The leftover pieces are exactly the value the read wants, so the request is now completed into an end-to-end tiling (a fresh input for any hole, as `guardInput` does) and folded into one full-size input with the upstream `Funcdata::combineInputVarnodes` primitive, which destroys the pieces, rewrites concatenating PIECEs into COPYs and repoints every other reader at a SUBPIECE of the whole. Only write-masked pieces fully contained in the request are folded -- a write-masked Varnode is never pushed onto a `VariableStack`, so the rewrite cannot strand a renaming-stack entry -- and every other overlap still raises, exactly as upstream. (The recovered body still reads the spill slot as an undefined `stack - 0x38` local: keeping the va_list spill alive needs `Heritage::guardLoads`/`handleNewLoadCopies`, which kuna does not port and which Ghidra cannot demonstrate here because it fails on the same function.) | 675/675 PARITY OK and 346/346 stages with no re-pin and no per-test opt-out (the stages delta is purely the 8 new keys); across the 267-binary O2 decbench corpus (76,527 functions) the overlapping-input class goes 48 -> 0 functions in 48 -> 0 binaries and total error records 56 -> 8 (the 8 residuals are an unrelated "Unable to load 512 bytes" class, unchanged); 0 functions stop being produced and 48 start; whole-binary text diffs on gzip, coreutils `sort`, diffutils `diff`, findutils `find` and coreutils `nohup` change exactly 1 function each -- the one that used to fail; interleaved medians of 5, `decompile-all --mode reliable`: gzip 4,796 -> 4,792 ms (-0.08%), coreutils `sort` 13,477 -> 13,114 ms (-2.69%), findutils `find` 8,362 -> 8,279 ms (-0.99%); `tests/stages/ghdec-inputtile-overlap.xml` (1 of 8 assertions passes pre-fix, 8/8 post-fix) |
| DIV-51 | short-circuit `0`/`1` select diamonds re-roll into a boolean assignment (`iteboolean`, default-ON) | gcc at `-O0` materializes a source `x = a && b;` as a jump-based select whose constant-`0` arm is the SHARED target of every short-circuit exit, and kuna printed it as an explicit two-arm constant diamond (`if ((a0) && (v2 = a0[1] & 0x100, v2)) v2 = 1; else v2 = 0;`) where the source and Hex-Rays have one statement. P3 `RuleConditionalMove` is the rule that folds `MULTIEQUAL(0,1)` into `zext(cond)`, and its bail here is a **faithful port** of upstream Ghidra (`ruleaction.cc:9424-9439`, `if (inblock->sizeIn() != 1) return 0;` on each MULTIEQUAL input block) -- which is why this is a DIV and not a bug fix. Upstream's bail is also right at the IR level: the constant arm's extra predecessor is a DIFFERENT CBRANCH whose boolean does not dominate the join (folding to `zext(cond)` would be an SSA violation), and reconstructing the whole chain would hoist a guarded load (`p[1]` under `p != 0`) above its guard. The re-roll is therefore done at P8, after structuring, where the chain is already ONE `BlockCondition` that C's own `&&`/`||` renders with short-circuit semantics: `ActionIteBoolean` (`p8_structure/kuna_iteboolean.rs`, ELEM 4119) marks a 3-component gotoless `BlockIf` whose condition is that `BlockCondition` and whose two arms are each a single `COPY` of the constants `1`/`0` to the same integral storage, and `PrintC::emit_block_if_bool` emits `v = ( cond );` (arms `0`/`1` -> `v = !( cond );`). Print-only (an addl-flag on the condition's terminal CBRANCH; no p-code cloned or mutated), so `option iteboolean off` is byte-identical to the pre-DIV-51 render. Requiring the `BlockCondition` is what makes it VALUE-safe and not just shape-safe: `&&`/`||` and the negating `!` always yield exactly 0/1 in C, while a bare leaf condition renders truthy under DIV-37 (`if (x & 0x100)` prints `(x & 0x100)`, worth 0x100) and would store the wrong number -- that bare-leaf shape is also the one P3 already folds. Pointer/float/aggregate destinations, labelled arms, computed arms and multi-statement arms are all declined. Like `iteregion` (DIV-17) it stays a RUNTIME CHOICE: a source that really wrote `if (c) x = 1; else x = 0;` compiles to the same bytes | 675/675 PARITY OK with the flip and no re-pin (one stage test, `ghangr-earlyreturn`, takes a per-test `option iteboolean off` because its witness IS a 0/1 diamond); GED on the mined case `O0-bash-bash-time_command` 55 (recorded) / 26 (today's build) -> **6**, exactly the hand-rerolled pane the triage predicted (28 nodes / 43 edges vs the source's 28/44); corpus breadth over 14 `-O0` decbench binaries / 10,494 functions: 126 functions change text (1.2%) and 160 diamonds collapse (81 positive `v = ( a && b );`, 79 negated `v = !( ... );`), and a mechanical audit of all 160 confirms every emitted expression is boolean-valued at its top level; interleaved medians of 5, `decompile-all --mode reliable`: grep 7,308 -> 7,092 ms (-2.96%), gzip 2,563 -> 2,540 ms (-0.90%), diffutils `diff` 7,659 -> 7,431 ms (-2.98%) -- no measurable cost (the pass is one linear walk of the structured tree and mutates no p-code); `tests/stages/ghdec-iteboolean.xml` (2 of 4 assertions fail with the option off, 4/4 with it on) |
| DIV-52 | one local declaration per ScopeLocal Symbol (bug fix, no new flag) | `PrintC::emitLocalVarDecls` walks HighVariables where upstream `emitScopeVarDecls` walks the ScopeLocal **Symbol** table (printc.cc:2667/2696), which makes one symbol one declaration by construction. When two live ranges of one mapped stack slot did not merge, the shared-storage naming gave both the same name and P5 recovered different types for them, so the function opened with the *same identifier declared twice at two widths* — `unsigned int v12; // stack - 0x24` beside `uint4 v12; // stack - 0x24` in betaflight `applyLedFixedLayers`. That is not compilable C and leaves no way to tell which `v12` a body occurrence means (Ghidra emits one `undefined4 local_24;`, IDA one `int v23;`). The only collapse that existed, `option dedupvardecls` (DIV-7, default on), keys on the fully rendered **line**, so two spellings of one slot both survived by construction — the composite (array/struct/union) collapse beside it was already Symbol-keyed and the scalar case was the documented gap. The scalar collapse is now Symbol-keyed too, inside that same option: highs whose declaration representative resolves to the same containing ScopeLocal Symbol (base-byte containment, the `Funcdata::linkSymbol` query of `funcdata_varnode.cc:1190`) **and** that render the same identifier emit one declaration. Requiring the identifier to match is what makes the collapse incapable of removing the last declaration of a referenced name — the mirror-image failure `verify_declared_vars` was built for, now pinned in the same test. A group that disagrees about the type declares the Symbol's own type (upstream `emitVarDecl`'s `sym->getType()`, printc.cc:1719) unless that type is narrower than the widest storage the group covers, in which case the widest member wins: kuna's `ScopeLocal` ranges can be narrower than the accesses that reach them (upstream `restructure` would have grown the Symbol), and declaring an object smaller than the one the body writes through would be a fresh bug rather than a faithful one | 675/675 PARITY OK and 352/352 stages with no re-pin and no per-test opt-out (the stages delta is purely the 6 new keys); across 14 decbench binaries / 11,874 functions (x86-64 and ARM Cortex-M, -O0 and -O2) the functions emitting a duplicate local declaration go 18 -> 0, and the blast radius is exactly those 18 — no other function changes a byte, 21 declaration lines disappear (35,412 -> 35,391), no function loses a declared name and none gains one. Every survivor is at least as wide as every access it absorbed (`undefined3` / `unsigned short` / `char` shadows of a 4-byte slot become one `unsigned int`), and the campaign case declares `unsigned int v12; // stack - 0x24` exactly once, the same choice as Ghidra's single `undefined4 local_24`. The collapse only runs where two declarations share an identifier, so the Symbol lookups are skipped on almost every function; interleaved medians, `decompile-all --mode reliable`: betaflight STM32F405 (6,387 functions) 69,782 -> 69,754 ms (-0.04%, 5 pairs), bzip2 9,926 -> 9,863 ms (-0.63%, 5 pairs), gzip 3,162 -> 3,211 ms (+1.55%, 9 pairs). `tests/stages/ghdec-symbol-keyed-local-decls.xml` (4 of 6 assertions pass pre-fix, 6/6 post-fix) |
| DIV-53 | the browser inventory shares the decompile/project discovery policy (bug fix, no new flag) | `kuna_wasm list` — the command that fills the web UI's function sidebar — was the one surface that skipped the `decompile-all` driver injections (`listing`, plus the arch-gated `funcstart_patterns`/`aif` on a non-x86-64 target), inheriting `kuna functions`' cheap-enumeration trade. In the browser that trade is wrong in kind: the sidebar is the ONLY way to reach a function, so an entry the inventory omits is a function the UI cannot open at all, while the **same wasm module's** `project` button downloaded a `.c` full of functions the page had never listed. The `list` command now loads exactly as `decompile`/`project` do, so the inventory is by construction the project export's target set (canonical entries; import pointer slots stay in the inventory-only surplus, as DIV-33 defines). Only the `reliable` window (500 KiB–<2 MiB) was ever inconsistent — `aggressive` (<500 KiB) already turns all three options on and `fast` (>=2 MiB) turns all three off, and a mode that names an option still owns it | 0/675 and stages unchanged (the wasm front-end is not a datatest surface); private i386 PE `27d7b038…03ab5` (1,154,048 B, resolves to `reliable`): sidebar 308 entries (286 `plt` + 22 `func`) against its own project export of 3,015 functions — 2,850 of those 3,015 had no sidebar entry; after: 3,158 entries (1,952 `func` / 920 `thunk` / 286 `plt`) and 0 missing. Per-option contribution on that PE (canonical entries): baseline 308, `listing` 308, `aif` 308, `funcstart_patterns` 1,847, `listing`+`aif` 1,514, `listing`+`funcstart_patterns` 2,807, all three 3,158 — i.e. no single injection recovers it. Cost: native `list` 0.19 -> 3.25 s (47 -> 243 MB peak RSS); under Node WASI, the ABI the browser shim implements, 0.60 -> 4.29 s, against the 4.73 s one address decompile on the same binary already cost (that surface always ran the injections). x86-64 control `/bin/bash` (1,396,520 B, `reliable`): 2,503 -> 2,503 entries at 0.31 -> 2.18 s — on x86-64 the arch-gated scans stay off and oracle 5 already covers prologues, so that arm buys consistency, not entries. `--mode reliable` fixture deltas: `entrymain_arm` 10 -> 13, `entrymain_riscv64` 3 -> 6, `plt_sparc64` 17 -> 27, `plt_ppc64le` 14 -> 19, `i386_pie_nl` 139 -> 154, `entrymain_aarch64` 12 -> 13, `sample_aarch64.o` 6 -> 8, `fmt_riscv64` 8 -> 9, `plt_riscv64` 9 -> 10, every x86-64 fixture unchanged; web gates re-run green (parity 19/19 native==wasm byte-for-byte, glue/zip/auto-mode/worker OK); `kuna-wasm`'s `reliable_list_inventory_covers_the_project_export` pins the invariant (`entrymain_arm`: 2 of 12 exported functions unlisted pre-fix, 0 after) |
| DIV-54 | `returndup` default-ON (**supersedes the DIV-18 revert**) | The whole-block gotoless return duplicator (angr SAILR `ReturnDuplicatorHigh`, `p8_structure/kuna_returndup.rs`) becomes a shipped default again. DIV-18 first made it a default and it was reverted on 2026-07-05 after decbench measured **-976 aggregate GED-perfect** across 21,768 firings. That measurement described the *unselective* pass, which also split `return <variable>` epilogues; **#137** then ported angr's `ReturnDuplicatorHigh._should_duplicate_dst` const-return gate (`Funcdata::returndup_is_const_ret`, `substrate/funcdata_block.rs`), which declines every share whose return value is not a literal / COPY chain / phi-of-constants and drops ~40-60% of firings -- precisely the over-eager class the -976 was counting. The revert's justification therefore no longer describes the code, and the option is re-measured from scratch here rather than reinstated on the old evidence. It stays a runtime choice (`option returndup off` is byte-identical to upstream): the merged and split forms compile to the SAME object code, so nothing in the binary says which one the source wrote. `--mode aggressive` deliberately keeps naming the option so the preset re-enables it after an explicit `--option returndup off` | **Decbench A/B** (isolating ablation, same `36641d35` binary in both arms, `decompile-all --json --max-fn-seconds 120`, 536 slices / 52,862 scored functions, scored with decbench's own `GEDMetric`): ON 804,689 aggregate GED / **18,036 GED-perfect** versus OFF 812,445 / 17,619 -- **+417 perfect and -7,756 GED**, 608 perfects created against 191 destroyed (3.2:1), 3,676 functions improved against 2,079 worsened. Harness controls: the 46,005 functions the pass never fires on score identically in both arms, and the published `kuna_update-passwd.c` reproduces byte for byte. Net-positive in **all nine** partitions tested causally (split count 1-8, void vs value return, distinct return values, emitted return count, contains-a-`switch`, all-returns-identical, body size, project, opt level); the single net-negative cell -- a split that de-structures a loop (321 firings, -257 GED) -- had a gate built and scored at +2 perfect / -209 GED and was **not** kept. Full analysis: `docs/decbench/returndup-regression-triage.md`. **Datatests 675/675 PARITY OK with no re-pin**, via per-test `option returndup off` on the 9 upstream files whose merged rendering it changes (16 assertions moved without them: `ccmp`, `orcompare`, `condexesub`, `copytrim`, `condconst2`, `bitfields2`, `partialunion`, `statuscmp`, `union_datatype`); **stages 356/356 PARITY OK** with the same opt-out on 5 two-pass isolation tests (`gh6904-ppc-e500-concat44`, `gh9203-condexe-loopcopy`, `ghangr-1after909-doit-73591e`, `ghangr-earlyreturn`, `ghangr-ite-region-converter-missing-5db28e`) and no baseline re-record; `tests/stages/ghangr-returndup.xml` and `tests/stages/modes-aggressive.xml` already pinned the option explicitly. **Speed** (interleaved A/B on one binary, `decompile-all --mode reliable`, medians; the box runs concurrent builds so the blocked `scripts.pipeline.timeit` form is unusable here -- it reported factor::factor at -33.9% and again at -0.5%, and ls::main at +57.3% where the interleaved measurement gives +2.45%): base-passwd `update-passwd` 494.3 -> 502.9 ms (+1.74%, 7 pairs), coreutils `factor` 2,409.9 -> 2,427.6 ms (+0.74%, 7), coreutils `ls` 6,927.9 -> 7,006.9 ms (+1.14%, 7), gzip -O0 2,389.9 -> 2,453.4 ms (+2.66%, 9), gzip -O2 4,809.9 -> 4,753.3 ms (-1.18%, 7), coreutils `sort` -O2 12,944.2 -> 12,947.3 ms (+0.02%, 7) -- worst case +2.96%, inside the +5% budget. Per-function interleaved medians of 9: `ls` main 247.8 -> 253.9 ms (+2.45%), `factor` factor 111.9 -> 112.1 ms (+0.17%), `update-passwd` xmalloc 103.0 -> 103.4 ms (+0.34%), gzip main 519.8 -> 376.8 ms (-27.5%) |
| DIV-55 | a datatype copy split declines a read-only output (bug fix, no new flag) | `SplitDatatype::testCopyConstraints` (`p3_dataflow/subflow.rs`, upstream `subflow.cc:2390`) now also declines a COPY whose **output Varnode is read-only**. Without it kuna emitted a block of per-byte assignments **storing into a `.rodata` string literal** on a return path where the binary performs no store at all: `s_822f0[0xf] = SUB81(s_822f0[8],7);` in iproute2 `ip` `netns_add` (@0x20e10), 16 of them, on the source line `return -1`. `.rodata` there is `0x7e000..0x95335`, flags `A` and no `W`; the whole binary contains exactly ONE instruction that references `0x822f0` and it is the `movdqa` **load**. The chain: `strings` (P1, default on) plants a typelocked `char[N]` at the literal; the 16-byte SIMD read makes `ram:0x822f0(8)`/`ram:0x822f8(8)` heritaged persistent ranges, so `Heritage::guardReturns` inserts its synthetic `glob = glob` `return_copy` before each RETURN; kuna's own `stackguard` (DIV-14) strips the canary epilogue, leaving a bare-epilogue RETURN block that `ActionReturnSplit` node-splits; and `CloneBlockOps::buildOpClone` copies a flag mask that does **not** carry `return_copy` while `patchInputs` re-inputs the clone from a fresh unique-addressed COPY -- so the clone is neither marked `return_copy` nor address-tied at its own address, and both of upstream's guards miss it. The split then puts each one-byte piece in a different HighVariable, past `Merge::markInternalCopies`, and P9 prints them. The predicate `isReturnCopy()` (which the round-1 triage proposed) fixes nothing: instrumentation shows `rc=true` never reaches a successful split, because the same-address gate already declines the un-cloned form. Read-only-ness of the output is the property that survives the clone, and the invariant is one upstream already recognizes -- `Funcdata::fillinReadOnly` warns `Read-only address (ram,X) is written` -- it is just gated behind `readonlypropagate`. No option: a store the program cannot perform is never a judgment call (`option togglerule splitcopy off` reproduces the pre-fix pipeline for bisection) | **675/675 PARITY OK with no re-pin** and stages 375/375 with no per-test opt-out (the stages delta is purely the 6 new keys). Whole-binary `decompile-all` before/after over 12 corpus binaries / **15,386 functions** (x86-64, -O0 and -O2): exactly **3 functions change**, and they are the 3 the triage found -- `ip` `netns_add` (-18/+1 lines), `ip` `__get_hz` (-34/+1), openssh `sshd` `sub_230a0` (-64/+0); 116 lines removed and 2 added corpus-wide, every other function byte-identical. **112 phantom statements go to 0** and no real store is lost: after the fix every surviving assignment whose LHS is an address-named global lands in a **writable** (`WA`) section -- 22 in bash `.data`/`.bss`, 6 in tar `.bss`, 4 in coreutils `sort` `.data`, 1 in dpkg `.data`, 1 in sshd `.bss` -- and all of those counts are unchanged by the fix. -O0 `ip` (1,962 functions) is byte-identical, matching the triage's finding that the preconditions need an -O2 SIMD copy out of a literal. **Speed**: no mechanism for cost (one varnode-flag test per candidate COPY, and a declined split does strictly less work) and none measurable -- interleaved A/B medians on a box running concurrent agents: `ip` `netns_add` 1,349.4 -> 1,334.9 ms (-1.08%, 15 pairs), `ip` `__get_hz` 1,002.1 -> 1,006.2 ms (+0.41%, 15), openssh `sshd` `sub_230a0` 2,371.1 -> 2,117.5 ms (-10.69%, 15) after +5.25% on a 9-pair run of the same target, coreutils `sort` `decompile-all --mode reliable` 5,735.1 -> 5,816.3 ms (+1.42%, 11) after +9.17% on a 5-pair run, coreutils `ls` 9,179.3 -> 8,802.5 ms (-4.10%, 5). The noise floor is the honest caveat: a **byte-identical** control function (`ip` @0x20780) moved -12.18% and -20.23% across two runs, and `sort`'s output is byte-identical in both arms. GED is unchanged by construction (removing statements moves no block or edge): `O2-noinline-iproute2-ip-netns_add` 8.0 -> 8.0. `tests/stages/ghdec-returncopysplit.xml` (3 of 6 assertions pass pre-fix, 6/6 post-fix) |
| DIV-56 | a diamond whose condition was concatenated with its predecessor still re-rolls (`itecondlist`, default-ON) | `iteregion` (DIV-17) and `iteboolean` (DIV-51) both reach the diamond's condition component through one helper, `p8_structure/kuna_iteregion.rs (leaf_bblock)`, which descends a `BlockList` of ONE component but returns `None` for a list of two or more. The collapse structurer concatenates a just-collapsed predecessor together with the following condition block into a two-component `BlockList`, and that list becomes component 0 of the NEXT `BlockIf` -- so in a RUN of structurally identical diamonds every other one was declined and kuna re-rolled exactly ceil(N/2) of N (measured N=1..8: 1 1 2 2 3 3 4 4). The tell was that WHICH diamonds were missed depended on the neighbouring structure rather than on the diamond itself. `p8_structure/kuna_itecondlist.rs (cond_list_tail)` (ELEM 4121) now descends a condition-position `BlockList` to its LAST component before the leaf test -- `iteregion`'s `cond_cbranch`, and `iteboolean`'s `BlockCondition` gate (NOT inside `cond_terminal_cbranch`, whose leaf arm is only ever reached for the individual clauses of an already-identified chain). The arms keep the strict single-statement match, the labelled-goto-target guard still applies to the descended leaf, and the match keeps the OUTER list as its `cond_block`. Sound because the rewrite is print-only and the printer already renders this shape: `PrintC::emit_block_if` emits the condition component twice, once under NO_BRANCH (its statements) and once under ONLY_BRANCH (its branch), while `PrintC::emit_block_ls` emits every component under NO_BRANCH but only the LAST under ONLY_BRANCH -- so the leading components print as ordinary statements ahead of the `if` header either way, and `emit_block_if_ite`/`emit_block_if_bool` do the same two emissions on the same block. Restricted to `BlockList`: `PrintC::emit_block_graph` ignores ONLY_BRANCH and emits every component, so descending a `BlockGraph` would print the whole graph twice. It is a strictly narrower match than the two options it widens, so `option itecondlist off` can only ever fold FEWER diamonds, never different ones, and is byte-identical to the pre-DIV-56 render | **675/675 PARITY OK with the flip and no re-pin and no per-test opt-out**; **stages 381/381 PARITY OK** (the delta is purely the 6 new keys of `tests/stages/ghdec-itecondlist.xml`, which folds 2 of 3 identical diamonds with the option off and 3 of 3 with it on); `make rust-test` 4,424 passed / 0 failed; GED on the mined case `O0-bash-bash-shell_initialize` **46 -> 24** (-48%): all three source-identical `x = a||b||c||d;` call arguments now emit as one statement each instead of one plus two explicit `if (...) v = 1; else { v = 0; }` diamonds (the O2-noinline sibling was already GED 0 and is unmoved). Corpus breadth over 4 binaries / 12,241 functions (`decompile-all --mode reliable`): 53 functions change text (0.43%), 59 additional diamonds fold (coreutils `ls` +5, iproute2 `ip` +3, `bash` +17, betaflight STM32F405 ARM +34), ternaries 381 -> 434 and `iteboolean` re-rolls 124 -> 134, with error/diagnostic line counts unchanged in every binary (no function starts or stops producing output). | DIV-57 | `peimportcall` (new kuna option, default-ON) | A Windows PE reaches its imports through an **Import Address Table slot**, which is *data*: `call dword ptr [slot]` lifts to a `CALLIND` whose target is the contents of a global, unlike an ELF PLT stub or a Mach-O `__stubs` entry, which are code and resolve as ordinary direct calls. The only pass that resolves such a target, `ActionDeindirect` (`coreaction.cc:1235`), gates its `queryExternalRefFunction` arm on `isPersist() && isExternalRef()` -- and `Varnode::externref` was set **nowhere in the workspace**, because Ghidra sets it from an `ExternRefSymbol` (`Scope::addExternalRef`, an `engine_unavailable` stub in kuna). So every Windows API call in every PE stayed an unnamed `(*dat_4112c4)(0)`: no name, no prototype, and -- the damaging part -- no no-return flow effect, so a function ending in `ExitThread`/`ExitProcess` kept its fall-through and swallowed the whole of the next function. Three parts, all behind the one gate: (a) `loader/kuna_peimportcall.rs (PeImportCallPass)` reports one `[slot, slot+ptr)` range per import-descriptor entry and the commit ORs `Varnode::externref` over each in the symbol-table property map -- the same `Database::setPropertyRange` the loader's read-only section ranges use, so no second symbol is mapped at the slot and `ActionDeindirect` resolves against the `FunctionSymbol` `pe_iat` already registered at that VA (kuna's `query_function` keys on the Varnode's own address where upstream indirects through `ExternRefSymbol::refaddr`); (b) `Database::build_global_query` now carries the resolved callee's no-return flag into `GlobalEntry`, which `Architecture::query_function` puts on the prototype it hands `ActionDeindirect` -- upstream returns the callee's live `Funcdata`, so it never had to, and without it `deindirect` took its `lateRestriction` success path and never scheduled the restart whose re-flow plants the artificial halt; (c) upstream's PE-only `PEFunctionsThatDoNotReturn` is vendored verbatim and matched against a **PE/COFF object only**. kuna merges the PE and Mach-O name sets into one shipped file where upstream keeps them separate; rather than widen that merged file with `ExitProcess`/`KeBugCheck`/the MSVC-mangled `std::_Xbad_alloc` family -- Windows names a Mach-O would then be matched against -- the Windows list stays its own file on its own format arm. PE/COFF-only by construction (the pass is registered only for those formats and self-gates in `run`), so every ELF and Mach-O target is byte-identical either way; `option peimportcall off` restores the pre-DIV-55 rendering byte for byte on a PE too | **675/675 PARITY OK with the flip and no re-pin, and 374/374 stages** (the stages delta is purely the 5 new keys). **Benchmark**: the mined case `O0-mydoom-mydoom-mmsender_th` goes GED **37 -> 0 (perfect)**, and its un-triaged ida-pool sibling `O0-mydoom-mydoom-sync_visual_th` **39 (recomputed; 34 recorded) -> 0 (perfect)** -- both now matching IDA, Ghidra and angr, which all scored 0. **Corpus breadth**, whole-binary `decompile-all` over the two PE projects in the decbench corpus: unnamed `(*dat_...)()` IAT-slot call sites **927 -> 8** (mydoom 501 -> 4, dexter 426 -> 4) with **71 (mydoom) and 90 (dexter) distinct Win32 API names** appearing in the emitted C where there had been zero (`ExitProcess`, `CloseHandle`, `HeapFree`, `RegOpenKeyExA`, `WideCharToMultiByte`, ...); mydoom 5,247 -> 4,768 lines and dexter 3,422 -> 3,115 with **no function gained or lost** in either (156 and 109); 8 functions stop overrunning a no-return API (`// no-return` marks 9 -> 14 and 7 -> 10) and the phantom loops they had absorbed disappear (mydoom `while` 65 -> 60); gotos never increase (23 -> 23, 20 -> 19); of the 79 functions that shrink, the largest are dexter `Uninstall` 152 -> 19 lines (now 16 statements ending `ExitProcess(0);`) and mydoom `sub_402a62` 169 -> 64 and `sub_402eda` 96 -> 28; exactly two functions grow, both by one line, and both because a named import now takes a typed argument through a temporary (`a1 = (*dat_411464)(*(unsigned short *)(a0+a1)); a1 &= 0x3fff;` -> `v2 = *(unsigned short *)(a0+a1); a1 = ntohs(v2) & 0x3fff;`). On the shipped MinGW PE fixtures: `pe_imports_stripped.exe` 79 -> 3 unnamed slot calls, `pe_dwarf.exe` 50 -> 0. **Controls**: option-ON output is byte-identical to option-OFF on every non-PE target measured (coreutils `b2sum`, `mirai`, `tar` -O2), and option-OFF is byte-identical to the pre-change binary on both PEs. **Speed**: `scripts.pipeline.timeit` on the target function, 15 repeats, 387.96 -> 380.58 ms (**-1.9%**, within the +5% budget) -- an earlier 7-repeat run on a loaded box reported +49% and did not reproduce in three re-runs (+0.67%, -0.99%, -3.74%), so the interleaved form is quoted alongside: per-function interleaved medians of 11 pairs 338.0 -> 340.7 ms (+0.79%), and whole-binary interleaved medians of 7 pairs are **negative** because the truncated functions are smaller work -- mydoom 1,820 -> 1,689 ms (-7.23%), dexter 1,552 -> 1,520 ms (-2.09%), ELF control `b2sum` 5,663 -> 5,575 ms (-1.55%). `tests/stages/ghdec-peimportcall.xml` (2 of 5 assertions fail with the option off, 5/5 with it on) |

Upstream that string is **unobservable**, for two reasons kuna inherits neither of: `ActionNameVars::apply` renames every still-undefined Symbol before it returns (its final statement is `localmap->assignDefaultNames(base)`, coreaction.cc:3079), and `PrintC` reads `sym->getDisplayName()` **live** at emit time. kuna caches the name per HighVariable (`HighVariable::kuna_name`) and its port of `ScopeInternal::assignDefaultNames` (`Database::assign_default_names`) had **zero call sites**, so the placeholder reached emitted C, the `variables` array of `decompile-all --json`, and `decompile-project`'s `.asm` stack-frame comments. `$$` is not a legal C identifier character, and the leak was worse than cosmetic: the cache is written by `ScopeLocal::resolve_default_name_override`, which renames the covering Symbol only for a WHOLE-symbol cover (the C++ namerec gate `sym->isNameUndefined() && high->getSymbolOffset() < 0`) and otherwise returns whatever the Symbol is called at that moment -- so a partial cover visited before its whole-cover sibling froze the placeholder while the sibling renamed the shared Symbol to its `vN`. One stack slot, two identifiers, and a body that **reads a name nothing ever writes**: in `tar` `xstrcat` the x86-64 `va_arg` loop tested and incremented `$$undef00000000` while the induction update landed on `v12`. The instrumented proof is a backtrace on `set_kuna_name` plus the `--json` `variables` array, which shows exactly ONE Symbol at that offset and no `$$undef` Symbol at all -- so the printed placeholder corresponded to no live Symbol, ruling out the second-Symbol reading. The gate itself is a **faithful port** and is left alone; relaxing it would move when a Symbol consumes `base` and renumber every later local. Instead `ActionNameVars` grows the tail it was missing (`p6_variables/kuna_undefname.rs`): wire the catch-all `assignDefaultNames`, then re-resolve every HighVariable still holding a placeholder through the same `queryProperties(addr,1,usepoint)` containment query that bound it -- kuna's stand-in for the printer's live read. Both steps run after every `vN` is handed out and after the spacebase attach pass, so neither renumbers an existing local (a Symbol named by the catch-all takes the highest index in the function) and every `&symbol` render stays byte-identical. No option: an identifier containing `$$` is not valid C under any naming policy (`namestyle ghidra` reaches the same tail and produces upstream's `xStack<offset>` form) | **675/675 PARITY OK with no re-pin and no per-test opt-out**, and stages 386 -> 394 where the delta is purely this test's 8 new keys; `make rust-test` green (4,427 tests), `make check-spec` OK, `kuna catalog --check` OK. Breadth over a stratified decbench sample -- one binary per project per optimisation level, **116 stripped binaries / 90,808 functions**, x86-64 and ARM Cortex-M, O0/O2/O2-noinline -- before vs after on the same tree: binaries emitting a placeholder **73 -> 0**, functions **371 -> 0**, occurrences **2,142 -> 0** (1,503 reads / 639 writes), functions whose ScopeLocal Symbol table itself carried one **93 -> 0** (125 -> 0 Symbols); 90,808 functions produced in both arms and 0 error records in either, so nothing stopped being emitted. The blast radius is the same functions and nothing else: twelve binaries (the eight leakiest plus four clean controls), 18,590 functions, decompiled whole in both arms and diffed function by function -- **162 changed, and every one of the 162 had a `$$undef` identifier in its before text (0 unexplained)**; the function set is identical in both arms, error-record counts match (2/2), all four controls are byte-identical, and stack-local declaration lines go 16,828 -> 16,612 as the printer's existing whole-sibling guard folds the duplicate declarations the two names had been keeping apart. (A first pass with the watchdog ENABLED reported 3 'unexplained' changes; all three were per-function timeouts in the loaded before arm -- an aborted function has no `code`, so it diffs against a real body with no placeholder to match -- and re-run with `--max-fn-seconds 0` they are ordinary placeholder fixes, one of them byte-identical.) `tests/stages/ghdec-undefname.xml` carries both mechanisms as real bytes (tar `xstrcat` for the stale cache, iproute2 `set_qlen` for the unwired catch-all): **3 of 8 assertions pass against the pre-fix build, 8/8 with the fix**, and pass 2 (`option dedupvardecls off`) pins that neither witness depends on the P9 declaration collapse. Benchmark: GED is a CFG-shape metric and a rename cannot move it -- `scripts.decbench.rescore` on `O2-noinline-iproute2-ip-netns_add` (a case whose stored pane carries the placeholder) gives before 8.0 / after 8.0, **delta 0.0**, with no metric drift. Speed (interleaved before/after medians, `decompile-all --mode reliable`; `scripts.pipeline.timeit` flips an option on ONE binary and so does not apply to a default-path fix): tar-O2 52,998.0 -> 50,085.7 ms (-5.50%, 7 pairs), ip-O2-noinline 30,959.3 -> 31,025.3 ms (+0.21%, 7 pairs), gzip-O2 4,733.7 -> 4,891.3 ms (+3.33%, 7 pairs) -- the added work is one bounded `nametree` range query plus one high walk that exits immediately on any function with no placeholder |
| DIV-59 | a C++ (namespaced) callee renders its qualified name at the call site, and `--addr` names the function it addresses (bug fix, no new flag) | `kuna decompile`/`decompile-all` printed `sub_<addr>` at every call to a demangled C++ function on an UNSTRIPPED binary, for a name kuna already had: `kuna functions` listed `Account::deposit`, `kuna decompile <bin> 'Account::deposit'` resolved, and `--mode reliable` rendered `d = Account::deposit(a,amount);` -- while the default (`auto` -> `aggressive` under 500 KiB) rendered `d = sub_271c(a,amount);`. Not a demangler defect: a demangled name with no namespace (`_Z8topleveli` -> `toplevel`) rendered fine, and there is no identifier filter on `::` anywhere. The mechanism is **scope shadowing in the gated-commit seam**. `read_loader_symbols` installs a demangled funcsym in its NAMESPACE scope (`std::terminate` = base `terminate` in scope `std`); a discovery pass (`fast_funcdisc` / `funcstart_patterns`, both in `AGGRESSIVE_OVERRIDES`) then re-reports the same VMA, and `commit_analysis_output`'s discovered-entry arm named it `sub_<addr>`, resolved that synthetic name to a scope -- no `::`, so GLOBAL -- and probed `find_function(scope, addr)` in that scope ONLY. The real symbol was in scope `Account`/`std`, so the probe missed it and installed a DUPLICATE generic FunctionSymbol in GLOBAL at the same address; `FlowInfo::queryCall` resolves through `Database::find_function_across_scopes`, which searches global FIRST, so the duplicate shadowed the real name for every call site. Either discovery pass alone was enough (both report the VMA), which is why `--option fast_funcdisc off` alone did not restore the names. The in-source comment claiming the probe "still lets a real `.symtab`/`.dynsym` name win on a non-stripped binary" was false for every `::`-qualified symbol, and `find_function_across_scopes` already documented the hazard verbatim -- the two facts had never been connected. Fix: the probe resolves ACROSS SCOPES (the port of C++ `Scope::queryFunction`, which spans the scope tree), so an entry already known under a namespaced name is recognized as present and no placeholder is installed over it. Discovery is unchanged -- the passes still run, still find the same VMAs, a genuinely unnamed find is still `sub_<addr>`, and `register_symbol` is untouched so `sub_<addr>` remains a working selection alias. A second, independent defect closed in the same PR: `IfcAddrrangeLoad` jumped straight to `Architecture::name_function`, so `kuna decompile <bin> --addr 0x271c` printed a `sub_271c` header on an UNSTRIPPED binary while the by-name path printed `Account::deposit`; it now prefers the qualified FunctionSymbol installed at that address (an explicit `load addr <vma> <name>` still wins). No option: this is the driver tier, which has no settable axis (all 76 `phases.toml` rows carry an engine phase P1-P9), the post-fix rendering already ships under `--mode reliable`, and a synthetic placeholder shadowing a real symbol at the same address is wrong under every naming policy including `namestyle ghidra` | **675/675 PARITY OK and stages PARITY OK with NO re-pin of either baseline** -- structurally guaranteed: every datatest/stage file is an XML `<binaryimage>`, whose bootstrap never produces an `AnalysisOutput`, so `commit_analysis_output` is a no-op there and neither corpus can reach the defect (which is also why nothing caught it: `verify_noreturn_demangle` drives the CONSOLE with shipped defaults, i.e. no discovery pass, and printed `std::terminate()` and passed, while the CLI on that same fixture printed `sub_401070()`). `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` OK, `kuna catalog --check` OK. Blast radius, `decompile-all` before vs after over 13 binaries with every changed line classified by script: the 8 plain-C binaries are **byte-identical** (including `/bin/grep`, 35,070 lines, and `/usr/bin/ls`, 14,788 lines) -- structurally, a C symbol has no `::`, so the global-only probe always found it and no duplicate was ever created; the 5 C++ binaries change 184 lines, of which **181 are call-target renames** (`sub_2170` -> `std::runtime_error::runtime_error`, `sub_401136` -> `foo::Bar::baz`, `sub_4011fa` -> `app::Guard::throw_it`) and **3 are address-taken renames** (`sub_2ca0` -> `perimeter`, `sub_401120` -> `~runtime_error`), with 0 unexplained. Every C++ diff is exactly -N/+N: no line added or removed, no structural or signature change. The 3 address-taken lines render the symbol's BASE name because `Funcdata::link_spacebase_symbol` binds `name_for_global_varnode` (unscoped) where the sibling `ActionNameVars` path uses `name_for_global_varnode_scoped` + `kuna_qualify_global_name`; that gap is PRE-EXISTING and unrelated -- on `main` the same fixture already emits `{virtual override thunk}` in exactly that position and `void Account::~Account(void)` as a function header -- and is recorded as a follow-up in `docs/features/cppcallnames/analysis.md`. The C export is untouched on every axis (0 `::`, 8 errors, before and after), so no C-binary recompile metric can move. New gate `verify_cppcallnames.rs` over the already-vendored `cpp_noreturn_x86_64` with the discovery passes enabled in the live-CLI order: the call-site render, the `load addr` header, and a control that discovery still names its genuinely-unknown finds `sub_<addr>` and an explicit `load addr <vma> <name>` still wins. One existing test changed with it and is worth naming: `verify_multiformat_dwarf` used `load addr` as its "no-DWARF-name baseline" and asserted `sub_140001550`/`sub_0` -- a baseline that only held because `load addr` ignored the symbol table, i.e. it was pinning the bug. Earlier 5-pair medians on a box shared with concurrent agents reported +40.96% on probe_O0; the 11-pair minimum resolves it to -0.46%, and the C rows are flat throughout (/usr/bin/ls -1.32%, /bin/grep `functions` -0.06%). That is right for C and wrong for C++: an out-of-line member or namespace DEFINITION is emitted with only `DW_AT_specification` pointing at its in-class/in-namespace declaration, so it carries no name of its own and the `snap.name.is_empty()` guard dropped the whole DIE -- name, typed signature AND `DW_OP_fbreg` stack locals -- before any binding was attempted. | DIV-61 | `fdeinterior` (new kuna option, default-ON) | A kuna `FunctionSymbol` is an **entry address with no extent** -- `Database::add_function` takes an address and `find_function` answers *is a function registered AT this address*, never *is this address INSIDE one* -- so every discovery oracle's dedup is an equality test and a candidate 12 bytes into a body passes it. Not every FDE describes one function: the linker gives the WHOLE PLT a single FDE and every stub inside it is real, so a range is used only when no other named function start, no other FDE `pcBegin`, and no `.plt`/`.plt.sec`/`.plt.got`/`.iplt`/`.MIPS.stubs` section falls inside it; an entry AT an FDE start is never suppressed, so oracle 3's own product is preserved.
