| DIV-62 | `cppsig` (new kuna option, default-`proven`) | kuna applied **no** demangled C++ signature. The demangler reduces a mangled symbol to its qualified NAME only -- a hard requirement of the `::`-scope splitter, which nests on every `::` and would otherwise build junk scopes out of a signature tail -- and `demangle_raw`, the full c++filt form, had shipped with **no production caller** since the demangler landed, its own header recording the signature application as "a deferred follow-up". The cost is a **stripped** C++ shared library, where the exported `.dynsym` mangled name is the ONLY signature source left: on stripped `google/leveldb` `-O0`, functions whose first parameter is a NAMED pointer type ran ghidra 188 / ida 150 / binja 60 / angr 0 / **kuna 0** across 527 functions, and 37 of ghidra's 42 outright wins were `.dynsym`-exported (on the 112 non-exported `this`-shaped functions ghidra gets 1) -- so the lever is the demangler, not type inference, which the plain-C control corpus confirms by showing kuna's type recovery to be ordinary rather than weak. `analyzers/demangle/kuna_cppsig.rs` parses the demangled STRING, the same shape as upstream's `GnuDemanglerParser` (which consumes the c++filt text rather than an AST, because Ghidra's GNU demangler has no Java grammar to mirror): the last depth-0 parenthesis group is the parameter list, the last depth-0 token before it the qualified name, a trailing `const`/`volatile`/`&`/`&&` the cv/ref qualifier. Each declared parameter maps to a pointer of any depth (a C++ **reference** included -- a reference is a pointer at the ABI level, the same reduction `cppproto` gives `DW_TAG_reference_type`), a primitive at its LP64/ILP32 width, or -- as a POINTEE ONLY -- a named opaque structure carrying the bare innermost class name (upstream's `createPlaceHolderStructure`, and what the DWARF ground truth spells). An aggregate passed **by value**, an array, a function pointer or a pointer-to-member refuses the whole signature: the mangling carries declarations, not layouts, and a wrong width shifts every following parameter. The **return type is deliberately not applied**. Itanium encodes one only for a template function, so upstream's `resolveReturnType` returns null and `ApplyFunctionSignatureCmd` keeps whatever the analysis recovered -- which is why ghidra still prints `undefined8 leveldb::TableBuilder::NumEntries(TableBuilder *this)`. kuna expresses it as a `PrototypePieces` with no `outtype`, read by `FuncProto::seed_locked_from_pieces` as "lock the INPUT half only"; inventing `void` would DELETE every recovered return value. What makes this a **three-valued** option rather than a flag is the implicit object parameter: Itanium mangles a **static** member function exactly like a non-static one and like a namespaced free function (`leveldb::Status::OK()`, `leveldb::TableBuilder::WriteBlock(...)`, `leveldb::NewMemEnv(Env*)` share one shape), and inventing a `this` that is not there does not cost precision -- it shifts every following parameter, which is binja's bug in the other direction (it applies the declared parameters and OMITS `this`). Both tiers were measured on 1329 mangled `.dynsym` symbols with DWARF ground truth (915 really take `this`) BEFORE either was written: `proven` -- constructors (`C1`/`C2`/`C3`), destructors (`D0`/`D1`/`D2`), cv-/ref-qualified members (`_ZNK`), unqualified globals, and the MSVC forms, which state the access specifier, `static` and the convention outright -- is **precision 1.0000 at recall 0.7093**; `inferred`, which additionally decides the ambiguous nested names from class evidence mined out of the binary's own symbols (a scope owning a ctor/dtor/cv-member or a `_ZTV`/`_ZTI`/`_ZTS` symbol is a class), is precision 0.9278 at recall 1.0000; the naive "every nested name takes `this`" rule is 0.6942. For calibration, **Ghidra 12.1's own `this` decision on the same binary runs at precision 0.85** -- upstream resolves the ambiguity in `DemangledFunction.isThisCall` by comparing the mangled parameter count to the count its analysis recovered, which kuna has no access to at the analysis tier. In `inferred` BOTH directions need positive evidence: a scope with no witness at all is DECLINED, not assumed to be a namespace (a class whose constructor is implicit emits no witness, and calling its one exported member a free function drops the `this` and shifts every parameter LEFT); that refinement takes recall 0.9978 -> 1.0000 at unchanged precision by declining 100 of 1322 symbols. Complementary to `cppproto` (DIV-60), not overlapping: DWARF is ground truth and wins on an unstripped binary (the commit applies `cppsig` FIRST so the DWARF prototype overwrites it), the mangled symbol is what survives `strip`. Deliberately NOT here, and named as the follow-up: automatic `__thiscall` selection -- #265 registered the model on all 294 language:compiler pairs, but `PrototypePieces` carries no model back-pointer and `ArchContext` exposes only `defaultfp`; it is not needed for the win either, since every ABI in the corpus passes `this` as ordinary argument 0, and the one that does not (**32-bit MSVC `__thiscall`**, `this` in ECX) is REFUSED rather than mis-placed. Like `cppproto` the pass runs at `load file`, upstream of the `option` commands, so BOTH certainty tiers are computed there and stashed in `AnalysisOutput::cpp_sig`, and the mode selects which the analysis commit applies; `option cppsig off` restores name-only demangling byte for byte | **675/675 PARITY OK with no re-pin and no per-test opt-out**, stages 400/400 PARITY OK, `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` OK (lenient + strict), `kuna catalog --check` OK -- structural, because the XML `<binaryimage>` datatest bootstrap never runs the analyzer tier and so cannot reach this pass. **Benchmark, the number this PR exists for**: decbench leveldb O0, n=525 scored functions, scored with decbench `origin/main` @325046f (PR #60, the C++ `type_match` artifact fixes; `cache_version` 5, `DECBENCH_NO_CACHE=1`) so the pointer-spelling, namespace-qualifier and `DW_TAG_reference_type` artifacts are all corrected -- `type_match` perfects **9 -> 35 at the shipped default (+26, and ZERO lost)**, 38 under `inferred` (+29, zero lost); mean 0.1277 -> 0.1784 (`proven`) / 0.1920 (`inferred`); 28 (`proven`) / 44 (`inferred`) functions improved and **0 worse in either**. Against the same fixed scorer the rivals are ghidra 44, ida 35, binja 11, angr 8 -- so kuna moves from LAST to SECOND, tied with ida at the default and 6 short of ghidra. The 26 newly perfect functions are exactly the destructors and trivial accessors decbench's own improvement miner nominated (`~Cache ~Comparator ~DB ~Env ~EnvWrapper ~FileLock ~FilterPolicy ~Handler ~Logger ~RandomAccessFile ~Rep ~SequentialFile ~Snapshot ~Table ~TableBuilder ~WritableFile ~WriteBatch` + `ApproximateSize Env FileSize Iterator NumEntries Options Rep TableBuilder WriteBatch`), and `inferred` adds `ChangeOptions NewErrorIterator NewMemEnv`. **Breadth**: whole-binary `decompile-all` off vs on with every changed function classified. **Plain C is untouched on every axis** -- 11 binaries / 1,351 functions (coreutils `fmt` `-O2 -g`, `fmt_x86_64`, `dwarf_globals`, `fauxware`, static `mcount_x86_64` at 1,073 functions, `stripped_dynamic`, `switchtab`, `noreturn_propagate`, `fmt_aarch64`, `fmt_arm`, and the Rust-mangled `rust_hello`): **0 functions changed** in either mode, which is structural (a C symbol is not C++-mangled, so the pass emits nothing). C++: leveldb `-O0` stripped (3,962 functions) 257 changed at `proven` / 503 at `inferred`, leveldb `-O2` stripped (919) 43 / 93, the `cppsig_x86_64.so` fixture (14) 3 / 6, `cppproto_x86_64` (25) 3 / 3, `cpp_mangled_x86_64` (12) 0 / 0, `cpp_noreturn_x86_64` (16) 0 / 0, `eh_lsda_x86_64` (21) 1 / 1 -- **0 new errors anywhere in either mode**. **The wrongness axis, measured rather than argued**: raw arity change is not the harm signal (an arity DECREASE is often kuna over-recovering, with the demangled list right), so it is counted as a NEW read-before-write of an argument register (`int8 *v1; // rsi`) -- the symptom of a locked parameter list shorter than the ABI uses. Over leveldb `-O0` stripped: `proven` **2 of 3,962 functions (0.05%)**, `inferred` 29. Both `proven` cases are one mechanism, the SysV **hidden return pointer**: a function returning a class by value takes an sret pointer in argument slot 0, which the mangling does not mention, so the declared `this` binds to it (`leveldb::TableBuilder::status`, `leveldb::WriteBatch::Iterate`). Ghidra gets these wrong too and worse -- its parameter-count heuristic sees 3 recovered vs 1 mangled, concludes "not a thiscall", and emits `Handler * leveldb::WriteBatch::Iterate(Handler *param_1)` with `this` dropped entirely and slot 0 mis-typed. The sweep is what found the three defects the witness could not: (1) `outtype: None` handled at `Funcdata::apply_locked_prototype` instead of `FuncProto::seed_locked_from_pieces` left the CALLER-side rebuild (`ActionDefaultParams`, which reconstructs a callee's prototype to type a call's arguments) hitting `assign_map`'s `expect("outtype null")`, killing every `main` that called such a function; (2) `cpp_demangle` renders the two-parameter forwarding constructor `_ZNSt4pairI...EC1IRS1_S4_Lb1EEEOT_OT0_` with ONE parameter where c++filt renders two, so explicit function-template specializations are now refused outright -- which also sidesteps the module's known `maxof<int>`/`maxof<double>` collision, and alone cut the measured harm from 8 functions to 2; (3) overloaded operators put unbalanced brackets into the demangled text and are the densest source of static/free false positives, so they are refused. **Speed** (the pass walks the symbol table once at load and demangles each C++ name, so LOAD is the metric; interleaved off/on, MINIMUM of 13 pairs on a box shared with concurrent agents): `kuna functions` (load + commit only, the tightest isolation of the changed code) on stripped leveldb-O0 183.1 -> 183.8 ms (**+0.35%** `proven`) / 184.0 ms (+0.48% `inferred`) and on the plain-C `regglobal_fmt_x86_64` 175.3 -> 175.2 ms (-0.08% / -0.07%); `kuna decompile` leveldb `~Cache` 894.0 -> 897.0 ms (+0.34% / +0.04%); `decompile-all` `cppproto_x86_64` 127.8 -> 128.2 ms (+0.36% / -0.08%) and the `cppsig_x86_64.so` fixture 107.9 -> 108.1 ms (+0.19% / +0.10%) at n=31 -- an n=13 run of that last, 108 ms case reported +4.01% and did not reproduce. **Worst delta +0.48%, budget 5%.** The marginal cost is one demangle per C++-mangled symbol at load plus one parked prototype per applied function, and `off` still pays the demangle (the facts are computed then dropped at the commit), so the C rows are flat because a C binary has no mangled symbol to walk. Covered by `decompiler/crates/kuna-console/tests/verify_cppsig.rs` (7 two-pass e2e assertions over a new `strip --strip-all`ped `cppsig_x86_64.so` fixture, including the DWARF-wins precedence check and a test that PINS `inferred`'s known static-member cost so it cannot drift silently) plus 19 hermetic parser/`this`-decision unit tests; no stage XML, because the datatest path cannot reach the analysis tier. Two `verify_cppproto` baseline arms now set `cppsig off` explicitly -- with the sibling recovery at its default the "gate off = the bug" baseline stopped reproducing, since a `const` member and an unqualified global are both PROVEN shapes |
| DIV-63 | full-depth DWARF type resolution (`typedepth`, new kuna option, default-ON) | kuna's DWARF type mapper truncated ordinary C declarations to `void`. `MAX_TYPE_DEPTH = 3` counted **every** hop of the `DW_AT_type` chain, the transparent `typedef`/`const`/`volatile`/`restrict` links included, and its comment cited Ghidra's `DWARFDataTypeImporter.trackRecursion` -- which is not what upstream does. Upstream counts **re-entries of one DIE offset** and refuses the third (`recursionTrackingOffsetToLoopCount`, `case 3`), so it fires only on a genuine cycle; an acyclic chain visits each offset once however long it is. The reduction conflates "the same DIE again" with "a deep but finite chain", and four DIEs is ordinary C: `const char *const *` (5), `const size_t *` (4), `char *const []` (4), `char ***` (4), a `const` member function's `this` (4). Counting the sites straight out of `.debug_info` across six `-g` C binaries (coreutils `fmt` O0/O2, grep, bzip2, zlib `minigzip`, gzip): **294 truncated** -- 238 `DW_OP_fbreg` stack locals/params, 55 CU-top-level globals, 1 prototype parameter -- and the new rule resolves **all 294**. Raising the cap was never the fix: with no cycle detection under it, any cap is simultaneously too low for real C and unable to bound a self-referential chain, so it only converts a hang into a wrong answer. `analyzers/dwarf/kuna_typedepth.rs` restores upstream's counter (`TypeWalk::Cycle`, a per-offset re-entry map bracketing every recursive step) plus `MAX_NESTING = 64` as a native-stack backstop a Java port does not need; `TypeWalk::Depth` is the old budget verbatim, so `--option typedepth off` is byte-identical. Termination no longer rests on a bound that also has to be small: an offset already twice on the path is refused and a compilation unit has finitely many offsets. **Scope note, because half of the reported symptom was already gone**: #264 (`cppproto`, DIV-60) added `strip_qualifiers` on its own `cpp` path, and because `cppproto` is default-ON and rebuilds *every* subprogram's prototype -- C included -- binding it by address over the name-keyed one, plain-C **prototypes** and the C++ `const`-method `this` (`Account::available` renders `Account *this` on main today) inherited that fix. What remained was the `cpp = false` callers: `collect_fbreg_locals` (stack locals) and the global-sizing arm, where a truncated element type sizes the global at **one byte** so the covering-`SymbolEntry` query misses and it renders `dat_<addr>` or address-taken -- plus purely structural depth, where three *pointer* hops exhaust the budget even with the qualifiers collapsed (gzip's `add_envopt(int *argcp, char ***argvp, ...)` is `void ***` on main). Two consequences ride along. The qualifier collapse now runs for the C callers too, which is what carries an anonymous aggregate's typedef name onto it (a local `mbstate_t`/`position`/`direntry_t`, not the shared `anon_struct` every unnamed struct fuses into); and because a borrowed name can already be taken, `intern_aggregate` falls back to the anonymous name when the alias does not yield an aggregate. That guard was found by the sweep, not by argument: kuna registers a **core type named `code`** and zlib's `inftrees.h` really does `typedef struct {...} code;`, so `get_type_struct("code")` hit the core type, the factory refused the redefinition, and the pointer arm's `.or_else(get_type_void)` degraded `code *next` to `void *next` -- the exact degradation this change exists to remove (and already live on main for the *parameter*, via #264's path). Only an aggregate with no name of its own falls back, so no new name is ever asserted for a genuinely named type | **675/675 PARITY OK with no re-pin and no per-test opt-out**, stages 400/400 PARITY OK, `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` OK (lenient + strict), `kuna catalog --check` OK -- structural, because the XML `<binaryimage>` datatest bootstrap never runs the analyzer tier. **Benchmark**: the decbench scored corpus is `strip`ped, so it carries no `.debug_*` and this change is a structural **zero** there -- verified, not assumed (`decompile-all` off vs on over the stripped zlib `minigzip`: 0 lines differ). That also re-frames the C `type_match` standing (kuna 5.17% vs angr 7.29%, ida 5.55%): that gap is measured with no debug info present, so it is an *inference* gap, not a DWARF-consumption one. Where debug info exists the delta is large: decbench `type_match` over the same projects' **unstripped** `compiled/` copies (zlib + bzip2 O0, 9 binaries, n=872, scorer `main` @325046f, `DECBENCH_NO_CACHE=1`) moves perfects **458 -> 626 (+168)**, mean 0.8438 -> 0.9083, **193 functions improved and 0 worse** (zlib 401 -> 561 of 764, bzip2 57 -> 65 of 108). **Breadth**: whole-binary `decompile-all` off vs on with every changed line classified -- 204 changed lines over four `-g -O0` C binaries (zlib `minigzip` 91, grep 83, gzip 28, coreutils `fmt` 2), of which 102 are `void *` -> a concrete type, the rest `anon_struct` -> the real typedef name and globals whose corrected extent lets an array decay (`&(&crc_table)[i*4]` -> `crc_table[i*4]`, `dat_337e8` -> `unibyte_mask`, `*(int8 *)((int8)v13 + (uint8)*p * 8)` -> `v13[*p]`); **0 changed lines** on coreutils `fmt` O2, bzip2 O0, the stripped `minigzip`, and the three C++ fixtures (`cppproto_x86_64`, `cppsig_x86_64.so`, `eh_lsda_x86_64`, `cpp_noreturn_x86_64`). **The wrongness axis** -- a wrong concrete type asserted where `void *` was honest -- is **0 after the `intern_aggregate` guard** (2 before it, both the `code` collision). Covered by `decompiler/crates/kuna-console/tests/verify_typedepth.rs` (6 two-pass e2e assertions over a new `-g -O0` C fixture `typedepth_x86_64`: the `const char *const *` local, three pointer hops, the anonymous-typedef alias, the `code` collision, the global's extent, and termination on self-referential + mutually recursive structs) plus 6 hermetic unit tests including a **forged** `.debug_info` whose type chain closes on itself (self-pointer, mutually pointing `typedef`/`const`, self-referential array) asserted to terminate under BOTH arms; no stage XML, because the datatest path cannot reach the analysis tier |

| DIV-65 | `libcsigs` (new kuna option, default-ON) | kuna's built-in libc prototype table carried **27 entries**. Its own header calls the shortfall a documented LOSS and says why: Ghidra applies parsed C headers shipped as binary `.gdt` data-type archives, that format is not vendored into the kuna tree, and the table is "a faithful, minimal stand-in". The constraint is that no header archive exists here -- not licensing, since these are standard C library declarations, and `docs/missing-ghidra-analyses.md` classifies the whole item as a dependency/data substitution of the same kind as BFD -> `object`. So the table was small because nobody had grown it. The cost is that **every libc callee outside those 27 leaves its callers' arguments an inferred `unsigned long`**, which is kuna's weakest axis on plain C: of the 1,587 cases where IDA scores a perfect C `type_match` and kuna does not, 568 (35.8%) sit in a function that calls a libc name the table did not know, against 395 (24.9%) that call one it did. `analyzers/protos/kuna_libcsigs.rs` adds the measured remainder as a SECOND table (the 27-entry base table is left byte-for-byte alone, so its behaviour cannot move). **Which names was measured, not guessed**: `objdump` over the 547 frozen-decbench C binaries that carry a scored case gives a PLT call-site histogram (1,930 distinct imports), and each of those 1,587 cases mapped into its containing function block gives a per-callee ranking of what would actually move the metric; the rule is **>= 100 corpus call sites or >= 3 such cases**, plus a named-witness set, and 206 entries result -- headed by `exit` (119 cases / 5,456 sites), `gettext` (91 / 8,311), `__errno_location` (87 / 24,084), `strcasecmp` (63), `close` (55), `__stack_chk_fail` (50 / 29,055), `error` (42), `strncasecmp` (38 / 86 binaries), `open`, `strdup`, `strerror`, `dcgettext` (14,664 sites), and the FORTIFY `__fprintf_chk` (12,824) / `__printf_chk` (3,806). **Which signature is machine-derived, never written from memory**, because a wrong prototype is worse than a missing one -- it asserts a false type where `unsigned long` was merely uninformative: 193 entries are reduced by a fixed rule from the 1,520 `NC`-line prototypes `gcc -aux-info` emits over a TU including the platform headers with `_GNU_SOURCE` + `_FORTIFY_SOURCE=2`; `__isoc99_{scanf,sscanf,fscanf}` from the `__REDIRECT` at `/usr/include/stdio.h:434-441`, which is the header *proving* them ABI-identical to the names they replace; the FORTIFY `mem`/`str` `_chk` family (no header prototype exists -- they are GCC builtins) from GCC's own builtin table via nine compiling `_Static_assert(__builtin_types_compatible_p(...))` checks; `__stack_chk_fail` from glibc `debug/stack_chk_fail.c`. Sixteen were spot-checked by hand against their man pages as well, all matching. A declaration whose every slot is not **width-stable** across ILP32/LP64 is **REJECTED rather than approximated** -- `Ty` deliberately has no spelling for `off_t`, `time_t`, `long long`, a function pointer or a `char` parameter -- so `lseek` (746 sites), `signal` (574), `time` (399), `qsort` (323), `strtoll` (247), `fseeko`, `mmap`, `poll`, `iconv`, `connect` and `pthread_once` are deliberately absent; `strtoll` is the one that mattered, because an earlier reducer classified `long long int` as a 4-byte integer and would have shipped `int strtoll(...)`. The same principle drives the **one novel rule**: an entry here applies only to a name the image **IMPORTS** and does not itself define. `readelf` over all 677 binaries of the frozen O0/O2/O2-noinline trees found exactly one real collision outside NuttX (which implements the genuine POSIX API anyway) -- zlib's `minigzip` DEFINES `void error(const char *msg)` where glibc's is `void error(int,int,const char*,...)`, and typing that call `error(0,0,...)` would be strictly worse than what it replaces. A PLT/IAT import named `error` is definitively the platform's; a defined one is the program's own function that happens to share a spelling. This is also Ghidra's model (archives apply to EXTERNAL functions), and it makes a statically linked image inert. Finally the FORTIFY entry points are modelled as the distinct functions they are, not as aliases: `__printf_chk` takes a leading `int flag` so the format sits at slot 1, `__fprintf_chk` a `FILE *` then a flag -- treating either as its plain namesake would shift every argument of the most frequent call in the C corpus. Gated at the analysis COMMIT boundary (`engine.rs::analysis_pass_enabled`), the same seam `cortexmvectors`/`fdeinterior`/`cppsig` use, since the producing pass runs at `load file` upstream of any `option` command; `option libcsigs off` renders exactly what the 27-entry base table alone renders | **675/675 PARITY OK with no re-pin and no per-test opt-out**, stages 400/400 PARITY OK, `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` OK (lenient + strict), `kuna catalog --check` OK -- structural, because the XML `<binaryimage>` datatest bootstrap never runs the analyzer tier and so cannot reach this pass. **Benchmark, the number this PR exists for**: decbench `origin/main` @325046f, `DECBENCH_NO_CACHE=1`, `type_match` over the frozen C corpus, option off vs on across all three optimisation levels -- **grep** n=247 perfects 33 -> 37, mean 0.3105 -> 0.3243 (9 improved / 2 worse); **diffutils** n=420 perfects 24 -> 35, mean 0.2522 -> 0.2987 (40 / 0); **gzip** n=368 perfects 57 -> 62, mean 0.3740 -> 0.4007 (35 / 1); **bzip2** n=267 perfects 8 -> 8, mean 0.2617 -> 0.2712 (8 / 0); **findutils** n=790 perfects 21 -> 23, mean 0.1079 -> 0.1237 (45 / 0). Pooled over those five projects, **n=2,092: perfects 143 -> 165 (+22, +15.4%), mean 0.2272 -> 0.2501 (+10.1%), 137 functions improved against 3 worse**. **Breadth**: whole-binary `decompile-all` off vs on over 11 stripped binaries / 5,589 functions (coreutils dircolors-O0 + sort-O2 + ls-O2ni, grep-O0 + grep-O2ni, gzip-O2, zlib minigzip, openssh scp, statically linked nuttx, and C++ leveldbutil + libleveldb.so), **function count identical in every one**, every changed function classified: 1,141 changed -- 471 (41.3%) the import's own thunk gaining its signature, 281 (24.6%) a cast removed because the callee's return is now typed, 120 (10.5%) an `unsigned long` replaced by a concrete type, 116 variable renumbering, 74 the function's own signature retyped, 74 a call's arity restored to the declared signature, 5 structuring churn. **The wrongness axis, measured rather than argued**, on two independent counts: (1) **argument-count drift** -- 75 call sites had their arity RESTORED to the declared signature (`abort(a0,a2,0)` -> `abort()`, `openat` 0 -> 4 args, `reallocarray` 0 -> 3, `getopt_long` 4 -> 5), and the only 4 flagged as moving away sit in two functions (`gzip-O2 sub_f900`, `coreutils-ls-O2ni sub_a550`) that **already** render `free(v43,v24[-8])`, `malloc(v10,v24[-8])`, `memcpy(...,v24[-8])` and `strlen(v10,v4)` -- BASE-table names with 1 parameter -- with this option OFF, so the phantom trailing stack argument is a pre-existing frame-recovery defect in those functions, not one this table introduces; (2) **parameter specificity** -- 296 slots gained it, **2 lost** it, and both losses are the same function (grep-O2ni `Pexecute`, `char *` -> `void *`) where the loss is the *truthful* prototype, since `memchr`/`rawmemchr` really do declare `const void *`. The statically linked `nuttx` image is **0 of 924 functions changed**, which is the imports-only guard doing exactly its job; the no-return annotations are identical on every C binary (coreutils-sort `abort` 24->24 / `exit` 5->5 / `__assert_fail` 3->3, grep 28/7/8, openssh-scp `error` 10->10 / `exit` 39->39). **Speed** (the marginal cost is building + committing up to 206 more `PrototypePieces` at load, so LOAD is the metric): `kuna functions` -- load + the whole analysis pass list + commit, no function body decompiled -- interleaved off/on, min AND median of 21 pairs: coreutils-dircolors 293.6 -> 289.4 ms min (-1.45%) / 321.6 -> 320.7 median (-0.28%), openssh-scp 836.5 -> 835.4 (-0.12%) / 851.9 -> 846.0 (-0.69%), nuttx 1423.4 -> 1432.2 (**+0.62%**) / 1465.9 -> 1452.2 (-0.93%), grep 849.9 -> 695.4 (-18.18%) / 1012.6 -> 1014.9 (+0.23%) -- the grep minimum is noise in both directions (an earlier 15-pair run of the same case reported +13.99%), the medians are the honest read. `scripts.pipeline.timeit` on grep `main`, 15 samples: 1547.61 -> 1547.82 ms (**+0.01%**). Whole-binary `decompile-all`, interleaved min/median of 13: dircolors 1832.4 -> 1827.1 (-0.29%) / 1852.6 -> 1848.6 (-0.22%), zlib minigzip 4973.3 -> 4981.3 (+0.16%) / 5034.3 -> 5047.5 (+0.26%). **Worst stable delta +0.62%, budget 5%.** Covered by `decompiler/crates/kuna-console/tests/verify_libcsigs.rs` (5 two-pass e2e assertions over the vendored `fauxware` fixture: the caller's `unsigned long a0` -> `char *a0` from `open`, the fd `unsigned int` -> `int4` from `open`'s return, the thunk's full `int open(char *,int,...)` including the variadic tail, `void exit(int4)`, and a function the table does not touch being byte-identical) plus 5 hermetic table-invariant unit tests including the FORTIFY argument slots and the imports-only rule; no stage XML, because the datatest path cannot reach the analysis tier. Deliberately NOT here and recorded as the follow-up: `formatstring/apply.rs`'s `VARIADIC_SUBSTRINGS` matches by `contains`, so `__printf_chk` is already treated as a format-string callee with the format at slot 0 when it is at slot 1 -- a separate, pre-existing behaviour change that belongs in its own PR. Bundle: `docs/features/libcsigs/` **Preset decision, made on evidence rather than left implicit**: `libcsigs` is deliberately NOT in `AGGRESSIVE_OVERRIDES`. #267's `aggressive_carries_every_default_off_option` invariant exists because `auto` resolves to `aggressive` under 500 KiB, so a default-OFF option absent from the preset ships inert on the default path -- which is how `itaniumrtti` recovered 0 of 29 until it was caught. This option ships default-ON, so it is out of that invariant's scope by construction, and the liveness was measured rather than assumed: on `dircolors` (55,680 bytes, so `auto` -> `aggressive`), default vs `--option libcsigs off` differs in 57 of 184 functions under `auto`, 57/184 under `aggressive`, 58/183 under `reliable` and 58/184 under `fast`, with the cast-removal witness present in all four -- no preset can silence it, since none turns anything off except `fast`'s three discovery gates. Adding it would be worse than redundant: the preset **owns** an option it lists (its own doc records that `--option returndup off` earlier on the command line is re-enabled by the preset), and every benchmark and sweep number above was produced by `--option libcsigs off` on sub-500 KiB binaries, i.e. straight through `auto` -> `aggressive`; preset membership would silently re-enable it there and destroy the ablation hook that is half the reason the flag exists. So no second DIV row for preset membership is needed -- this row covers the default-ON decision alone. **Counter derivation**: the counts here were COUNTED out of a freshly captured `phase_catalog.json`, not computed from the previous value -- the rebase onto #267 auto-merged `catalog_bytecompat.rs` to 94 with NO conflict, because #267 and this PR had independently made the identical 93 -> 94 edit, so the committed number was short by one until the capture corrected it to 95 (tiers (20, 41, 34), 94 JSON rows, and `docs/options.md` independently reporting 95 option headings). |
| DIV-66 | `decompile-all` / `decompile-project` / wasm: one shared decompile step, and `formatstring` leaves the `aggressive` preset (no new flag) | Two defects, one root. **(a) The whole-binary surfaces and the console `decompile` command did not decompile the same way, and the whole-binary one was the weaker** -- which is the surface decbench, the browser front-end, `decompile-project` and every agent-facing workflow use, so every benchmark number kuna has reported was measured on it. `IfcDecompile` wrapped the engine drive in Ghidra's `FormatStringAnalyzer` half-B loop (decompile -> read the constant format string at each printf/scanf-family call's format-argument slot -> install the derived per-call-site prototype override -> decompile again) and enabled `readonlypropagate` for its duration; `project::decompile_targets` called the drive directly and did neither, so `--option formatstring on` was a **silent no-op** there (`kuna decompile ... --addr 0x14c9` on stripped zlib `minigzip` gave `void error(char *a0)` where `decompile-all` gave `void error(unsigned long a0)`; IDA 9.2 gives `const char *`). The scoped `readonlypropagate` flip is the LARGER of the two effects: it constant-folds every read-only load, not just the format constant, so on ARM Cortex-M -- where a global address arrives through a PC-relative literal pool -- it rewrites most of a function body (`*(unsigned int *)(dat_14001c5c + 0x204) = 0x200;` becomes `v1 = dat_10003af8;`, `sub_140053aa(a1,dat_140013fc,6)` becomes `sub_140053aa(a1,"1.2.0",6)`), which is why the ARM cases were worst hit. Fixed by making the shared pipeline the single source of truth rather than copying the loop: a new `kuna-console/src/decompile_step.rs (decompile_one)` holds the drive call, the loop and the scoped flip, and BOTH `IfcDecompile` and `decompile_targets` go through it, each passing only the facts it has via one `DecompileSeed`. Discovered call-site overrides are RETURNED, not stashed globally: the console persists them for a later re-decompile of the same function, the whole-binary loop drops them, because persisting them across functions would make a whole-binary run order-dependent and would itself be a new divergence. **(b) Honouring the option exposed what it costs, and it is over budget**, so `formatstring` is removed from `AGGRESSIVE_OVERRIDES` and joins `v850indirectbranch`/`dwarf_lines` in `EXCLUDED_ON_PURPOSE`: it is a per-run opt-in on EVERY surface, which is standing requirement 4's answer to a feature that misses the 5% speed budget, and which also stops `kuna decompile` being charged for a feature `decompile-all` never delivered. The option's own shipped default was already `off`; only the preset made it effectively on | **675/675 PARITY OK with no re-pin and no per-test opt-out**, stages 400/400 PARITY OK, `make rust-test` green (**4,535 passed / 0 failed / 37 ignored**), `make check-spec` OK (lenient + strict), `kuna catalog --check` OK, and every shared catalog counter re-derived from the BUILT artifact rather than by arithmetic -- live `kuna catalog --json` 95, `catalog_bytecompat.rs` 95, `kuna_phases/tests.rs` 95, `phase_catalog.json` 95, `docs/options.md` 95 and byte-current against a regeneration (this PR adds no option, so 95 is main's number carried through the rebase unchanged). The preset change is enforced, not just documented: `modes.rs`'s `aggressive_carries_every_default_off_option` invariant makes an unlisted default-off option a build failure, so `formatstring` had to be entered in `EXCLUDED_ON_PURPOSE` with its reason. **Parity, the number this row exists for**: `kuna decompile --addr X --mode M` vs `kuna decompile-all --addr X --mode M` over 182 (binary, mode, address) records -- 13 binaries (C and C++, stripped and unstripped, O0 and O2, x86-64 executable + shared library + ARM Cortex-M firmware) across `auto`/`reliable`/`fast` -- **13 divergent before, 0 after**, and 0 name differences in either arm; re-run with `--option formatstring on` forced on BOTH surfaces (the load-bearing case now that the preset no longer sets it) also **0 of 182**. The pre-fix divergence was confined to `auto` (which resolved to `aggressive`): `reliable` and `fast` were already 0/14 each, which is itself the proof that this one feature was the whole divergence. Worst-hit case: the ARM firmware `CMSIS_DAP.axf` at **8 of 14** sampled functions, versus 1-2 of 14 on the x86-64 C binaries. Seven further asymmetries were enumerated and dispositioned rather than assumed away: `funcstart_patterns`/`aif` for non-x86-64 and the `--max-fn-seconds` watchdog are deliberate whole-binary policy (DIV-20 and spec 0.2) and are left alone; the flow-extent bound (`fd.get_size()` vs unbounded), `--addr` naming, the console-only seeded facts and `print raw`/`--regions` are not divergences for the same input, and the 0/182 residual confirms it. **Sweep**: the DEFAULT path's emitted C is **unchanged** -- with `formatstring` out of the preset no surface runs the loop unless asked, and the pre-rebase 3-arm sweep already measured the `formatstring off` arm byte-identical to the genuine pre-fix binary over 15,990 functions / 33 cases (**0 differences**), which is what proves the refactor itself inert. Under the OPT-IN the same 33-case / 15,990-function sweep changes **1,824 bodies with 0 entries added or removed**, hunk classes {'signature': 112, 'body-only': 469, 'dat-count-change': 1278, 'sig:ulong->charp': 23, 'shorter': 447, 'longer': 74, 'new-string-literal': 398}; every hunk is one of exactly two intended kinds -- an `unsigned long` -> `char *` at a printf/scanf call, or a read-only load constant-folding (`dat_<hex>` -> a resolved global or a string literal), the latter dominating on ARM (`cf2.elf` 1,523 of 2,790, `CMSIS_DAP.axf` 177 of 267). **Benchmark**: **0 by default**; opted in, decbench `origin/main` @325046f (`cache_version` 5, `DECBENCH_NO_CACHE=1`), both arms re-decompiled and re-scored against the same source CFGs over three O0 C projects (zlib, cronie, sysvinit), `type_match` perfects **80 -> 88** of 1,133 scored functions (29 improved, 10 regressed, every project's mean up) and GED 4 improved / **0 regressed** of 1,168, with cronie's GED mean 9.8844 -> 7.8241. The ten `type_match` regressions are the `formatstring` feature's own known cost -- a `%c` argument typed `uint1` where the C variadic promotion makes the parameter an `int`, and a format-derived width narrowing a recovered `uLong` -- which `kuna decompile` has always produced. **Speed**: the DEFAULT path is free, measured rather than assumed -- default vs an explicit `--option formatstring off` (both disable the loop), interleaved minimum of 13 pairs: crontab-O0 1,626.9 -> 1,625.1 ms (-0.11%); psktool-O0 330.1 -> 330.6 ms (+0.16%); gzip-O0 2,700.0 -> 2,699.5 ms (-0.02%); minigzip-O0 4,947.7 -> 4,955.6 ms (+0.16%); CMSIS_DAP.axf ARM 2,503.1 -> 2,503.7 ms (+0.02%); tar-O0 (1570 fns, large) 24,228.9 -> 24,238.1 ms (+0.04%); b2sum-O0 5,302.8 -> 5,303.6 ms (+0.02%); worst **+0.16%**. The OPT-IN price is what took the feature out of the preset in the first place: crontab-O0 1,632.1 -> 2,863.7 ms (**+75.46%**); psktool-O0 331.5 -> 505.7 ms (**+52.54%**); gzip-O0 2,713.6 -> 3,884.1 ms (**+43.13%**); minigzip-O0 4,986.9 -> 5,081.2 ms (**+1.89%**); CMSIS_DAP.axf ARM 2,513.9 -> 2,653.6 ms (**+5.56%**); tar-O0 (1570 fns, large) 24,352.2 -> 28,067.0 ms (**+15.25%**); b2sum-O0 5,433.7 -> 5,633.7 ms (**+3.68%**); worst **+75.46%** against a 5% budget. Attributed with a third arm (`formatstring off` + upstream `readonly on`, min of 9): the scoped read-only propagation is FREE (crontab +1.15%, psktool -0.14%, gzip +0.08%), so **100% of the cost is the second decompile**. The obvious optimisation was implemented and REJECTED on evidence: declining an override whose format-derived types already match the call's argument types removed only 12 of 25 overrides on `crontab` and moved the re-decompiled-function count 25 -> 24 (psktool 5 -> 3, gzip 25 -> 25) -- the redundant overrides sit in the same functions as real ones -- while changing emitted C on 12 of 15,990 functions, so it bought nothing and broke the parity this PR exists for. The instrumented rates are the reason: only 15%/5%/10% of functions re-decompile on crontab/psktool/gzip; they simply carry most of the decompile time, because a printf-heavy function is a big function. Covered by `decompiler/crates/kuna-console/tests/verify_decompile_all_parity.rs` (three assertions over the four vendored `fmt_<arch>` fixtures, including the ARM literal-pool case: the two-pass shape on the whole-binary surface, plus byte-identical output between the two surfaces in BOTH option states); no stage XML, because the XML `<binaryimage>` datatest bootstrap never produces an `AnalysisOutput` and so cannot reach an analysis-tier option at all. Bundle: `docs/features/dallparity/` |
| DIV-67 | `funcboundflow` (new kuna option, default-ON) | A kuna `FunctionSymbol` is an entry address with **no extent**, and CALL/CALLIND are fall-through, so the S2 flow follower stops only at a RETURN or a callee it can *prove* no-return (`FlowEnvironment::query_call_no_return`). A function whose last act is a `call` to a no-return routine kuna cannot prove no-return compiles with **no trailing `ret`**, only inter-function alignment padding — and in a stripped, statically-linked binary that describes the unnamed `exit`/`abort`/`__stack_chk_fail` bodies and every app-level `die()`/`throw` wrapper built on them (no symbol to key `noreturn_known` off, no PLT name to key `noreturn_externmatch` off). The follower runs the padding's fall-through **straight into the next function's entry** and decodes THAT function's body into the current one, so the following function is emitted twice: once correctly under its own entry, once as a garbage tail of its predecessor (dead code after the `die()` call, uninitialized reads, a second unrelated function inlined). Found by the interp-bee/jit-bee static-pie study (`docs/features/funcboundflow/`): **70 of 195 (36%) Bee application functions** end in exactly such a `call` and are corrupted this way — e.g. `numeric_item_to_i64 @ 0xa070` (`.eh_frame` FDE `0xa070..0xa0a8`, last op `call bee_error_throw` → `exit`) swallowed the whole bodies of `require_numeric_pair @ 0xa0b0` and `print_value_lines @ 0xa100`, both of which kuna also lists as their own functions; IDA 9.2 and Ghidra both bound decompilation to the function body and stop at `0xa0a8`. Fix: `p2_lift/kuna_funcboundflow.rs (kuna_should_bound_at_entry)` + a truncation at the fall-through push of `flow.rs (FlowInfo::process_instruction)` — when the fall-through target is the entry of another known function (`query_call(next).is_some()`) and is not the current function's own entry, plant a no-return artificial RETURN (mirroring `check_for_flow_modification`'s no-return-call halt) instead of decoding the target, and emit a `funcboundflow` truncation WARNING. The gate is fast-pathed (default-off does zero symbol-table lookups; only the default-on path probes `query_call` per fall-through, a hashmap lookup dwarfed by decode+analysis). This OVERLAPS `noreturn_extern`/`noreturn_externmatch` (which stop the same leak by callee *name*) but is name-independent: it bounds at the function *boundary* whatever the callee, which is why it fixes the stripped-static case those two cannot name. Restore the upstream flow-into-the-next-function rendering with `option funcboundflow off` | **675/675 PARITY OK with no baseline re-pin**: the flip is byte-identical on the datatest corpus except two tests that deliberately exercise flow-into-an-adjacent-function and opt out per-test (NOT a re-pin) — `longdouble` (a tail `jmp` flowed into its callee and on across `pass`→`writeLongDouble`→past `printf_chk`, already `tailcalljump off` for the same reason) and `ghangr-noreturn_extern` (isolates the `noreturn_extern` toggle; already `noreturn_externmatch off`, now `funcboundflow off` too, since all three bound the SAME `__stack_chk_fail` fall-through). Stage corpus **407/407 PARITY OK** with a new two-pass `tests/stages/funcboundflow.xml` (`caller` ends in `call fatal` and either truncates cleanly (default) or absorbs `neighbor`'s `a0 - 5` body (`funcboundflow off`)). `make rust-test` green (unit test `kuna_funcboundflow::tests` + the count/tier bumps: settables 95→96, transform tier 41→42, surfaces 101→102, corpus files 193→194). Every shared catalog counter re-derived from the BUILT artifact: live `kuna catalog --json` 96, `catalog_bytecompat.rs` 96, `kuna_phases/tests.rs` 96, `phase_catalog.json` 96, `docs/options.md` 96, `kuna catalog --check` OK, `make check-spec` OK. **Speed** (interleaved on-vs-off, whole-binary `decompile-all`): neutral-to-positive because the bound can only REMOVE decoded instructions — a merge-heavy target is materially FASTER (interp-bee Bee subset min 2.62 s on vs 5.49 s off, since the runaway absorption stops), and a normal target is within measurement noise (zlib `example` min 6.41 s on vs 6.34 s off; `mv` min 15.38 s on vs 16.72 s off), well inside the 5% budget. Bundle: `docs/features/funcboundflow/` |
| DIV-68 | `kuna functions` takes the whole-binary DISCOVERY policy (driver-tier bug fix, no new flag) | **`kuna functions` listed 1 of the 5,797 functions `kuna decompile-all` decompiled on the same binary in the same mode.** `load_program`'s `default_listing` parameter gated THREE unrelated driver defaults behind one flag, and `list_functions` -- the only caller passing `false` -- switched off all three: the DIV-15 `listing on` (whose rationale, "no-return facts never add or remove entries", is true and was the justification for the cheap enumeration path), and the DIV-20 non-x86-64 `funcstart_patterns on` + `aif on`, which are pure DISCOVERY passes whose entire job is to add entries. Every other whole-binary surface -- `decompile_all`, `decompile-project`, and the wasm/browser front-end (DIV-53) -- takes the full policy; `functions` was the lone outlier, so the command that answers *what is in this binary* contradicted the command that decompiled it. Measured on decbench stripped firmware under `--mode reliable`, `kuna functions` vs `kuna decompile-all`: crazyflie `cf2.elf` **1 vs 2,790**; chibios `ch.elf` **5 vs 770**; betaflight `STM32F405` **1 vs 5,797**; coreutils `ls` (x86-64) 588 vs 588. A sweep of all 818 stripped benchmark binaries found 107 where `functions --mode aggressive` (which names the three options in its preset, so it escaped the bug) reported >20% more than `--mode reliable`; **every one of the 107 is non-x86-64** -- bare-metal ARM (libopencm3, crazyflie, betaflight, chibios, cleanflight, freertos, nuttx, riot-os, u-boot) plus three i386 PE. `auto` hid it below 500 KiB (it resolves to `aggressive`) and above 2 MiB (`fast` names all three off), so the hole was exactly the `reliable` band and any explicit `--mode reliable`. Fixed by naming the policy instead of overloading a bool: `load_program` takes a `DriverDefaults::{Inventory,Decompile}`, the DIV-20 discovery bundle is applied on **both**, and the Listing-only default stays the decompiling surfaces'. The Listing had to join the discovery bundle rather than stay separate, because it is the master gate those passes read: on `entrymain_arm`, `listing on` alone gives 10 entries and `funcstart_patterns on` alone gives 10, but the two together give 13. The x86-64 half is left exactly as it was, on evidence rather than assertion: over 40 stripped x86-64 ELFs sampled across the corpus by size (68 to 1,585 entries), `--option listing on` changes the reported entry set on **0 of 40**, so building it for enumeration would buy nothing and cost a whole-program decode | **Parity restored, measured as a set not a count**: after the fix every address `decompile-all` decompiles is present in `kuna functions`, on all four binaries -- `cf2.elf` 2,790 vs 2,790 (identical sets), `ch.elf` 770 vs 770 (identical), betaflight 5,798 vs 5,797 (**0 missing**; the one extra is a canonical entry outside any CODE section, which `function_entries_executable` is supposed to drop), `ls` 588 vs 588 (identical). x86-64 output is byte-identical -- the injection does not fire there at all. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK** (no re-pin), `make test-stages` **PARITY OK** (405/405), `make rust-test` green (**4,539 passed / 0 failed / 37 ignored**), `make check-spec` OK (lenient + strict), `kuna catalog --check` OK. **Speed**, interleaved minimum of 5 pairs, same box, before/after binaries built from the same tree: `kuna functions` on x86-64 is unchanged within noise -- `ls` 0.123 -> 0.125 s (+1.5%), `tar` 0.173 -> 0.158 s (-8.5%), `bash` (1.44 MiB, the corpus's largest) 0.245 -> 0.243 s (-1.0%); the decompiling surfaces are untouched -- `decompile-all` on `ls` 7.501 -> 7.494 s (-0.1%) and on ARM `ch.elf` 3.361 -> 3.225 s (-4.1%, the arch probe now reads the image once instead of twice). The cost lands where the fix does: `kuna functions` on ARM firmware pays the discovery it was skipping -- `cf2.elf` 0.109 -> 3.901 s, betaflight 0.083 -> 5.270 s. That is reported, not budgeted around: the 5% budget compares two ways of computing the same answer, and the cheap answer here was not slower-but-right, it was 1 of 5,797. Covered by two `kuna-cli` CLI tests (`decompile_all_cli.rs`): the ARM one asserts `functions` covers every `decompile-all` entry (it did not before -- 0x3e0 / 0x410 were decompiled but unlisted) and equals the explicit three-option bundle, the x86-64 one asserts the inventory still equals an explicit `listing off` and still covers `decompile-all`. No new option, no stage XML, no catalog count change |
| DIV-69 | `orchain` (new kuna option, default-ON) | **DIV-54 flipped `returndup` on evidence from two of the three optimisation levels.** Its ablation covered O2 and O2-noinline (52,862 functions); extended to all three over **795 slices / 85,195 scored functions**, the pass is +640 GED-perfect corpus-wide but **-192 at -O0** -- 1,217 functions moved to an exact structural match against 1,409 moved off, McNemar z = -3.75. Flipping the default back is still wrong (it would buy 192 at O0 and sell 832 at the optimized levels), but **one structural sub-shape carries essentially all of the O0 harm and almost none of the optimized benefit**: the split that permanently blocks `CollapseStructure::rule_block_or`. `returndup` gives every predecessor of a shared bare-epilogue RETURN its own private return; for a guard clause that recovers the source, but the operand blocks of a short-circuit expression are predecessors of that same block, and the shared out-target IS `ruleBlockOr`'s entire precondition (`bl -> {orblock, clauseblock}`, `orblock -> {clauseblock, X}`). `ActionReturnDup` runs in fullloop's `returnsplit` group and `collapse_conditions` runs later, so the fold is lost for good and one source `return a || (b && (c || d));` prints as a five-return constant-guard cascade (`iproute2 ip::sci_complete`, GED 21 against 0). `p8_structure/kuna_orchain.rs (shortcircuit_shared_targets)` is a read-only replay of `collapse_conditions` on the **bblocks** CFG that mirrors `ruleBlockOr`'s own admission test rather than approximating it -- one in-edge, two out-edges, not a switch, not `BlockBasic::isComplex` (the same `bb_is_complex` verdict the rule reads), a shared out-target, and the sibling's other target is not the head -- fusing to a fixpoint so a chain collapses inside-out; `returndup_apply` then declines the whole function's splits when one of its own candidates is a recorded target. It also protects a block joining two or more single-exit arms that are themselves fold targets, because before the constants reach the epilogue phi the arms are one bare `v = K` block each and the RETURN sits one edge further on -- the graph the FIRST `returndup` invocation of the fullloop sees, and without that clause the archetype recovers the `||` chain but not the 0/1 select `iteboolean` re-rolls. Default-ON rather than an `aggressive` preset member because `returndup` is itself a shipped default in every mode and **45% of the corpus (38,216 of 85,195 scored functions, 36 of 795 slices) is over the 500 KiB `auto` threshold and runs `reliable`**, where a preset-only gate would leave the split unnarrowed -- measured directly: with the gate in the preset only, the no-option arm reproduced the UNGATED body on `iproute2 ip` (76 functions), `bash` (366) and `openssh sshd` (201) | **675/675 PARITY OK with the raw flip -- zero assertion movement, no per-test opt-out and no re-pin** (the datatest corpus never reaches the shape: the gate only ever declines a `returndup` split, and the nine files that pin the merged epilogue already carry `option returndup off`); stages 415/415 PARITY OK with the new two-pass `tests/stages/ghdec-orchain.xml`; `make rust-test` green; `make check-spec` OK lenient + strict; `kuna catalog --check` OK. Every shared counter re-derived from the BUILT artifact on the rebased tree, not by arithmetic: live catalog 97, `catalog_bytecompat.rs` 97, `kuna_phases/tests.rs` 97 / tiers (20, 43, 34) / `},\n` 96, `phase_catalog.json` 97, `docs/options.md` 97, `xml.rs` corpus 195. **Sweep** (`scripts/decbench/optsweep.py`'s population and metric, base arm = shipped defaults at merge-base `82dd39a7`, var arm = the gate, 795 slices / 85,195 scored functions, all three optimisation levels): **O0 +611 GED-perfect / -2,816 aggregate GED, O2 -13 / +1,232, O2-noinline -15 / +617 -- +583 net perfect and -967 aggregate**. Both directions by name: **781 functions moved to perfect against 198 moved off (3.94 : 1, McNemar z = +18.63)**, 3,254 improved / 2,882 worsened, 6,919 bodies changed. Per architecture x86-64 +530, arm +47, pe-x86 +6 -- none negative; per project 34 of 37 net-positive and the three that are not are -3 / -3 / -2 (bzip2, libselinux, kmod). Largest single moves to perfect: `iproute2 ip::sci_complete` 21 -> 0, `gzip make_ofname` 20 -> 0, `bash skipname`/`wskipname` 17 -> 0, `crazyflie sensorsFindBiasValue` 15 -> 0, `cleanflight/betaflight nextArg` 14 -> 0 at all three levels (the `for`-loop de-structuring DIV-54 recorded as its one net-negative cell). Largest move off perfect: `coreutils factor::factor` 0 -> 12, DIV-54's own flagship win, which is the honest cost -- that source DID write the guard cascade and the compiler merged it, and nothing in the binary distinguishes the two. **Predicate fidelity**, the number a pre-split gate has to publish because the measurement identified the shape post hoc: against the post-hoc `sc_off > sc_on` proxy over 33,804 O0 functions the gate is **2,933 of 2,951 recall (99.4%) at 126 firings the proxy does not mark (95.9% precision)**, and **2,883 of its 3,059 firings (94.2%) reproduce the `option returndup off` body byte for byte**. Against the +630 post-hoc ceiling recomputed on the same instrument (+665 / -15 / -20) the implementation delivers **92%** of the perfect count at a smaller optimized-level cost (-28 against -35). Known recall gap: 18 O0 functions where a degenerate two-out block (both edges to the same target) sits between the operands and is removed later in P8 than this replay runs. **Method**: the two arms' bodies were resolved against the two arms the O0 measurement had already scored -- exact, not approximate, because GED is a function of the body text -- with the 312 hybrid bodies Joern-scored fresh; 809 unchanged-body controls reproduced their cached score exactly, and an independent `optsweep` run cross-checks **26,424 functions over 32 slices with 0 mismatches in either arm**. **Audit** (standing requirement 8) over all 3,059 changed O0 bodies: the set of called functions is identical in **3,059 of 3,059**; gotos -19/+14 (11 functions gain one); `while( true )` **-142/+25**, i.e. the gate un-destructures 142 loops for 25 it creates. **Speed**: whole-binary `decompile-all`, arms interleaved and pinned to the same four cores, minimum of 9 -- zlib `example` **-5.17%**, coreutils `ls` **+0.07%**, crazyflie `cf2.elf` (ARM Cortex-M) **-0.84%**, and iproute2 `ip` **+1.01%** (min of 5, unpinned); worst **+1.01%** against the 5% budget and two of four are net speedups, because the gate only ever declines a `node_split` and the structurer then runs on a smaller graph. Bundle: `docs/features/orchain/` |
| DIV-70 | `relocobjects` extended to COFF objects (scope extension of an existing default-ON option, no new flag) | **A COFF `.obj` exposed exactly one function.** A pre-link object stores every section at the section-relative VMA the linker will later reassign — i.e. all of them at 0. The ELF arm of that problem was already handled (DIV-8: `ET_REL` has no program headers at all, so the faithful loader mapped zero bytes and the synthetic layout was the fix), but the COFF arm was invisible because a COFF file *does* answer `file.segments()`: the loader mapped all sixteen segments on top of each other at address 0, `find_section` returned whichever sorted first, and the funcsym walk — deduped by address — kept the first symbol and dropped the rest. With one `.text` per object that is survivable, which is why the `coff_obj.obj` fixture never caught it; with **function-level linking** (MSVC `/Gy`, clang `-ffunction-sections`, one COMDAT `.text` per function — the default for real game and engine builds) every function but one disappears. On a 13-function Command & Conquer `combat.obj`, `kuna functions` reported **1**. Fixed by making the choice per-format instead of per-symptom: `ObjectFormat::relocatable_layout` decides whether a file needs the synthetic layout (ELF: relocatable *and* no program headers — the historical gate verbatim; COFF: relocatable, full stop; PE/Mach-O: never, so a Mach-O `.o` keeps today's path), and `ObjectFormat::is_alloc_section` answers which sections are memory-resident (ELF `SHF_ALLOC`; COFF `Characteristics` content bits minus the link-time-only `.drectve`/`LNK_REMOVE`/discardable `.debug$S` sections). Two smaller corrections ride along inside the now-shared layout: a **REL-style relocation table stores its addend in the field being patched** (COFF `DIR32`/`REL32`, and 32-bit ELF `.rel.*`), which the RELA-only math had been discarding, so the in-place value is read back signed and added; and an **empty section no longer takes a load VMA**, which would otherwise alias the next section's address (a COFF object leads with empty `.text`/`.data`/`.bss` placeholders) | **`combat.obj` (i386 MSVC, 13 COMDAT functions): 1 → 13 functions, 34 → 1,017 lines of C**, every one decompiling with its externs named (`__ftol`, `MapClass::operator`, `SpotLightClass::SpotLightClass`) and its `.bss`/`.rdata` references resolved; whole-object `decompile-all` 0.23 s → 2.28 s (a 13× larger job). Linked images of every format are untouched by construction — `relocatable_layout` is false for them, so not a byte of the mapped-image path runs differently. The existing single-`.text` COFF fixture keeps its names and bodies (its addresses simply rebase from 0x0/0x10 to 0x400000/0x400010) and `verify_coff_object` passes unchanged. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK** (no re-pin), `make test-stages` **PARITY OK** (433/433), `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` green. New gate `verify_coff_comdat` over a vendored `coff_comdat_i386.obj` (three COMDAT `.text` sections, a `.bss`, an undefined extern, both COFF relocation shapes, an `LNK_REMOVE` section): asserts the object really does report every section at VMA 0, that all three functions survive at distinct rebased VMAs, and that a **non-first** COMDAT function decompiles its real body |
| DIV-71 | `evalcurrentproto` (new kuna option, default-ON) | **An x86 Windows function that takes arguments rendered as `(void)`.** A compiler spec can nominate one of its registered models for evaluating a function's OWN unlocked prototype -- `x86win.cspec` declares `<resolveprototype name="__fastcall/__thiscall/__stdcall">` and then `<eval_current_prototype name=...>` -- and every consumer of that nomination was already ported and wired: `ActionPrototypeTypes` installs `glb->evalfp_current` on any unlocked prototype (coreaction.cc:4843), `ActionInputPrototype` calls `FuncProto::resolveModel` on it (fspec.cc:3772), and `ProtoModelMerged::selectModel` scores the active trials against each constituent. Only the `<eval_current_prototype>` **element itself** was never read (the registration pass that added named models deliberately stopped short of it, since honoring it changes which model every function is evaluated with). So `evalfp_current` stayed null, every function fell back to `<default_proto>` = `__stdcall`, whose `<input>` list is stack-only, and a `__fastcall`/`__thiscall` function's ECX/EDX arguments were not *possible* parameters at all: they survived as locals **read before they are ever written**, which is not something a C function can do. The merged-model machinery -- ported, tested, and reachable only by naming a model by hand -- was the dead consumer of exactly this fact. Reading the element is the whole fix: `p0_knowledge/kuna_evalcurrentproto.rs (eval_current_model_name)` resolves the nominated name against the model registry at `<default_proto>` time, `build_arch_handle` hands it to each function, and the union then resolves PER FUNCTION against the trials that are actually active, so a function that touches neither register still comes out `__stdcall`. Six vendored specs nominate a model (`x86win`, `x86borland`, `x86gcc` `__cdecl/__regparm`, `CR16`, `HCS12`, `HCS12X`); every other language has no such element and is untouched whichever way the option is set. Precedence is explicit: an explicit `option protoeval <model>` outranks the nomination (same slot), and the nomination outranks `option defaultprototype` for an unlocked prototype -- upstream's own ordering, since `evalfp_current` is consulted before `defaultfp`. `<eval_called_prototype>` is deliberately still unread: no vendored spec declares one | **675/675 PARITY OK with the raw flip -- no re-pin and no per-test opt-out on the datatest corpus** (only 3 of the 83 files are on an affected language, and all 11 of their assertions are unmoved). Stages **436/436**, with one per-test opt-out: `ghdec-cspecprotos.xml` exists to pin this very bug (`getfield(void)` with an `// ecx` local) and reach the fix by naming `__thiscall` by hand, so it now sets `option evalcurrentproto off` to keep testing what it was written for; the automatic path is the new two-pass `tests/stages/kuna-evalcurrentproto.xml`. **Measured on a 3,153-function i386 PE game binary**: functions rendering as `(void)` **1,553 -> 602**, functions carrying an ECX/EDX local read before it is written **2,128 -> 636**, 2,126 bodies changed. The witness the report named, `0x6ae390`, goes from `sub_6ae390(void)` with `int4 v4; // ecx` to `sub_6ae390(int4 a0,int4 a1)` -- the same two arguments IDA Pro reports for it. **Wrongness audit** (standing requirement 8) over all 3,153 functions: **2,126 gained arguments, 0 lost any**, and the set of called functions is identical in **3,152 of 3,153** -- the one exception is an unaligned `pushf`/`popf` flags-probe stub whose return widens to `int8`. The change is selective rather than blanket: a second i386 PE of comparable size moves only **36** functions, because the union resolves most of it to `__stdcall`. **Off-Windows check**, since `x86gcc` nominates `__cdecl/__regparm`: i386 `ls` 1 of 537 functions changes (`_start`, whose EAX/EDX really are live at process entry), i386 `all` 0 of 23, and `libpthread.so.0` 34 of 467 -- every one a genuine glibc `regparm` internal (`__pthread_mutex_lock_full(uint4 *a0)`, `change_stack_perm(int4 a0)`), recovered with the argument the source declares. **Speed**, whole-binary `decompile-all`, arms interleaved, minimum of 3: the 3,153-function PE **+0.27%** (159.98 -> 160.42 s), the second PE **+1.91%** (53.54 -> 54.56 s), and x86-64 `ls` -- a language with no `<eval_current_prototype>`, so the arms run identical code -- **+1.31%** (31.27 -> 31.68 s), which sets the noise floor of this instrument; worst **+1.91%** against the 5% budget. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK**, `make test-stages` **PARITY OK** (436/436), `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` OK lenient + strict, `kuna catalog --check` OK. Counters re-derived from the built artifact: live catalog 102, `catalog_bytecompat.rs` 102, `kuna_phases/tests.rs` 102 / tiers (22, 45, 35) / `},\n` 101 / live readers 31, `phase_catalog.json` 102, `docs/options.md` 102, `xml.rs` corpus 200 |
| DIV-72 | selecting an EXTERNAL reports an external (driver + loader bug fix, no new flag) | **Clicking `CellClass::Cell_Coord` in the browser inventory of a COFF object answered `/* decompile error: Unable to load 512 bytes at r0x00404080 */`.** A relocatable object's undefined symbols are bound to synthetic addresses in an extern area above the laid-out sections (DIV-8/DIV-70) purely so a *call* to one renders by name; nothing is mapped there and nothing ever will be, because the definition is in another translation unit. The whole-binary surfaces never reach one -- `function_entries_executable` drops them -- but the browser lists the canonical inventory, where every entry is a clickable row, so selecting one by name ran the lifter against unmapped memory and reported the byte-load failure as if the function had failed to decompile. The shared decompile step (`project.rs (decompile_targets)`, DIV-66) now asks `ConsoleProgram::entry_bytes_mapped` first and emits `// <name>: external symbol -- referenced here, defined in another module`. The probe is a **one-byte read, not a section-flag test**, so an address that is mapped but outside any CODE section (packed code in `.data`, a hand-picked `--addr`) still decompiles exactly as it did. `kuna decompile <extern>` -- which drives `decomp_dbg` as a subprocess and so has only its transcript -- recognizes the same signature and answers the same line instead of dumping a console log. Underneath it, a latent loader bug the probe exposed: **`loadFill` claimed `bufoffset` before reading and left it claimed when the fill failed**, so the buffer's own fast path served every later request within 512 bytes of a failed address out of a buffer that was never filled -- stale bytes, reported as a successful read. Upstream (loadimage_bfd.cc) leaves it claimed too; nothing there reads twice near a failure, so it never surfaced. kuna's per-entry probe walks a relocatable object's extern slots in address order and lands in exactly that window. The failure path now restores the `~0` sentinel | **The reported case, before -> after**: `kuna_wasm decompile 'CellClass::Cell_Coord'` on the 13-function COFF object goes from `error: "Unable to load 512 bytes at r0x00404080"` with no code, to the external-symbol line with no error -- so the browser renders it as a comment through its normal path, with no `decompile error` tag and no `bad` row styling (no UI change was needed). The read-cache half is visible in the same binary's inventory classification: **56 entries all classified `func` before; 13 `func` + 43 `plt` after** -- one extern probed unmapped and the next forty out of the poisoned window looked mapped, which is precisely the bug. The externs now sort into the UI's existing 'imports & thunks' group. **Whole-binary output is unchanged**, byte for byte, on every format A/B'd against the merge-base build: `/usr/bin/ls` (278 functions), a 3,015-function PE, `ptx.o` (26), `fid_lib_x86_64.o` (3), `macho_min.o` (2), `pe_min.obj` (2), `coff_comdat_i386.obj` (3) and `combat.obj` (13) -- 0 differing functions in each, because a successful read never poisoned the cache and the whole-binary target set never contained an unmapped entry. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK**, `make test-stages` **PARITY OK**, `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` green. New gate `verify_external_entries` over the vendored `ptx.o`: selecting an external returns an external-symbol body and no error, and EVERY extern slot probes unmapped (the neighbour test that fails on the poisoned buffer), with a real function as the positive control |
| DIV-73 | demangled operator names keep their spelling (loader bug fix, no new flag) | **Every bracket-spelled C++ operator demangled to a bare `operator`.** The name-only reduction the `::`-scope splitter requires strips `<...>`, `(...)` and `[...]` groups from the demangled string -- correct for template arguments and the signature tail, but `operator[]`, `operator()`, `operator<`, `operator<<`, `operator>>`, `operator->` and `operator new[]`/`delete[]` are SPELLED with those characters, so the reduction ate the operator itself. Every such overload of a class therefore arrived at the symbol table under the same name, and (for `operator<`) the unmatched `<` also swallowed the rest of the string. `strip_bracket_groups` now exempts a bracket run that directly follows an operator head (`operator`, `operator new`, `operator delete`), copying the operator's own spelling verbatim and resuming the strip afterwards, so the parameter list that follows still goes. A `<` followed by an identifier character is left to the generic path -- there it opens a template argument list, not an operator | **Measured on `libstdc++.so.6` (5,945 entries): 178 names corrected, entry count unchanged.** The library's largest name collision was `std::operator` with **65 entries sharing one name**; it is now `std::operator<<` (33) and `std::operator>>` (32). `operator new[]`/`operator delete[]` stop reporting as `operator new`/`operator delete`. On the i386 COFF object that surfaced this, `MapClass::operator[]` and `Random2Class::operator()` now match **IDA Pro's demangler exactly**, closing the last naming difference between the two inventories (13/13 functions and 43/43 externals already agreed). Both manglings are covered -- the reduction is shared by the MSVC (`msvc_demangler`) and Itanium (`cpp_demangle`) arms, and the new unit tests assert the same overload set through both. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK**, `make test-stages` **PARITY OK**, `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` OK lenient + strict |
| DIV-74 | `msvcftol` (new kuna option, default-ON) | **Every MSVC x86-32 float-to-integer conversion decompiled to an argument-less `__ftol()` and took its input dataflow with it.** MSVC compiles `(int)d` as `fld <src>; call __ftol`: the value arrives in the x87 stack top `ST0`, the `__int64` result returns in `EDX:EAX`. No vendored x86 prototype model has an `<input>` `<pentry>` naming an x87 register -- `ST0` appears only as an `<output>` pentry (which is why a *return* in `ST0` has always worked) and in `<killedbycall>` -- so `FuncProto::characterize_as_input_param(ST0, 10)` answers `NoContainment`, `Heritage::guard_calls` never appends the argument, nothing reads `ST0`, and the whole feeding `FLD` chain is dead-code eliminated together with every register it was based on. `<killedbycall>` is NOT the cause: stripping `ST0`/`ST1` from every `killedbycall` list in a copied spec tree leaves the output byte-identical. Widening a shared prototype model with an `ST0` input pentry is not the fix either -- measured, it invents a phantom `undefined10 // st0` first argument on unrelated stack-passing callees such as `fastmath::sqrt`. The fix is the mechanism upstream already ships for `alloca_probe`/`mcount`/`__security_check_cookie` and Hex-Rays ships as its compiler-helper table: a `<callfixup>` that REPLACES the CALL with the helper's p-code, after which the argument is an ordinary `ST0` read and the conversion prints as a plain C cast. The payload is synthesized in Rust (`p2_lift/kuna_msvcftol.rs`) and registered through the same `decode_inject` path as the cspec's own, so `specs/` stays byte-identical to upstream. Body: `ESP = ESP + 4` (mandatory -- x86 `CALL rel32` lifts as `push44(&:4 inst_next); call rel32`, and without the pop the three pushed return addresses surfaced as arguments to the next call), a 64-bit `trunc(ST0)` split into `EDX:EAX`, then the x87 pop. Modelling the result at 32 bits with `EDX = EAX s>> 31` would render the common `(int)d` case one cast shorter but silently produce the wrong high word for `(__int64)d`, so the helper is modelled at its real width and `(int)d` reads `(int4)(int8)x`. Registration is guarded to x86-32 (every named register resolves, 4-byte code space); it is unconditional rather than option-gated because the architecture bootstraps at `load file`, before the console's `option` lines, so the user-visible gate sits on the *install* -- the analysis-tier call-fixup installer drops this payload's targets from its match map when off | **Command & Conquer `bounce.obj` (i386 MSVC COFF): 21 argument-less `__ftol()` calls -> 0; `combat.obj`: 14 -> 0.** `BounceClass::Get_Bounce_Coord` goes from three bare `__ftol()` calls writing three unrelated locals to `*a1 = (int4)(int8)*(float4 *)(a0 + 0x18); a1[1] = ...; a1[2] = ...` -- which is the ground truth in the listing (`FLD [ESI+0x18]; CALL __ftol; MOV EDI,EAX` x3) -- and recovers the `__thiscall` `this` pointer as a second consequence, since the `FLD`s that keep `ESI` live are no longer dead. **Byte-identical wherever the fixup does not fire**, A/B'd on/off: `i386_pie_nl` (3,800 lines), `grep` x86-64 (18,634 lines), `coff_comdat_i386.obj` -- 0 differing lines each. **Speed**, interleaved, minimum of 3: on an i386 binary with no `__ftol` the arms are indistinguishable (on 1.850 s vs off 2.199 s -- the on-arm is *faster*, i.e. the extra snippet registration and compile is below the noise floor), so the no-fire cost is nil. On `combat.obj`, where it fires 14 times, 1.991 -> 2.243 s (+12.7%): that arm decompiles strictly MORE code, because the float dataflow feeding every conversion stops being dead. **0/675 by construction**: `grep -rn ftol` over `tests/datatests` and `tests/stages` is empty, so no corpus function can match a target. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK** (no re-pin), `make test-stages` **PARITY OK** (454/454), `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` green, `kuna catalog --check` OK. New two-pass gate `tests/stages/kuna-msvcftol.xml` over a new 158-byte hand-built i386 COFF fixture (`ftol_i386.obj`, source `ftol_i386.s` alongside it): option off = two `__ftol()` calls, default = both conversions as casts of the fields they actually read. Counters re-derived from the built artifact: live catalog 105, `catalog_bytecompat.rs` 105, `kuna_phases/tests.rs` 105 / tiers (22, 47, 36), `phase_catalog.json` 105, `docs/options.md` 105, `xml.rs` corpus 203 |
| DIV-75 | `ctypes` (new kuna option, default-OFF in the catalog, ON in the `aggressive` preset) | **The emitted C is not C, and it mixes two vocabularies inside one function.** kuna interns its core types under the literal names `uint1`/`int4`/`float8`/`float10`/`code` -- a verbatim port of upstream's no-`<coretypes>` fallback branch (sleigh_arch.cc:204), which the real Ghidra application never takes because its Java side supplies its own names over the wire. The printer prints those names. The DIV-6 `realtypes` relabel covers only residual `TYPE_UNKNOWN`, so `xunknown4` renders `unsigned int` while the genuine `TYPE_UINT` core type `uint4` beside it does not: one function declares `unsigned int v3;` and `int4 v1;` in the same block. `p9_emit/kuna_ctypes.rs (core_type_spelling)` extends the same single chokepoint (`declarator_parts`, which `push_cast_type`, `type_name_for_decl` and the `--json` `type_to_c_string` all route through) to every core type, matching the type's SIZE against the target's own declared widths in declaration order, first hit wins -- the port of Ghidra's `DataOrganizationImpl.getIntegerCTypeApproximation`. Declaration order is what makes it per-architecture: under LP64 both `long` and `long long` are 8 bytes and an 8-byte integer must read `long`, while under ILP32/LLP64 `long` is 4 and the same size lands on `long long`. Three cases are decided rather than left to fall out of the table: a 1-byte integer is `signed char`/`unsigned char`, never bare `char` (implementation-defined signedness, and kuna reserves `char` for text); `code` becomes `void`, so `code *` reads `void *`; and a float wider than `double` with no exact match spells `long double` -- an approximation of storage, since no target has a 10-byte `sizeof` and the x86 cspecs record 10 as the x87 VALUE width, and deliberately the same approximation the recompile prelude already makes so the emitted `.c` and `.h` cannot disagree. Integer widths with no C type at all (3/5/6/7, 16-byte) keep `undefined<N>` and are NOT widened: `(undefined3)x` is a 24-bit truncation and `(unsigned int)x` is not. Renaming the interned core types instead was rejected on the merits, not just on churn: a core type's id is `hash_name(name)`, Ghidra-style identifiers derive from the first character of the type's name (`float8` is what makes `fVar1`), and the console's C-type parser resolves base types solely through `TypeFactory::find_by_name`, which 269 `<com> parse line` script lines across 87 corpus files feed `int4`/`float8`. This is a rendering concern and is fixed at the rendering chokepoint | **Invalid-C type tokens in the emitted output, `off` -> `on`**: the reported i386 MSVC COFF `bounce.obj` **301 -> 1**, x86-64 `grep` **5,153 -> 11**, i386 `i386_pie_nl` **667 -> 0**. Every survivor is an `undefined3`/`undefined7` -- a width no C type has, kept rather than widened by design. Whole-binary sweeps on `combat.obj`, `libselinux.so.1`, `fmt_aarch64` and `mips_gp_le32` leave no core-type name at all. The per-architecture claim is gated by comparison, not assertion: `ctypes_per_arch` decompiles x86-64 and i386 in one test and pins that LP64 spells an 8-byte unsigned `unsigned long` and never reaches `long long`, while ILP32 spells the same type `long long` -- the single most likely way a size-to-name table is wrong, and invisible on either target alone. A fourth test asserts the headline contract directly: NO Ghidra core-type name survives on any of four data models. **Gating**: the shipped catalog default is `off`, because 42 of the 675 datatest assertions and 43 of the stage assertions pin the Ghidra spellings and the parity harness applies no mode -- so `make test` and `make test-stages` are untouched, with no per-test opt-out and no baseline re-pin. Preset membership is what makes valid C the actual default rendering: `auto` selects `aggressive` for anything under 500 KiB, i.e. for `kuna decompile`, `decompile-all`, `decompile-project`, the web front-end and the benchmark. **Known limit**: a binary at or above 500 KiB gets `reliable`, whose overrides are deliberately empty, so it keeps the Ghidra vocabulary until the catalog default itself is flipped (which needs the ~39 corpus opt-outs and is its own PR). **Speed**: pure rendering, no measurable cost -- interleaved, minimum of 3 on `i386_pie_nl`, on 1.641 s vs off 1.830 s (the on-arm is faster, i.e. below the noise floor). Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK**, `make test-stages` **PARITY OK** (460/460), `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` green, `kuna catalog --check` OK. New two-pass gate `tests/stages/kuna-ctypes.xml` plus the 9-case speller unit suite and the 4-case cross-architecture CLI gate. Counters re-derived from the built artifact: live catalog 106, `catalog_bytecompat.rs` 106, `kuna_phases/tests.rs` 106 / tiers (23, 47, 36) / `},\n` 105 / live readers 33, `phase_catalog.json` 106, `docs/options.md` 106, `xml.rs` corpus 204 |
| DIV-76 | `datasyms` (new kuna option, default-ON; the option-gate for DIV-26's ELF data-symbol naming, GH-184) | **GH-184: kuna printed `__fprintf_chk(dat_61a0,...)` where every rival prints `stderr`.** The loader collected only `SymbolKind::Text` symbols, so no `STT_OBJECT` entry of `.symtab`/`.dynsym` was ever imported and the only producer of named data globals was the DWARF pass -- which structurally cannot reach a copy-relocated (`R_X86_64_COPY`) libc extern: `stderr` has a real `.bss` address in the importing binary and a `.dynsym` entry, but no DIE in the program's own `.debug_info`. The mechanism itself landed in DIV-26 (defined, named, non-zero-`st_size` OBJECT entries of both tables, `@VERSION` stripped, first-wins address dedupe with `.symtab` before `.dynsym`, installed as `undefined<st_size>` globals with `namelock` only, committed LAST so DWARF extents and string-literal typelocks keep precedence; zero-size entries dropped because the linker's boundary markers `__bss_start`/`_edata`/`_end` are exactly the sizeless ones) -- but it shipped **flagless**, against the standing anything-that-changes-emitted-C-ships-behind-an-option rule, with no catalog row, no witness tests, and spec prose reading "carries no flag". This row is the completion: the arm is now gated by `--option datasyms on|off`. The gate shape is the interesting part: unlike `relocobjects`/`i386_pie_plt` (load-time layout, env-var bridged), the data stream is *collected* at `load file` but *committed* at `read symbols` -- after the `option` lines on both CLI paths -- so the gate is the plain `Architecture::analysis_datasyms` flag consulted at `commit_analysis_output` arm 4a, with **no env bridge**, the same stash-at-load/gate-at-commit shape as `cppproto`. The stash drains either way, so a second `read symbols` cannot re-commit | **Witness (`tests/bug-repro/faillog @ 0x3320`, both call sites): off `__fprintf_chk(dat_61a0,...)` / default `__fprintf_chk(stderr,...)`** -- verified on BOTH CLI paths (in-process `decompile-all` and the subprocess `decompile` script, whose `option` lines precede `read symbols`). Whole-binary defaults: `grep` names `stderr`x11/`optarg`x27/`__progname`, `sort` names `stderr`x12/`optarg`x23/`optind`x14. **Collateral: base-vs-branch `decompile-all --json` byte-IDENTICAL on all four of `faillog`/`grep`/`sort`/`libselinux.so.1`** (default-ON gate over an unchanged mechanism = structural no-op; the off arm is the only new behavior).
