Datatest evidence trivially 0/675 and 0/460: the XML path never constructs an `ObjectLoadImage`, so no stage XML is possible and the cargo suite hosts the two-pass bug/fix pair instead (`verify_loader_data_symbols.rs`: default asserts `stderr` and no `dat_61a0` at 0x3320, off asserts the reverse) plus a `kuna-analysis` fact-level unit test over the vendored `datasyms_faillog_x86_64` (md5-identical to `tests/bug-repro/faillog`) pinning exactly `stdout@0x6160/8`, `stderr@0x61a0/8`, `optind@0x6168/4`, `optarg@0x6180/8`. **Speed**: one bool read per analysis commit -- interleaved, minimum of 11 on whole-binary `decompile-all` of `grep`: base 15.797 s vs gated 15.669 s (**-0.81%**, the gated arm is faster, i.e. below the noise floor). Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK**, `make test-stages` **PARITY OK** (460/460), `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` OK, `kuna catalog --check` OK. Counters re-derived from the built artifact: live catalog 107, `catalog_bytecompat.rs` 107, `kuna_phases/tests.rs` 107 / tiers (23, 47, 37) / `},\n` 106 / live readers 33 (datasyms joins the no-codegen-live-reader gates, read via `kuna_live_value`), `phase_catalog.json` 107, `docs/options.md` 107; `tests/stages/kuna-catalog.xml` unmoved (ranged #5/#9; joins no exact bucket) |
| DIV-77 | `loadguardrange` (new kuna option, default-ON; completes the stubbed LoadGuard/StoreGuard ValueSet range refinement, GH-182) | **GH-182: for a stack array written through a computed index, kuna printed a bounded array object and then subscripted it past its own declared bound.** betaflight `cliServo` declared `int2 v5 [4]` for the source's `int16_t arguments[6]`, wrote it at `v5[v2]` under a guard proving `v2` reaches 5, and split elements 4/5 off as separate stack scalars that are range-checked and stored into the servo config but **never assigned** -- a fake stack buffer overflow plus fake uninitialized reads, carried verbatim into the `decompile-project` recompile artifacts. Root cause: the only index bound P6 ever saw was `gatherOpen`'s `min_items = 3` fallback, because the whole upstream refinement chain was stubbed -- `ValueSetSolver::establishValueSets`/`solve` were `Err` shells, `LoadGuard::establishRange`/`finalizeRange` + `Heritage::analyzeNewLoadGuards`/`handleNewLoadCopies` were `STUB(W6)`, and `gather_open` skipped the `MapState::addGuard` loops (which upstream gates on `step != 0`, so restoring the loops alone does nothing). This PR ports all three parts from the pinned upstream: (1) the ValueSetSolver IR binding in `p5_types/rangeutil.rs` (setVarnode/iterate/computeTypeCode, the CBRANCH constraint pipeline `generateConstraints`/`applyConstraints`/`constraintsFromPath` with relative-constant support, the live `ValueSetEdge` graph over descend edges, and the solve driver over the already-ported Bourdoncle ordering + wideners; the C++ `Varnode->ValueSet` back-pointer and mark bits become a `VarnodeId -> node` map + side sets on the solver, observationally identical since upstream always sets them together), (2) `LoadGuard::establish_range`/`finalize_range` + `analyze_new_load_guards` (WidenerNone establish pass, WidenerFull finalize pass, range locking at `analysisState == 2`) + the full `handle_new_load_copies`/`find_address_forces`/`propagate_copy_away` chain (live path is the faithful empty-list early return while `guardLoads` stays behind the disabled `highPtrPossible` seam), called at the upstream point at the end of each heritage pass, and (3) `Funcdata::add_guard` -- `MapState::addGuard` (varmap.cc:1003) with the `gatherOpen` guard loops, supplying `highind = ((max-min)+1)/step - 1`. Two documented defensive deviations: upstream's post-`break` update loop can `establishRange` one already-analyzed boundary guard through a dereferenced `readNodes.find()==end()` (UB) -- the port skips guards with no read node; and solver inputs outside the system degrade to set-full instead of a null deref. Default-ON because this *is* stock upstream Ghidra behavior; `option loadguardrange off` reproduces the pre-port output byte-identically | **Witness (`tests/bug-repro/betaflight_STM32F405.elf @ 0x801b4a4`): off `int2 v7 [4]` + never-assigned `v8`/`v9` read into the config store / default ONE `int2 v7 [8]` with every access in bounds (`v7[4]`,`v7[5]`) -- the same `[8]` shape stock Ghidra prints (open range fills to the next mapped local).** The not-vendored second witness (decbench diffutils `diff3` main @ 0x2a20) is unchanged BY DESIGN: its bound is data-dependent (`rev_mapping[mapping[i]]`), which upstream's solver cannot derive either (full-range LOAD index, no step, `addGuard` bails). **Collateral** (base-vs-branch `decompile-all --json`, every changed function reviewed): grep 5/449, sort 2/490, faillog 0/69, libselinux.so.1 3/502, betaflight 8/5797 changed; every change is (a) an indexed array gaining its real bound (`[4]`->`[5]`/`[8]`/`[9]`, four scalars -> `char *[4]` string table), (b) an over-wide open-fill narrowing to the guard range -- libselinux `get_default_type` and betaflight `sub_8025944` verified **shape-identical to stock Ghidra headless** output, or (c) dead copy-shadows eliminated because `Merge`'s untied-call intersection now tests the real store range. **Speed** (interleaved on/off, same binary): grep whole-binary min-of-11 on 13.610 s vs off 13.621 s (**-0.08%**, below the noise floor); betaflight (5,797 functions, guard-rich) min-of-3 on 59.212 s vs off 58.670 s (**+0.92%**). Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK** (the flip changes 0 assertions -- the corpus asserts upstream behavior WITH this machinery live), `make test-stages` **PARITY OK** (481/481), `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` green, `kuna catalog --check` OK. New two-pass gate `tests/stages/gh182-loadguardrange.xml` (the real Thumb `cliServo` bytes, callees stubbed): default = the in-bounds `[8]` array, off = the `[4]` cap + split-off never-assigned scalars. Counters re-derived from the rebased build: live catalog 108, `catalog_bytecompat.rs` 108, `kuna_phases/tests.rs` 108 / tiers (24, 47, 37) / `},\n` 107 / live readers 34, `phase_catalog.json` 108, `docs/options.md` 108, `xml.rs` corpus 207; no new scheduled Action (the refinement runs inside the existing heritage pass, as upstream), so the action-tree oracle and adjacency allowlist are untouched |
| DIV-78 | ELF known-no-return name list (data; no new option -- it rides the already-default-ON `noreturn_known` + `noreturn_externmatch`) | **GH-273: every clang/g++ C++ binary emitted the dead code after a `std::__throw_*` call as if it ran.** Every helper in libstdc++'s `<bits/functexcept.h>` (plus `__throw_regex_error` from `<bits/regex_error.h>`) is declared `__attribute__((__noreturn__))` and every body ends in `_GLIBCXX_THROW_OR_ABORT` -- a `throw`, or `abort()` when the library is built `-fno-exceptions` -- so none of the 20 can return. Upstream Ghidra's `ElfFunctionsThatDoNotReturn` names `__cxa_throw` and the terminate entry points but omits the whole family, and the source attribute survives into no binary artifact: at the decompiler's boundary `std::__throw_length_error` is an ordinary UND `.dynsym` import reached through a PLT stub, so nothing but the shipped list can prove the call cannot return. Flow therefore followed the fall-through and emitted whatever the compiler had parked after the call -- clang's `call __stack_chk_fail` unreachable-trap, the next function's entry, or (apt-get `sub_6480`) the exception-cleanup landing pad, rendered as `__cxa_begin_catch(std::__throw_logic_error(...)); ... _Unwind_Resume()`. The gap was masked until #272: `noreturn_disc`'s evidence tally counts a call site whose fall-through 'is not a valid instruction start', and a *failed decode* satisfied that test, so clang's 15-byte alignment NOP was silently voting `std::__throw_length_error` no-return; with the padding decoding the tally drops below its 3-site floor and the dead code reappeared. **Shape of the fix**: the 20 helpers are appended to the vendored ELF list in place, fenced by a `# (kuna divergence, DIV-78)` comment block exactly as DIV-21's libc additions are -- the file is not covered by `tools/sync_upstream.py` (whose vendored scope is `tests/datatests/` plus `Ghidra/Processors/*/data/languages/`), so an in-place block cannot conflict with a future upstream sync, and both `include_str!` consumers pick it up with no new wiring. Each helper is listed **twice**, because the two matchers see two spellings of the same symbol: the analysis-tier scan matches the **mangled** `.dynsym` name before demangling (written as a trailing-`*` wildcard over the Itanium `ZSt<len>__throw_<name>` prefix, so a signature change -- `__throw_ios_failure` gained a `const char*, int` overload in GCC 7 -- cannot age the entry out), while the flow-time matcher sees the **demangled** display name, which the `std`-only namespace guard admits and the leading-underscore strip reduces to `throw_length_error`. No new option: the behavior is already gated by `noreturn_known` (analysis tier, default-on) and `noreturn_externmatch` (flow seam, default-on, `include_str!`s the same file); `--option noreturn_known off --option noreturn_externmatch off` restores the pre-fix rendering byte-identically. NB `noreturn_extern` (DIV-14) does **not** read the file -- it carries a frozen hard-coded copy of upstream's 21 names, missing DIV-21's additions too -- but it is queried only after `noreturn_externmatch` declines, so it is subsumed; that pre-existing duplication is left alone here | **Witness 1, `/usr/bin/FileCheck-14` (clang-14, 528 KB, 1,155 functions -- the GH-273 binary): 72 functions change, every one of them contains a `std::__throw_*` call, ZERO grow, `// no-return` annotations 69 -> 102, emitted lines 63,247 -> 63,141.** Smallest case `sub_44eb10`: `std::__throw_length_error("basic_string::append")` now terminates the function instead of falling into 0x44eb40 (the next function's entry). **Witness 2, `/usr/bin/apt-get` (gcc, PIE, 124 functions -- a different compiler and a different family member set: `__throw_logic_error`, `__throw_bad_cast`, `__throw_out_of_range_fmt`, `__throw_length_error`): 6 change, all shrink, annotations 11 -> 17, lines 2,493 -> 2,469**; `sub_7890` loses a `label_9355:` chain of three consecutive `__throw_length_error` calls the fall-through had cascaded through, `sub_6480` loses the bogus `__cxa_begin_catch`/`_Unwind_Resume` landing-pad body. Witness 3, `/usr/bin/clang-format-14` (242 functions): 13 change, all shrink, annotations 12 -> 16, lines 6,867 -> 6,834. **Collateral on C binaries is exactly zero** (base-vs-branch `decompile-all --json`): grep 0/449, sort 0/490, faillog 0/69, libselinux.so.1 0/502 -- byte-identical, as it must be, since no C ELF imports a `std::__throw_*`. Every changed function on all three C++ witnesses was reviewed; three removals spot-checked against `objdump` (FileCheck `0x40e5ab call __throw_length_error` + `0x40e5b0 call __stack_chk_fail`; `0x44eb35` + the 15-byte NOP + the next function entry at `0x44eb40`; `0x43a7e3 call __throw_system_error` -- whose successor `0x43a7e8` IS an independent branch target from `0x43a735 jne`, and is correctly still emitted). **Speed**: interleaved min-of-11 `kuna decompile-all /usr/bin/FileCheck-14 --json` on this box (siblings building concurrently, so the spread is wide -- the min is the signal): base **68.64 s** vs branch **66.01 s** (**-3.83%**), means 76.30 s -> 74.19 s (-2.76%). A small WIN, as expected: 106 fewer emitted lines and 72 functions with a shorter CFG is less work, not more; the list itself is 40 extra lines parsed once per load. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK** (no re-pin -- no datatest imports a libstdc++ throw helper), `make test-stages` **PARITY OK** (481/481), `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` green. Tests: `kuna-console/tests/verify_cxx_throw_noreturn.rs` (two-pass e2e over a new clang++-built fixture `kuna-analysis/tests/fixtures/cxxthrow_noreturn_x86_64`: default = the throw terminates, list consumers off = the dead `* 7` / `* 11` tail returns) plus two matcher tests (`kuna-analysis` `div78_cxx_throw_family_present_both_spellings` + the fixture's PLT-stub address, `kuna-decomp` `matches_cxx_throw_family_demangled_and_mangled`). No new option, so no catalog counts move (live catalog stays at 109 on the rebased tree, the value #309 left) and no stages XML was added (the no-return list is consumed by the analysis tier, which the XML `<binaryimage>` path does not run), so `xml.rs`'s corpus count stays 207 |
| DIV-79 | `relocrebase` (new kuna option, default-ON; rebases the load-time analysis facts of a relocatable object into the loaded image's address space, GH-289) | **GH-289: `kuna functions` and `kuna decompile-all` disagreed on how many functions a `.o` has, and a `-g` object's DWARF, strings and named globals never attached at all.** The loader lays a relocatable object (ELF `ET_REL`, COFF `.obj`) out synthetically above `RELOC_BASE` (DIV-8/DIV-70), so every address the engine holds is post-layout; the LOAD-TIME analysis passes re-parse the same file through their own `object::File` and compute PRE-LINK, section-relative addresses, and the two spaces then mixed in one inventory. On the in-repo `ptx.o`, `kuna functions` reported 95 entries — the 26 real functions plus 27 phantoms at `0x0`, `0x20`, `0x34`, … — while `decompile-all` (which filters to the loader's CODE sections) reported the correct 26, violating the inventory-agreement invariant DIV-68 established. The phantoms were the `.eh_frame` FDE oracle reading `initial_location` fields whose PC-relative relocation the linker had not yet applied (each field reads back as its own section offset), plus one DWARF `DW_AT_low_pc` that reads 0 for every subprogram because its relocation was never applied — which is also why the whole object's DWARF landed on one phantom function named after whatever string sits at `.debug_str`+0. #286 had fixed the Listing/xref half by DECLINING for such an object; declining here would have thrown real information away, since a `-g` `.o` carries full DWARF. So this rebases instead, and — because a fact is a bare `u64` by the time it reaches `AnalysisOutput`, and in a relocatable object every section sits at address 0 (`.text`+0x20 and `.rodata`+0x20 are the same number) — it rebases the analyzer tier's INPUT rather than each output fact: `loader/kuna_relocrebase.rs` re-presents the object with each laid-out section carrying the loader's own relocated bytes and its load VMA, each unlaid `.debug_*` section's relocations applied (a target in a laid-out section resolves to its load VMA, a debug-to-debug target to its own section-relative offset), and each ELF symbol shifted by its OWN section's delta — sections are laid out non-contiguously, so there is no single global offset. A COFF symbol needs no separate shift (`object` reports it as `VirtualAddress + value`). A fact that still lands in no laid-out section is DROPPED rather than passed through unrebased; the one exception is a `NoReturnFact`, retained with its address zeroed because the commit resolves it by NAME (an undefined `exit` in a `.o` has always had address 0). Default-ON because the addresses it replaces are provably wrong, not a judgement call: `ptx.o`'s `functions` goes 95 → 68 with zero entries below `RELOC_BASE` and `decompile-all` still 26 (the two now agree), its recognized string literals go 2 → 41, its DWARF parameter names and types apply (`char to_uchar(char ch)`, `ignore_case`/`folded_chars` instead of `dat_403520`/`0x403160`), `arm_thumb_le32.o` goes from decompiling NOTHING to both functions (the ARM `TMode` context paints now land at the rebased addresses), and `msvc_mangled.obj` recovers its `Bar *this`. Loader-tier, so the gate is the `KUNA_RELOCREBASE` env bridge (the analyzer tier runs inside `load file`, upstream of every per-function `option`); `--option relocrebase off` restores the pre-fix behaviour byte for byte. A linked image is never touched — the whole feature is structurally bound to `reloc_object::is_synthetically_laid_out`, so all four gates and every executable in `tests/bug-repro/` are byte-identical. Mach-O `.o` is deliberately out of scope (it is not synthetically laid out — its sections carry real addresses — so its analysis facts were already in the loaded space), and the MSVC CodeView (`.debug$S`) reader is too: kuna's `pdb` pass is PE-only and reads an external `.pdb`, never a `.obj`'s embedded CodeView. |
| DIV-81 | `cleanupcode` (new kuna option, default-ON; deletes the Rust drop/deallocate call sites, ported from SEFCOM Oxidizer's `CleanupCodeRemover`) | **A decompiled Rust function is mostly resource-management glue that is nowhere in the source.** Rust emits a drop call at every scope exit and every `?` early return, so the showcase `FakeCrypt::fileops::encrypt_file` renders eight `core::ptr::drop_in_place<...>(v)` statements and a four-deep if/else pyramid built around them, none of which a reader wants. **Shape of the fix**: a new `deadcode`-group Action registered at the TOP of `mainloop` (`p2_lift/kuna_cleanupcode.rs::ActionRemoveCleanupCode`), self-gated on `get_heritage_pass() == 0` -- the pre-SSA window `kuna_loweredswitch`/`kuna_outline` already use, and the analogue of Oxidizer's `STAGE = BEFORE_VARIABLE_RECOVERY`. Timing IS the mechanism: before heritage there are no INDIRECT call effects to unpick and no MULTIEQUAL to patch, CALL is not a control-transfer op so no edge moves, and -- the payoff -- the register/stack writes that existed only to set up the drop's arguments lose their last reader and are collected by the ordinary `ActionDeadCode` fixpoint. Each victim goes with the stock `delete_call_specs` + `op_destroy` pair `block_remove_internal` already uses; a call whose output still has a reader is skipped. **Matching is normalize-then-compare-exactly**, reproducing Oxidizer's `normalize(monopolize=True, use_trait_name=True)`: un-escape the legacy rustc `$LT$`/`$GT$`/`$C$`/`$uXX$` sequences and the `..` separator, then collapse the INNERMOST angle-bracket group repeatedly -- deleting a plain generic argument list, replacing a `<T as Trait>` qualified path with the TRAIT name -- so `_$LT$alloc..vec..Vec$LT$T$C$A$GT$$u20$as$u20$core..ops..drop..Drop$GT$::drop` reduces to `core::ops::drop::Drop::drop` and matches, while `FakeCrypt::fileops::drop_ransom_note` and `<T as crossbeam_epoch::atomic::Pointable>::drop` do not (which is why a `starts_with` test would be wrong). **Two deliberate departures from Oxidizer.** (1) Its `free`, `close` and `_close` entries are NOT carried: kuna's primary corpus is C, and deleting `free()` from C output would be a catastrophically wrong answer. What remains cannot occur in a C program, and THAT is the whole default-ON argument -- the pass is structurally inert on a C binary, so no compiler-detection channel from the loader to the engine is needed (there is none). Its `smallvec::deallocate` is dropped as unwitnessed. (2) The pass declines outright when the analyzed function is ITSELF one of the listed names: a drop routine's body is calls to other drop routines, and that is its content -- unguarded, 25 of the 26 functions that grew were a `<RawVec<T,A> as Drop>::drop` whose bare tail call to `RawVecInner::deallocate` was its only statement, leaving the argument registers rendered as a `-> [u8; 16]` return. rustc's v0 mangling renders the drop shim without its `core::ptr::` prefix, so a bare `drop_in_place<T>` is matched too -- but only when the name really carries generic arguments, which no C symbol does | **Witness (showcase), `FakeCrypt::fileops::encrypt_file` @0x55300: 81 -> 68 emitted lines (-16.0%), 8 `drop_in_place` calls -> 0**, and the four-deep if/else pyramid the shared drop tail forced collapses into four flat early returns. **Whole binary, `FakeCrypt` (rustc 1.90, 4.9 MB, 4194 functions): 227 functions change (5.4%), 363 cleanup call statements removed, emitted lines 126,670 -> 125,720 (-950); within the 227 changed functions 19,363 -> 18,413 (-4.91%)**; 217 shrink, 9 keep their length (all structural improvements -- `Iterator::fold` flattens to an early return plus a flat loop), 1 grows (`once_cell::imp::wait`, +1 line, both its drop calls still removed and its first parameter better typed); decompile errors 3 -> 3. **Second Rust witness, a `rustc -O` demo (3.9 MB, 605 functions): 36 change (6.0%), 69 cleanup call statements removed, 62,627 -> 60,023 lines (-4.16% overall, -17.49% within the changed functions)**; 34 shrink, 2 grow. **Collateral on C is exactly zero**: base-vs-branch `decompile-all --json` over faillog 0/69, grep 0/449, sort 0/490, libselinux.so.1 0/621, the ET_REL `fid/lib.o` 0/3 and the ARM Cortex-M `betaflight_STM32F405.elf` 0/5797 -- **0 differing `code` fields across 7,429 C functions**, as it must be, since no C ELF resolves a call to a `core::ptr::` path. **Speed is inside the budget on both sides of the gate**: interleaved min-of-N `kuna decompile-all --json` on this box -- C, where the pass is inert, `tests/bug-repro/grep` (9 pairs) min **14.181 s -> 13.959 s (-1.56%)**, median -0.62%; Rust, where it does all its work, the `rustc -O` demo (5 pairs) min **85.399 s -> 85.933 s (+0.63%)**, median -0.15%. One honest cost: on the small Rust demo the single pathological 2,400-line `std::backtrace_rs::symbolize::gimli::Context::new` goes 10.4 s -> 11.4 s (removing a CALL removes a caller-save/INDIRECT barrier, so values flow further and heritage/merge/types do more work) and crosses `--mode fast`'s 10 s per-function budget, becoming an `error` record; FakeCrypt's error count is unchanged at 3/4194. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK** (no re-pin -- no datatest binary carries a Rust drop symbol, so the flip is structurally byte-identical), `make test-stages` **PARITY OK** (498/498, 5 new), `make rust-test` **4694 passed / 0 failed**, `make check-spec` green lenient and strict, `kuna catalog --check` OK. Test: `tests/stages/oxidizer-cleanupcode.xml`, two-pass, asserting the drop call, the dealloc call AND both argument constants appear exactly once across the two `print C` passes (present with the option off, gone by default) while the real neighbouring call appears in both |
| DIV-84 | `dynrelocs` (new kuna option, default-ON; applies a LINKED image's dynamic relocations at load and marks the `PT_GNU_RELRO`-frozen slots constant) | **A PIE called its own functions through a GOT slot that read back as zero, so kuna printed `(*dat_e0dc8)(...)` for a callee it had already named.** kuna's ELF loader maps the `PT_LOAD` bytes the *linker* wrote, which is not the image a process runs: every slot filled by an `R_*_RELATIVE` / `GLOB_DAT` / `JUMP_SLOT` relocation is left at 0 for the run-time loader to complete. In a PIE -- the default link mode of every current toolchain -- that is the whole `.got` plus every relocated function pointer in `.data.rel.ro`, so an indirect call through a pointer the linker itself resolved reads a null target and can never deindirect. Zero is not an ambiguity to be judged; it is a byte the run-time image never holds, which is why this ships on rather than as an opt-in. `kuna-analysis/src/loader/kuna_dynrelocs.rs (resolve)` walks `.rela.dyn`/`.rel.dyn`/`.rela.plt` and writes what the dynamic loader would: `RELATIVE` takes the load bias plus the addend (kuna maps a linked image at its declared vaddrs, so the bias is 0), and `GLOB_DAT`/`JUMP_SLOT` take the symbol address **only when that symbol is defined in this same image** -- an undefined one is an import whose value lives in another object, so it is skipped and the existing PLT/import naming path (DIV-8/DIV-9) is byte-identical, lazy-binding stub contents included. x86-64, AArch64, i386 and ARM by relocation triple; `RELA` and `REL` (in-place addend) tables both; `COPY`/`IRELATIVE`/`TLS*` are not statically knowable and are skipped. Applying the relocation is only half the fix: `.got` is `SHF_WRITE`, so nothing downstream trusts it, and the constant fold of read-only storage is gated program-wide by `option readonly` (`readonlypropagate`, default off -- turning it on would fold every `.rodata` read in the program, a far larger change). The narrow warrant is `PT_GNU_RELRO`, the linker's own statement that the range is `mprotect`ed read-only before `main`: the written slots RELRO covers are reported both through `getReadonly` (painting `Varnode::readonly`) and separately as `Architecture::dynreloc_const`, and `ActionVarnodeProps` folds a read-only varnode inside one of those ranges with global propagation still OFF. Blast radius is therefore exactly the relocated RELRO slots. Slots outside RELRO are still filled in (that IS the value at process start) but never declared constant. Loader-tier, so the gate is the `KUNA_DYNRELOCS` env bridge (the image is snapshotted inside `load file`, upstream of every per-function `option`); `--option dynrelocs off` restores the pre-fix bytes exactly. Distinct from `relocobjects`/`relocrebase` (DIV-8/DIV-79), which own the pre-link `ET_REL` path and are untouched. | **0/675.** The XML `<binaryimage>` datatest path never reaches `bootstrap_from_object`, so the corpus is structurally untouched; the fold gate is additionally inert wherever `dynreloc_const` is empty. Effect, static-PIE Rust binary (4.9 MB, 4,194 functions): unnamed indirect call sites **2,853 -> 560 (-80.4%)**, functions carrying at least one **1,172 -> 276**, 1,077 functions changed, function inventory identical. The showcase function (`FakeCrypt::fileops::encrypt_file`) loses both of its `(*dat_...)()` calls (`read_to_end`, `_eprint`) and one `dat_...` argument becomes a named global, with no other line changed. Second-order: `securitycheck` (DIV-82) was **measured dead on this binary** -- all seven rustc panic helpers are reached only through GOT slots, so it stripped 0 functions with `dynrelocs` off and 85 with it on. C collateral, option off vs on over whole-binary `decompile-all`: `grep` 0/449 functions differ, `sort` 0/490, `betaflight_STM32F405.elf` (ARM firmware, no dynamic relocs) 0/5,797; `faillog` 2/69 and `libselinux.so.1` 31/621 -- every one an improvement (`*dat_5ff8` -> `stderr`, `*dat_2bf40` -> `selinux_mnt`), never a regression, and no binary's function inventory moves. Speed: the sanctioned per-function gate on the showcase function is **-29.7%** (median of 5, budget +5%) -- a resolved call is cheaper to decompile than an unresolved `CALLIND`. Interleaved min-of-5 whole-binary `decompile-all`: `grep` +0.16%, `libselinux.so.1` -0.81%, `faillog` -0.33%; load+discovery only (`kuna functions`) on the 4.9 MB PIE -0.50%, i.e. the relocation walk itself is free. The one real cost is whole-binary `decompile-all` on that PIE, **+25.7%** (interleaved min-of-2, 137.6 s -> 172.9 s): the price of decompiling 2,293 newly-resolved direct calls, not of the walk. That cost has a **measured tail**: three of the 4,194 functions cross `--mode fast`'s 10 s per-function budget as a result and become `error` records where they previously decompiled -- `miniz_oxide::inflate::core::decompress` 6.7 s -> 18.6 s, `std::backtrace_rs::symbolize::gimli::Context::new` 12.6 s -> 20.6 s, `std::path::{impl#20}::next` 8.0 s -> 12.7 s (isolated single-function wall clocks, load included; deterministic, not contention). The same resolved-call work that names 2,293 sites is what pushes them over, so the trade is 3 functions lost against 2,293 call sites named; `--option dynrelocs off` or a larger per-function budget recovers them. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK**, `make test-stages` **PARITY OK**, `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` OK (lenient + strict), `kuna catalog --check` OK. Counters re-derived from the built artifact: live catalog 114, `catalog_bytecompat.rs` 114, `kuna_phases/tests.rs` 114 / tiers (24, 50, 40) / `},\n` 113 / live readers 34 (`dynrelocs` joins the no-codegen-live-reader gates, read via `kuna_live_value`), `phase_catalog.json` 114, `docs/options.md` 114; `tests/stages/kuna-catalog.xml` unmoved (`source_decompiler` `kuna` and `change_kind` `correctness-fix` join no exact bucket). |
| DIV-85 | `retinputhalf` (new kuna option, default-ON; keep a returned register half whose value is an input parameter the function moved there) | **Two functions differing only in whether the second returned half is `x` or `x*3+7` recovered different arities, and one lost a parameter outright.** `P wide(unsigned long s, unsigned long x){ p.a=s+1; p.b=x; return p; }` compiles to `mov %rsi,%rdx; lea 0x1(%rdi),%rax; ret` and came out `unsigned long wide(long a0)` -- the `RDX` half dropped, and then `x`, which had no reader left, gone from the signature; the arithmetic twin `w2` came out correctly as `undefined16 w2(long a0,long a1)`. The cause is `p4_calls/kuna_returnuncomputed.rs`, the (ida) repair that kills the callee-saved-restore phantom (`undefined16 main(...)` with a `v[8] = <uninitialized stack slot>` write) by rejecting a return half whose move-only chain ends at an **unwritten** Varnode. A formal input parameter is unwritten by definition, so the passthrough shape was indistinguishable from leftover. `p4_calls/kuna_retinputhalf.rs (is_input_parameter)` supplies the missing exception on storage evidence the prototype model already holds, and it takes two tests, both necessary. **Is it parameter storage?** `FuncProto::possibleInputParam` against the resolved model -- the same question input recovery itself asks -- so argument registers and the stack above the return address qualify while a *local frame slot*, what a callee-saved restore reads, does not; a clobber is an INDIRECT creation and is rejected earlier in the walk. **Did the function put it there?** The terminal's address is compared with the storage of the return half the walk started from (`computes_from`'s `placed_at`): a *different* address means the function executed an instruction to move the argument into the return register, a *same* address means the register was never touched and the caller's value is passing through -- leftover. The placement test is what makes this safe rather than merely narrower: without it the untouched `RDX` on libselinux `sub_1a330`'s cached early return beat the real `RAX` half and the function grew three invented parameters, and the GH-6990 SPARC void `main` got its `o0:o1` join back (SPARC passes arguments in the same registers it returns them in). A whole-pair variant that also rescued a pair whose halves are *all* untouched arguments -- the ARM `double f(double x){ return x; }` shape -- was implemented, measured, and **rejected** for exactly that reason: it recovered three betaflight soft-float helpers and re-broke `tests/stages/gh6990-returnpair.xml`, and nothing local to the pair separates the two. Runs inside `ActionOutputPrototype`, which precedes `ActionInputPrototype`, so the question goes to the model rather than to a not-yet-fixated parameter list. | **0/675**, byte-identical on the datatest corpus, which does exercise the `p4_calls` path. C collateral, `decompile-all` option off vs on: `grep` 0/449 functions differ, `faillog` 0/69, `libselinux.so.1` 0/502, `betaflight_STM32F405.elf` 1/5,797, `sort` 1/490 -- `sort`'s is `sub_148c0` (`mov %rdi,%rax; mov %rsi,%rdx; ret`, the identity struct-pair passthrough), which goes from `unsigned long sub_148c0(unsigned long a0)` to `undefined16 sub_148c0(unsigned long a0,unsigned long a1)`, and betaflight's is a soft-float helper that stops declaring a 4-byte return while one path returns `CONCAT44`. No regressions: every changed function recovers a return half, a parameter, or both. Largest effect, static-PIE Rust binary (4.9 MB, 1,577 named functions): **31 functions changed, all 31 recovering a two-word `[u8; 16]` return and 13 of them also recovering a parameter** -- `core::str::chars`, `std::path::str::as_ref`, `core::ops::range::Range::index`, `rayon::vec::DrainProducer::into_iter` and the rest of the fat-pointer/slice-returning family, which return `(ptr, len)` in `RAX:RDX` and were coming back as a bare `u64`. Cost, honestly measured: 4 new never-assigned locals across 3 of those 31 functions (a MULTIEQUAL arm the kept pair now exposes). **The phantom-discriminant-guard symptom this was expected to move does not move**: on the same binary, guards reading a never-assigned local are 167/911 before and after (33/198 under a narrower discriminant-only definition), and 0 -> 0 within the 31 changed functions -- that symptom has another cause. Speed, interleaved min-of-N whole-binary `decompile-all`: `sort` +0.05%, `grep` +0.01%, `libselinux.so.1` -0.29%, `betaflight_STM32F405.elf` -0.51% -- a wash, as expected for a predicate on a walk that already ran. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK**, `make test-stages` **506/506 PARITY OK**, `make rust-test` green (4,717 passed / 0 failed), `make check-spec` OK (lenient + strict), `kuna catalog --check` OK. | DIV-86 | DWARF aggregate-LAYOUT import (`dwarfstructs`, new kuna option, default-ON) | **kuna read a `-g` binary's structs as names only, and a name with no width is not a conservative answer -- the ABI classifier acts on it.** `analyzers/dwarf/mod.rs::intern_aggregate` resolved every `DW_TAG_structure_type`/`union_type`/`class_type` to `get_type_struct(name)`: a named, EMPTY, ZERO-SIZE shell. It never read `DW_AT_byte_size` and never walked a single `DW_TAG_member`; the module header said so ("Type recovery is name/signature-level") and the call site's comment called it sufficient ("A named opaque struct is enough for a pointer-to-struct to render"). It is sufficient for exactly that one case. Everywhere the WIDTH matters the zero propagates: x86-64 parameter-storage assignment reads the size, so `int take_struct(P8 p,int k)` came out `int take_struct(unsigned long,int)` and shifted the register apart by hand, and `P8 ret_struct(unsigned x)` -- an 8-byte REGISTER return on this ABI -- was classified as a hidden-return-buffer call and came out `P8 *ret_struct(P8 *rethidden,unsigned int x)` with the body doing arithmetic on the PHANTOM parameter (`(int)rethidden * 7`), the real parameter shifted one slot right. Field accesses were casts and offsets throughout (`*(int *)((long)n + 4)` for `n->inner.a`). The arm (`analyzers/dwarf/kuna_dwarfstructs.rs`, the reduction of Ghidra's `DWARFDataTypeImporter.makeDataTypeForStruct`/`makeDataTypeForUnion`) reads `DW_AT_byte_size`, walks the `DW_TAG_member` children, places each at its `DW_AT_data_member_location` VERBATIM, and recurses each member's `DW_AT_type` through the same DIE switch; bitfields come off `DW_AT_bit_size` with either the DWARF 4/5 `DW_AT_data_bit_offset` or the DWARF 2/3 `byte_size`+`bit_offset` spelling. Offsets go in through a new raw field-setting seam (`TypeFactory::set_fields_struct_raw`/`set_fields_union_raw`, the structural analog of `get_type_enum_sized`) rather than `assign_raw_fields_struct`, whose C packing rules skip a field whose offset is already set and would therefore also size the structure at 0. **Two hazards ride along with populating fields, and both were measured, not argued.** (1) NAME COLLISION: the factory interns by `(name, hash(name))` and `find_add` refuses a second, different definition of a name it holds -- invisible while every aggregate was a sizeless shell, because two shells compare equal. `rustc -g` names every enum payload struct BARE, and a five-function Rust witness carries four distinct `Some` DIEs of sizes **16, 24, 16, 12** (`core::option::Option<usize>`, `Option<(NonNull<u8>, Layout)>`, `Option<&[Placeholder]>`, `Option<rw::Pair>`). Aggregates are therefore interned parent-QUALIFIED (reusing `kuna_cppproto`'s ancestry walk) and, when that name is still held by a different-sized aggregate, under a size-suffixed variant. This is load-bearing and was verified by ablation: with the disambiguation clamped off, the fixture's second `struct Same` degrades its whole parameter to `int read_same_big(void *s)`; with it, `Same` (4 bytes) and `Same_16` both keep their members. (2) SELF-REFERENCE: `struct node { struct node *next; }` reaches its own DIE mid-population, so the shell is interned BEFORE the members are walked and a new `TypeWalk::begin_aggregate` claim refuses the re-entrant populate. LOSS (documented, and the same one the wire `decode_composite` path lives with): kuna's interned types are immutable, so completing one mints a new `Rc` and the pointer captured by the inner frame still refers to the pre-completion shell -- `n->next` renders and names its type, `n->next->val` does not chain. LOSS: `DW_TAG_variant_part`/`DW_TAG_variant`/`DW_AT_discr` are deliberately NOT read (Rust tagged enums are a separate change), so a Rust enum recovers its width and no fields. **Bitfields**, and a third-party defect found by building them.** Each bitfield member goes into the SMALLEST byte span that covers it, which is the geometry the compiler's own access agrees with and the one the printer's `checkBitFieldMember` reads to choose `.` versus `->` (a whole-storage-unit container reports `byte_offset == 0` for a member the compiler reaches at +3, and the access then prints `p.b` on a POINTER -- invalid C; measured, then fixed by the minimal span). A member occupying whole aligned bytes of a natural width is not a bitfield at all -- on little-endian `unsigned c:16` at bit 16 of a 4-byte unit IS the two bytes at +2 -- and is emitted as a plain field of that width. That last rule is also CONTAINMENT for a pre-existing engine defect this change is the first thing to reach: `BitFieldPullTransform` (`p5_types/bitfield/pull.rs`) diverges when several pulls share one extraction chain, because the second record's `op_destroy_recursive` walks a `mod_op` whose operands the first record's walk already unset, and `Funcdata::op_destroy_recursive` asserts on the cleared slot ("null input (C++ UB)"). `struct { int head; unsigned f1:4, f2:12, f3:16; }` with all three read reproduces it on a two-line C file. It is NOT specific to this pass -- the Ghidra-wire `<bitfield>` decode builds the same shapes -- and tolerating the cleared slot only converts the assertion into a non-terminating rule pool (tried, measured, reverted), so the fix belongs in that transform and is left to it; the byte-exact rule keeps the shapes this pass produces inside what the transform handles. A nine-shape bitfield stress corpus (1-bit runs, 31+1, signed, `unsigned char` units, a 33+31 `long` pair, four byte-aligned bytes, bitfields either side of a nested struct) decompiles clean. | **675/675 PARITY OK with no re-pin and no per-test opt-out**, stages 501/501 PARITY OK, `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` OK (lenient + strict), `kuna catalog --check` OK -- structural, because the XML `<bytechunk>` datatest bootstrap never reaches the analyzer tier, so the gates CANNOT see this change and the evidence below is the whole of it. **Breadth** (whole-binary `decompile-all`, `dwarfstructs` off vs on, 27 targets / 3764 functions: decbench-dataset zlib/bzip2/gzip/coreutils-fmt/diffutils/libexpat/openssh O0 + zlib/bzip2 O2, every repo `-g` fixture including the four C++ ones, a Rust `-g` binary, and purpose-built C witnesses): **891 of 3764 functions change (23.7%)**, and every counted pattern moves the right way -- cast-and-offset dereferences `*(T *)((long)x + N)` **10147 -> 1662 (-84%)**, `->field` accesses **6 -> 11319**, `void *` **1456 -> 1189 (-18%)**, and **`decompilation failed` 0 -> 0** (the safety metric for the bitfield defect above: no function crashes or hangs that did not before). Line counts fall on the bigger targets (ssh-keygen 38009 -> 37722, bzip2-O0 6860 -> 6730, gzip 6593 -> 6544) because a named field replaces a cast expression. Representative: zlib `gz_look` goes from `*(unsigned int *)((long)state + 0x28) = *(unsigned int *)((long)state + 0x2c);` to `state->size = state->want;` and from `inflateInit2_((z_stream_s *)((long)state + 0x80),...)` to `inflateInit2_(&state->strm,...)`; the C++ fixture's `Account::deposit` from `*(int *)((long)this + 4) = *(int *)((long)this + 4) + amount;` to `this->balance = this->balance + amount;`, its `main` from `char v3 [24]; Shape::Shape((Shape *)v3,argc)` to `Shape v3; Shape::Shape(&v3,argc)`, and a virtual dispatch from `(*(void *)**(unsigned long **)s)(s,scale)` to `(**s->_vptr.Shape)(s,scale)`; an eight-way 1-bit bitfield struct from a hand-rolled `(v1 & 1) + (v1 >> 1 & 1) + ...` to `p->a + p->b + ... + p->h`. **The wrongness axis**, reported as measured rather than as zero: `undefined<n>` occurrences **14 -> 22 (+8)** and the count of functions carrying a DUPLICATED local declaration (a pre-existing printer pathology on 1320 of the 3764 functions) **1320 -> 1326 (+6 net)**. Both come from the same place -- a local that is now a real aggregate with a UNION member gets per-use union resolution, and the declaration emitter prints one declaration per resolved width. That is the existing `dedupvardecls`/union-resolution seam, not a new one, and it is 6 functions against 891 improved. **Controls**: the STRIPPED copy of zlib `minigzip` is **0 changed lines** (verified, not assumed -- so the decbench scored corpus, which is stripped, is a structural zero here exactly as it was for DIV-63), and the four `-g` fixtures with no aggregates (`dwarf_globals`, `stacklocal`, `dwarf_enums`, `dwarf_stripped`) and three of the four C++ fixtures are 0 changed lines. **Speed** (the layout walk runs once per binary inside `load file`, so LOAD is the primary metric; interleaved off/on, MINIMUM of N pairs on a box shared with concurrent agents -- an early n=15 read of +2.87% on gzip and +3.58% on bzip2 was contention, and both vanish at higher N): `kuna functions` gzip-O0 311 -> 310 ms (-0.32%, n=25), zlib-minigzip-O0 424 -> 417 ms (-1.65%, n=15), ssh-keygen-O0 276 -> 278 ms (+0.72%, n=7); `kuna decompile` fmt-O0 `main` 430 -> 433 ms (+0.70%, n=15); `decompile-all` bzip2-O0 23453 -> 23466 ms (+0.06%, n=7), zlib-minigzip-O0 4697 -> 4732 ms (**+0.75%**, n=5), gzip-O0 2665 -> 2657 ms (-0.30%, n=5) and the fixture 203 -> 194 ms (-4.43%, n=10). **Worst delta +0.75%, budget 5%** -- the walk adds one member pass per aggregate DIE inside a load that already parses every DIE, and nothing in the decompile loop is touched. Covered by `decompiler/crates/kuna-console/tests/verify_dwarfstructs.rs` (9 two-pass e2e assertions over a new `-g -O1` two-translation-unit fixture `dwarfstructs_x86_64`: the by-value struct parameter, the phantom small-struct sret, the REAL 24-byte sret surviving, the union, a union keeping EVERY member and not just its first, the nested struct, the bitfield, the self-referential node terminating, and two same-named different-sized aggregates both surviving) plus the `dwarfstructs` env-gate and `TypeWalk` claim unit tests; no stage XML, because the datatest path cannot reach the analysis tier |
| DIV-87 | DWARF variant-part (discriminated-union) import (`dwarfvariants`, new kuna option, default-ON) | **A Rust tagged enum carries NO `DW_TAG_member` of its own, so DIV-86's member walk gave it a width and zero fields -- and a field-less aggregate is still an aggregate the ABI classifier acts on.** Its whole layout hangs off a `DW_TAG_variant_part`, whose `DW_AT_discr` points at the artificial member that IS the discriminant and whose `DW_TAG_variant` children each carry a `DW_AT_discr_value` plus one `DW_TAG_member` naming the variant and referring to its payload struct. `dwarfstructs` read none of it, so an 8-byte `fn(u32) -> Result<u32,u32>` came out `Result<u32,u32> *ret_result(Result<u32,u32> *rethidden,uint4 x)` -- a phantom hidden-return parameter with the real one shifted a slot right and the body reading `x` out of the phantom -- and a 16-byte three-variant enum, which IS a real sret, kept its parameters but wrote its variants as `*(uint4 *)&rethidden->field_0x4`. The arm (`analyzers/dwarf/kuna_dwarfvariants.rs`) reads the variant part into a factory-free `RawVariantPart` first, then interns a struct of the discriminant plus a UNION of one payload struct per variant. A union's members all sit at offset 0, which IS a variant overlay, so this reuses the existing type model rather than adding a `type_metatype` (that enum is matched at 1634 sites, mostly non-exhaustively, so a new variant would compile clean and behave wrong). Facet fields are RE-BASED to the overlay: DWARF gives a variant's payload struct the width of the whole enum with its members at absolute offsets (`Result<u32,u32>::Ok` is 8 bytes with `__0` at 4), which describes an overlay at 0 and cannot be placed beside a `tag` field there. **Why DWARF and not codegen:** the two questions a decompiler cannot answer from shape are *is this a discriminated union* and *which variant is which* -- two return paths storing different constants at offset 0 is equally a `#[repr(C)] struct {kind,val}`, a `(u64,u64)` tuple, a bitmask pair, or a `&'static str` fat pointer whose "discriminant" would be a `.rodata` address. PR #333 tried the inference and was withdrawn for exactly those false positives. **Three shapes get specific treatment, each measured.** (1) NICHE: a `DW_TAG_variant` with no `DW_AT_discr_value` is the DEFAULT variant, and when the discriminant's bytes overlap the payload (`Option<&T>` reads the tag out of the reference) there is no byte range that is only the tag, so the recovered type is the overlay ALONE -- the union under the enum's own name, no enclosing struct -- because a `tag` field would have to sit at an offset a variant already owns. (2) FIELDLESS variants (`None`, `Nil`) overlay nothing and get no union member: an empty struct of the overlay's width ties with the real facet in `ScoreUnionFields` and wins on declaration order; ablating the exclusion makes a std-linked `rustc -g` witness write an `Option<i64>` payload as `v13.payload.None = ...` and read drop glue as `(*a0).dropfn.drop.None` (2 functions of 612, measured). The name and discriminant value still reach the side table. (3) NAME COLLISIONS: rustc names payload structs BARE, and one std-linked witness with 65 variant parts carries 30 DIEs named `Some` across 5 distinct byte sizes (0/4/8/16/24), 30 named `None`, 18 each `Ok`/`Err` across 7 -- so every minted name is parent-QUALIFIED and goes through DIV-86's size-suffix collision policy. **Refusals are split by whether the shell exists yet**, which is the defect DIV-86 shipped: refusing AFTER interning would leave a zero-size incomplete type under the enum's own name that degrades to `void` downstream, so those refusals SEAL the shell at `DW_AT_byte_size` with no fields -- byte for byte the `dwarfstructs` answer -- rather than returning `None`. **What may be NAMED is a separate question from what is read, and the answer is mostly "nothing".** A union member selects itself by OFFSET and the discriminant is never consulted. For a tagged enum that is not a corner case but the definition: every payload variant begins immediately after the tag, so `Ok` and `Err` are at the same offset, always. Measured on a `Result<u64,u64>` witness, the label was not merely uncertain but consistently FALSE -- `Ok` printed on both arms of the producing `if`/`else` and on the consumer's `Err(e) => e + 100`, `Err` nowhere in the binary. So a name is installed only where it is forced, per byte range: a facet keeps its `DW_TAG_variant` name only when no other variant claims a byte it claims (both the union member AND the facet's interned type name, because a cast prints the type name), and a field inside a facet keeps its DWARF name only when every other variant either claims none of its bytes or names exactly that range the same way. `Option<T>` keeps `Some`; `enum Three { A(u32)@4, B(u64)@8, C }` keeps `A` and `B`; `Result<T,E>` keeps neither and is spelled `field_0x<offset>`, which is byte for byte the rendering `dwarfvariants off` gives. LOSS: for every enum with two or more payload-carrying variants -- most tagged enums, `Result` included -- no variant name reaches the emitted C, and the rule is conservative at facet granularity, so labels an access WIDTH could have singled out go too (`enum Multi { P{a,b}, Q(u64) }` rendered `Q.__0` correctly before and renders `field_0x8.field_0x8` now; the label was not provable, since a 4-byte access at the same offset rendered `P.b`). What is lost is the LABEL, never the layout: offsets, widths, member types, the enum's own size and the absent phantom `rethidden` are identical either way, and every variant's source name and `DW_AT_discr_value` stay on the side table, which is what a `match` renderer will read. LOSS: needs FULL debug info -- where DWARF carries no type DIEs the arm is INERT, not degraded, and no fallback is attempted because the only available fallback is the shape inference above. | **675/675 PARITY OK with no re-pin**, stages **532/532** PARITY OK (the 6 new `DWARFVARIANTS #11`..`#16` rows; nothing removed from `passing`), `make rust-test` **MAKE_EXIT=0** -- 321 suites, **4821 passed, 0 failed**, 38 ignored, 0 failure blocks -- `make check-spec` OK (lenient + strict), `kuna catalog --check` OK. All four are STRUCTURAL here, and stated as such: EVERY fixture in `tests/datatests/` and `tests/stages/` is a `<bytechunk>` with no symbol table, no DWARF and no relocations, so a DWARF-gated change is unreachable from the parity gates and "0/675 changed" is true but nearly VACUOUS. The real-binary A/B below is the evidence. **Witness** (two committed `#![no_std]` fixtures, `rustc 1.90 -C debuginfo=2 -C opt-level=1`, sources vendored beside them). `dwarfvariants_x86_64` (10 variant parts, the SHAPES): `ret_result`/`ret_option`/`ret_niche` all lose the phantom `rethidden` and recover the source signature (`core::result::Result<u32, u32> ret_result(uint4 x)`); `ret_three` KEEPS its `rethidden` -- verified against the disassembly (`mov %rdi,%rax`, writes through `%rax`), that sret is REAL -- but its body goes from `*(uint4 *)&rethidden->field_0x4 = x` to `(rethidden->payload).A.__0 = x`; **7 of 9 functions change**, `ret_pair` (a C-shaped struct) and `ret_plain` (a fieldless enum, emitted as `DW_TAG_enumeration_type`) unchanged as intended. `dwarfvariants_overlay_x86_64` (6 variant parts, the NAMING rule): **5 of 5**, `put_res` writes `(dst->payload).field_0x8.__0` and names neither variant, `put_opt` writes `(dst->payload).Some.__0` and names the one it may, `r16` still returns `core::result::Result<u64, u64>` by value with no `rethidden`. **The naming rule, measured** on a std-linked `rustc -C debuginfo=2` witness (152 variant parts, 639 functions -- `enum Tree { Leaf(i64), Node(Box<Tree>,Box<Tree>) }`, `enum Expr { Lit, Bin, Neg }`, `HashMap`, `BTreeMap`, `Box<dyn Error>`), A/B'd against this branch WITHOUT the rule: **9 of 639 functions change**, and the 15 variant labels those functions carried -- `Leaf` x2, `Node` x6, `Lit` x1, `Bin` x6 -- go to **0**. Every one of them was unsound and two were demonstrably WRONG: `depth` rendered the `Node` right child as `t = (*t).Leaf.__0`, and `eval` rendered the `Neg` payload as `(*e).payload.Bin.__0` while spelling offset 8 `Bin.__0` and `Lit.__0` in the same function. The 169 SOUND labels on the same binary are untouched -- `Some` x153 (`Option` has one payload-carrying variant), `Err` x14 (`Result<(), Box<dyn Error>>`, whose `Ok` is a ZST and therefore fieldless), `Ok` x2 (`Result<u64, u32>`, where rustc packs `Err(u32)` at offset 4 and `Ok(u64)` at offset 8, so the two really are disjoint). **C collateral**: whole-binary `decompile-all` off vs on over `tests/bug-repro/{grep,sort,faillog,libselinux.so.1}`, `fmt_aarch64`, `arm_thumb_linked_le32`, `/usr/bin/ncat`, `/usr/bin/htop` and a purpose-built `gcc -g -O1` C witness carrying structs, a union, a hand-rolled `{enum kind; union value;}` tagged union, a nested struct and bitfields: **0 changed / 2697 functions**, all nine targets. **Speed: NOT RESOLVABLE on this host today, and no number is claimed.** The box was running a decbench campaign (load average 8-15 on 80 cores). Across five interleaved minimum-of-N passes the NULL CONTROL -- `kuna functions` on `grep`, a C binary with zero variant parts whose whole-binary output is byte-identical either way -- read -1.38%, +0.79%, +43.50%, +4.08% and +30.80%, so the noise floor exceeds the 5% budget by itself; the DWARF-heavy witness read +0.81%, +1.83%, -1.26%, +4.05% and +0.53% over the same passes, i.e. inside that band. What can be said structurally: the naming rule adds no DIE traversal, no I/O and no new pass -- it is two scans over the variants of ONE enum inside the walk that already read them, at most V^2*F^2 integer comparisons with V<=5 variants and F<=2 fields in every witness scanned. **Tests**: 40 unit tests in `analyzers/dwarf/kuna_dwarfvariants/tests.rs` (every entry point direct, and every guard exercised as a REFUSAL as well as a keep -- the naming rule's refusals are `Result`, a `Tree`-style niche overlay, a suppressed DEFAULT variant, a multi-field variant and a five-variant enum; its keeps are `Option`, disjoint payloads and an agreeing field name; plus the post-intern seal the offset-derived facet name introduces), 15 cross-crate e2e tests in `kuna-console/tests/verify_dwarfvariants.rs`, and 16 stage assertions in `tests/stages/kuna-dwarfvariants.xml` |

| DIV-76 | ghidra-mode `setOptions`: unknown option elements are skipped per-element with a "Warning:" line instead of failing the command | **One stale option bricks the whole decompiler view.** Upstream `SetOptions::rawAction` decodes the `<optionslist>` through `OptionDatabase::decode`, which throws on the first element it does not recognize; the command answers `f` and Java's `DecompInterface.initializeProcess` turns that into `IOException("Did not accept decompiler options")`, failing `openProgram` for the entire program (DecompInterface.java:295-303). The option vocabulary drifts across Ghidra releases (12.2 added `baddatacount`, element 290, moving `ELEM_UNKNOWN` to 291), so a kuna core pinned to one vintage paired with a newer Java would refuse every program over an option it merely does not know. kuna's ghidra-mode `setOptions` (`kuna-ghidra/src/process.rs`, Phase 3) instead decodes through `OptionDatabase::decode_lenient` (`p0_knowledge/options.rs`): every known option applies, every unknown/unparseable element is consumed whole (`close_element_skipping`) and reported on the 16/17 warnings frame with a message beginning "Warning" — which `DecompInterface.isErrorMessage` deliberately treats as non-fatal — and the command always answers `t`. Output-invariant for every known option; the divergence only changes what happens to elements upstream would die on. Documented in advance in `docs/ghidra-integration.md` (version policy); this row records the behavior actually shipping | ghidra-mode only (the standalone surfaces never speak the wire protocol); `make test` 675/675 PARITY OK, `make test-stages` PARITY OK. Covered by `options/tests.rs::test_decode_lenient_skips_unknown_and_applies_known` (known options straddling an unknown element all apply, exactly one "Warning" line) and the `protocol_e2e` byte-exact session (a fully-known list applies silently) |
| DIV-77 | ghidra-mode defaults: the CLI `aggressive` engine-tier preset is applied at registerProgram, and address-derived fallback names print the Ghidra GUI convention (`FUN_`/`DAT_`/`LAB_` + `%08x`) | **The GUI is a first-class surface and had no defaults story.** Every other kuna surface resolves `--mode auto`, which selects `aggressive` for binaries under 500 KiB — i.e. the preset (`ctypes`, `stackalias`, `switchmodbound`, `switchguardbound`, `unrolledguard`, `regionedgeorder`, `returndup`, `orchain`, `iteexpr`, `sparcstructret`) *is* kuna's real-binary default rendering. Ghidra-mode has no `--mode` surface and no file to size, so registerProgram applies the preset's engine-tier members directly (`kuna-ghidra/src/process.rs::build_architecture`, before Java's `setOptions` replay so GUI options still win); the preset's analysis/loader-tier members (`listing`, `fid`, `aif`, …) run over a real file in `kuna-analysis` and are structurally N/A — their *products* are what the Phase-3 providers pull over the wire instead. Naming: kuna's angr-style LOCAL naming stays ON (it is the only ported local-naming pass; without it unnamed register-storage highs render as raw `EAX`/`RBX` tokens), but address-derived FALLBACK names — an unresolved callee, an unnamed global, a goto label — print `FUN_%08x`/`DAT_%08x`/`LAB_%08x` instead of `sub_`/`dat_`/`label_`, because Java-side heuristics key on those shapes (`ArchitectureGhidra::isDynamicSymbolName`, ghidra_arch.cc:928-947, and `GlobalSymbolMap`). Mechanism: a `name_style_ghidra` Architecture/ArchContext flag consulted through `kuna_name_style()` (`Ghidra` wins over `Angr` wins over `Func`), threaded through `fspec_printed_name`/`register_in_fspec_space`, `Architecture::name_function`, and the printer's unnamed-location/label tails; never set on the standalone path. The preset layer is re-applied after `Architecture::reset_wire_defaults` on EVERY setOptions (the upstream reset-then-apply contract — Java delta-encodes options, so deltas must land on this baseline each time). ghidra-mode also stamps a `Kuna v<MAJOR.MINOR>` plate comment at the top of every decompiled function (user-requested visibility that kuna is the active core; cache-only, release `KUNA_VERSION` bake, absent from every standalone surface) | ghidra-mode only; `make test` 675/675 PARITY OK, `make test-stages` PARITY OK (the flag is dead on the standalone path). Measured on the ghidra-sim faillog fixture together with the Phase-3 providers: the diff-vs-CLI ratio (style-normalized) 0.643/0.898/0.811 → 0.175/0.220/0.264 across the three pinned targets |
| DIV-80 | The output language follows the binary: `--language auto` is the default on `kuna decompile` / `decompile-all` and in the browser, and a Rust binary renders as Rust (driver tier; no new option -- it resolves to the already-registered upstream `option setlanguage`) | **A Rust binary rendered as C is worse in a way the reader has to undo by hand, and kuna already knew which it was.** `kuna-analysis/src/analyzers/sourcelang/detect_compiler` is the port of Ghidra's `SourceLanguageAnalyzer` and has reported `Compiler::Rustc` since the analysis tier landed -- from a `.comment` `rustc version` record, a `.rodata` signature, or a Rust-mangled symbol -- but nothing consumed it for rendering, so every user of a Rust binary had to know a flag existed. The policy now resolves in the driver (`kuna-cli` for the CLI, `kuna_wasm::resolve_language` for the browser, one implementation each side of the wasm boundary but the same detector), and lowers to `option setlanguage rust-language`. Detection is high-precision rather than heuristic, an unparseable file leaves the C default in place, and `--language c` always wins -- so the policy can only ever ADD a language, never take one away. Two surfaces are excluded BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by configuration: `decompile-project` never auto-selects, because its `.c`/`.h`/`.asm` export is C-shaped end to end and auto-selecting would turn a working export into an error on every Rust binary; and ghidra-mode never sees the policy at all (its Clang token-markup document is pinned to C). `decompile-all --json` gained a top-level `"language"` key, because a consumer cannot otherwise tell which language `code` is in once the choice is made for it | **Blast radius on the committed corpus is exactly zero, and structurally so.** The datatest and stage corpora are `<bytechunk>` images with no ELF for `detect_compiler` to read; every vendored fixture in `tests/bug-repro/` and `tests/fixtures/` detects `c-language` (verified: faillog, grep, sort, libselinux.so.1, prog, betaflight_STM32F405.elf), as does the entire decbench corpus, which is gcc-built C. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK**, `make test-stages` **PARITY OK**, `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` green. Browser side: `parity.mjs` 20/20 native-vs-wasm (including a `--language rust` case), `worker.mjs`, `glue.mjs`, `auto-mode.mjs`, `zip.mjs`, `fnfilter.mjs` all green. Witness: a `rustc -O` binary decompiles to `unsafe fn hello__main()` with `let mut v: u32` declarations under the default and to `void hello::main(void)` under `--language c` |
| DIV-82 | `securitycheck` (new kuna option, default-ON; the SEFCOM Oxidizer `SecurityCheckRemover` port) | **Every checked slice index, string slice and non-constant `/`/`%` in a Rust binary rendered as a branch to a `panic_bounds_check()` call, roughly doubling the CFG of ordinary Rust code.** rustc lowers each check to a conditional branch to a small block that loads the panic `Location`, calls one of seven helpers declared `-> !` in libcore (`core::panicking::panic_bounds_check`, `core::str::slice_error_fail`, `core::panicking::panic_const::panic_const_{div,rem}_by_zero`, `core::slice::index::slice_{start_index_len,end_index_len,index_order}_fail`) and diverges. Ghidra has no notion of them, so kuna emitted the guard and the call verbatim in front of every array access. The port is two parts under one option. (1) At the flow seam (`ArchFlowEnv::query_call_no_return`, the same hook and the same shape as `noreturn_externmatch`) a call to one of the seven is reported no-return from its NAME, because kuna's address-keyed no-return discovery only proves divergence for the helper bodies it can walk and the ones it misses leave a *returning* panic block. (2) `p7_regions/kuna_securitycheck.rs (ActionRemoveSecurityCheck)`, scheduled in fullloop's `returnsplit` group immediately after `ActionStripStackGuard`, severs the CBRANCH edge to a **diverging** basic block whose **single** call is one of the seven, with `Funcdata::remove_branch` + `remove_unreachable_blocks` -- the same primitive pair, the same sub-stage and the same destructive/transform contract as DIV-14's `stackguard`, on a different trigger. The match is on the path PREFIX before the first `<` (so `slice_start_index_len_fail<usize>` matches) and accepts a whole-`::`-component suffix down to the bare leaf, which is the shape kuna's x86-64 ELF symbol path produces; `slice_error_fail_rt` is a different leaf and never matches. Default-ON without a compiler-detection channel from the loader (none exists, and this builds none) because the trigger is a set of names that exist nowhere but Rust: on a C binary the scan finds nothing and both halves are structurally inert | **0/675 datatest assertions and zero differing `code` fields over the C fixtures -- no baseline re-pin, no per-test opt-out.** Gates: `make test` 675/675 PARITY OK, `make test-stages` PARITY OK, `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` green (`--strict` green). Collateral: whole-binary `decompile-all --json` over `tests/bug-repro/{faillog,grep,sort,libselinux.so.1}` and `tests/fixtures/fid/prog` (2,688 functions) is **byte-identical** on/off. Witness (a `rustc -O` binary, 551 functions): 17 of the 24 emitted panic-helper calls removed, 99 emitted lines and 26 `if (` guards saved, 5 gotos removed, and one function that previously hit the 10 s per-function watchdog (`std::backtrace_rs::symbolize::gimli::Context::new`) now completes. 9 functions change; every one of them is a function that actually indexes a slice. Speed (interleaved, min-of-N `decompile-all`): C `grep` 14.240 s -> 14.190 s (-0.35%, N=5), C `sort` 7.960 s -> 7.990 s (+0.38%, N=5), the Rust witness 131.34 s -> 132.29 s (+0.72%, N=3 -- and the ON side decompiles one MORE function, the watchdog rescue above), per-function `app::transpose` / `app::ratios` +0.00% (N=5). All inside the +5% budget. The one visible second-order effect is on the recovered prototype: a length argument whose only reader was the removed compare becomes genuinely unused and parameter recovery may trim it -- the same trade DIV-14 makes with the canary slot, and the reason the option exists. **Known limit:** the trigger needs a recovered callee name, and in a default (PIE, dynamically linked) Rust binary the helper is reached through a `.got` slot whose `R_X86_64_RELATIVE` relocation kuna never applies, so the call is an unnamed `CALLIND` and the pass is a measured no-op there (0 changes on `private/FakeCrypt`, all 263 of whose panic call sites are GOT-indirect). Closing that is an ELF sibling of the `peimportcall` pass, not part of this row |
| DIV-83 | Rust-mangled symbols are demangled by `rustc_demangle`, not by the Itanium C++ demangler that also accepts them (bug fix; no option -- the routing is decided by a marker only a Rust symbol carries) | **Every Rust binary rendered its own call graph as escape soup, in BOTH output languages.** Rust's *legacy* mangling reuses the Itanium `_ZN…E` envelope and escapes the characters an Itanium identifier cannot hold: `$LT$` for `<`, `$GT$` for `>`, `$C$` for `,`, `$u20$` for a space, `..` for `::`. `cpp_demangle` therefore does not DECLINE such a symbol -- it sees a well-formed nested-name whose components happen to contain dollar signs -- so kuna's Itanium-first ordering never fell through to `rustc_demangle` and returned the escapes verbatim. `core::ptr::drop_in_place$LT$core..result..Result$LT$usize$C$std..io..error..Error$GT$$GT$` is what a reader got where `nm -C` gives `core::ptr::drop_in_place<core::result::Result<usize, std::io::error::Error>>`. The previous code had noticed half of this -- its comment says "a legacy Rust `_ZN..` symbol is *also* valid Itanium, so cpp_demangle handles it but keeps the trailing `::h<hex>` mangling hash as a junk scope; strip it" -- and worked around the hash while leaving the escapes. The fix routes on a marker rather than on which crate answers first: `sourcelang::is_rust_mangled` already identified both Rust schemes exactly (a `_R` prefix, or the legacy `17h<16 hex>E` hash tail) for the source-language detector, and a symbol carrying either now goes to `rustc_demangle` first. A second half was needed to make that safe: a Rust demangling is a *type expression*, not a path, and the existing `strip_bracket_groups` deletes `<…>` groups wholesale -- which turns `<aes::Aes256 as crypto_common::KeyInit>::new` into `::new`, an empty scope component the symbol table rejects with "Non-global scope has empty name" (it did: the fix crashed the loader on the first Rust binary until `normalize_rust_name` landed). A generic list carries no path and is dropped; a qualifier carries TWO and must keep one -- `<X as Y>` keeps the type, `<impl X as Y>` keeps the trait, `<impl Trait for Type>` keeps the type | **Blast radius on C is exactly zero and structurally so**: the new arm is gated on a marker no C or C++ symbol carries, the datatest and stage corpora are `<bytechunk>` images with no symbol table at all, and the whole-binary C render over `tests/bug-repro/{faillog,grep,sort,libselinux.so.1}` and `tests/fixtures/fid/prog` is byte-identical (compared field-by-field, not by diffing the document). **Witness** (`private/FakeCrypt`, a 4.9 MB rustc-built ELF, function at `0x55300`): `core::ptr::drop_in_place$LT$…$GT$` -> `core::ptr::drop_in_place`, `_$LT$aes..autodetect..Aes256$u20$as$u20$crypto_common..KeyInit$GT$::new` -> `aes::autodetect::Aes256::new`, `alloc::vec::Vec$LT$T$C$A$GT$::resize` -> `alloc::vec::Vec::resize`. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK**, `make test-stages` **PARITY OK**, `make rust-test` green (317 groups), `make check-spec` OK strict. New unit test `normalize_rust_name_resolves_generics_and_qualifiers` pins all three group kinds plus the empty-component case |
| DIV-88 | C++ **anonymous namespaces** demangle to the `anonymous_namespace` identifier instead of being deleted (loader-tier name reduction; no flag -- a strict fix), plus `symbolnamerepair` (new kuna option, default-ON) as the backstop for any other degenerate qualified symbol name | **kuna could not load an unstripped C++ binary that uses an anonymous namespace -- at all, by any command.** `Database::find_create_scope_from_symbol_name` nests one Scope per `::` component and `attach_scope` rejects an EMPTY component; because the symbol table is installed inside `load file`, that `LowlevelError` escapes `bootstrap_from_object` rather than costing one symbol, so `decompile-all`, `functions` and `decompile-project` all answered `could not build an architecture for <binary>: Non-global scope has empty name` and emitted nothing; `kuna decompile` also produced nothing but printed the generic `(unsupported/!recognized binary)` instead, because `kuna-cli/src/decompile.rs (check_errors)` maps the subprocess's `Could not create architecture` onto that string -- which is what made this undiagnosable from that one surface, and is a separate driver-tier defect this row does not fix. The empty component came from the name-only reduction: Itanium renders `_GLOBAL__N_…` as `(anonymous namespace)`, `strip_bracket_groups` deletes every balanced bracket group, and `leveldb::(anonymous namespace)::HandleDumpCommand` therefore reduced to `leveldb::::HandleDumpCommand`. An anonymous namespace is the ordinary way C++ gives a definition internal linkage, so this was not an exotic input -- it is most unstripped C++. The root cause was diagnosed during the #270 leveldb work and recorded there as out of scope (`docs/features/dallparity/analysis.md`); this closes it. **Shape of the fix, two independent parts.** (1) `analyzers/demangle/mod.rs (name_anonymous_namespaces)` rewrites both spellings -- Itanium `(anonymous namespace)` and MSVC `` `anonymous namespace' `` -- to the identifier `anonymous_namespace` BEFORE the bracket strip, matching what `analyzers/rtti/kuna_itaniumrtti.rs (sanitize_class_name)` already gives the same construct, so one toolchain's anonymous namespace is spelled like another's. The identifier spelling (not the verbatim c++filt text) is forced by two things: `load function` splits its argument on whitespace, so a name containing a space is unaddressable from the CLI, the console and every stage XML; and a scope name reaches emitted C unsanitized, where a `(` opens a parameter list. Two TUs that each define `helper` in an anonymous namespace still collide on one name -- the Itanium mangling is identical for both, so no demangler can separate them -- and their distinct addresses are what every resolver keys on. `demangle_raw` keeps the faithful c++filt text, unrewritten. No flag: this only ever corrects wrong output, and the wrong output was "no output". (2) `symbolnamerepair` (`p0_knowledge/kuna_symbolnamerepair.rs`) makes the seam skip an empty component instead of failing the build. It is a separate, gated decision because a name can be degenerate with no demangler involved: symbol-name bytes are attacker-controlled data that no header check validates, so a hostile binary buys a total denial of analysis for a few `.strtab` bytes -- relevant given the reporting binary was malware. Only the empty component is treated as degenerate; every other string names a Scope perfectly well however strange it looks, and dropping more would discard namespace structure the binary really carries. Loader-tier, so the gate is the `KUNA_SYMBOLNAMEREPAIR` env bridge (the symbol install runs inside `load file`, upstream of every per-function `option`), exported by both CLI surfaces; `--option symbolnamerepair off` restores the hard error, which is what someone investigating a binary's symbol table itself wants | **Collateral on the Itanium/ELF world is exactly zero and structurally so: on a binary with no `::`-qualified and no mangled symbol, neither code path executes.** The ONE deliberate output change on a binary that already loaded is the MSVC arm: a COFF symbol table carrying an anonymous-namespace name (`?A0x…@`) rendered `` `anonymous namespace'::Bar::foo `` and now renders `anonymous_namespace::Bar::foo` -- verified on a purpose-built fixture, since no tracked `.exe`/`.obj`/`.dll` in the repo (`native_payload.dll`, `binary.exe`, `bounce.obj`, …) contains a `?A0x` symbol, so the A/B set below cannot see it. Unflagged deliberately: the old spelling is not valid C and its embedded space makes the name unaddressable through `load function`, so this only ever corrects wrong output. Base-vs-branch `decompile-all --json` is **byte-identical on 8 real binaries**, four of them C++: faillog, grep, sort, libselinux.so.1, cpp_mangled_x86_64, cpp_noreturn_x86_64, cppsig_x86_64.so, cxxthrow_noreturn_x86_64. Witnesses that go from **zero output to full output**: the reporting binary `native_payload.dll` (MinGW PE32+, 2.7 MB, 1184 `_GLOBAL__N_` symbols) now exports a complete project -- **4061 of 4064 functions, 92.3 s, 222 MB peak RSS**, `.c` 3.8 MB / `.h` 313 KB / `.asm` 15.1 MB (the 3 failures are a 10 s budget timeout and two unmapped reads, unrelated); `libleveldb.so.1.23.0` **3751 functions, 273 `anonymous_namespace::` names**; `leveldbutil` **58 / 7**; and the new `anon_namespace_x86_64` fixture. **Speed**: interleaved min-of-9 `decompile-all --json` on `grep`, base **13.649 s** vs branch **13.751 s** (**+0.75%**) -- inside the measured noise floor of the box (a null A/B of the same binary against itself spreads 0.91 s over 9 reps), and necessarily so, since `grep` is C and reaches neither new path. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK** (no re-pin), `make test-stages` **493/493 PARITY OK**, `make rust-test` green (**4694 passed, 0 failed**), `make check-spec` green in **both** lenient and strict mode. Catalog 118 -> **119** settables (analysis tier 42 -> 43). Stages corpus 214 -> **215** files, `tests/stages/kuna-symbolnamerepair.xml` (6 assertions, 532 -> 538, baseline re-recorded): it loads the real `anon_namespace_x86_64` fixture and pins all three anonymous-namespace shapes plus the call-site rendering and a `min=0 max=0` guard that no `::::` survives anywhere. It covers the DEFAULT arm only, and that is a real limit rather than an oversight -- the broken arm is a LOAD failure and `option` requires a live image, so the gate that restores it has to be set before `load file` through its env bridge, which no script command can do; a regression is still loud, since a failed `load file` errors the script out and the baseline comparison reports REGRESSED rather than passing quietly. The option's own two-pass on/off coverage is the Rust gate, which can set the env var around each load. Tests: `kuna-console/tests/verify_symbolnamerepair.rs` (two-pass e2e over two new checked-in fixtures: `anon_namespace_x86_64` loads with all three anonymous-namespace shapes named, and the test forces the backstop OFF for that load, so it proves the two parts are independent rather than one masking the other; `hostile_scope_x86_64` -- a 4.7 KB ELF whose `.symtab` names a function `a::::b`, built reproducibly from checked-in assembly with no byte-patching -- loads by default and restores the exact `Non-global scope has empty name` error with the gate off), plus four `kuna-analysis` demangle unit tests and the gate's own env-token test |
| DIV-89 | the CLI's **stdout boundary** is fallible: a closed downstream pipe is a normal terminal condition, and the command's exit code survives it (driver-tier bug fix; no flag) | **Every `kuna` subcommand that writes stdout panicked when its reader went away.** `kuna catalog --markdown | head`, `kuna functions ... --json | head`, `kuna decompile ... | head` -- all of them died with `thread 'main' panicked at io/stdio.rs: failed printing to stdout: Broken pipe (os error 32)` and **exit 101**, because Rust `SIG_IGN`s SIGPIPE and `println!` unwraps the resulting `EPIPE`. Piping any kuna command into `head`/`less`/a closing reader was therefore a guaranteed panic trace. Every print site in `kuna-cli` now renders into a string and goes through `kuna-cli/src/output.rs (emit)`, which folds a write failure into the status the command already reached: `BrokenPipe` keeps that status and prints nothing, any other write error is reported and forces `1`. Keeping the status is the load-bearing half -- collapsing it to `0` would let `kuna test | head` report a REGRESSED parity run as green, and would silently void the DIV-45 failure contract for `kuna decompile`. `kuna specs` streams slacomp's stdout through the same boundary with stderr left inherited, so its per-spec progress and the warnings each `Compiling <spec>:` line attributes stay interleaved (capturing both streams cost 17s of silence and detached 615 of 1004 warnings from their spec on a 148-spec run). | 0/675; output byte-identical across 24 command invocations (stdout, stderr and exit code); `kuna-cli/tests/broken_pipe.rs` fails on the parent commit with exit 101 |
| DIV-90 | `kuna decompile` reports the console's own failure reason instead of a fixed string, for both the binary load and the analysis commit (driver-tier bug fix; no flag) | **The one surface an agent is most likely driving was the one surface that could not say what went wrong.** `kuna functions` / `decompile-all` / `decompile-project` load in-process and format the escaped `LowlevelError` (`kuna-cli/src/decompile_all.rs (load_program)`); `kuna decompile` drives `decomp_dbg` as a subprocess and so has to recover the reason from the transcript, and `kuna-cli/src/decompile.rs (check_errors)` threw it away -- it matched the trigger line `Could not create architecture` and answered `could not build an architecture for <binary> (unsupported/!recognized binary)` no matter what had actually happened. That is not merely less informative, it is sometimes an actively wrong diagnosis: `--sleighpath /nonexistent` masked `No sleigh specification for x86:LE:64:default`, i.e. a SPECS problem reported as a problem with the user's binary. It also made DIV-88's `symbolnamerepair` symptom (`could not build an architecture for <binary>: Non-global scope has empty name`, the string `docs/options.md` publishes as the flip trigger) unmatchable from this surface -- named in the DIV-88 row as a separate driver-tier defect that row did not fix. The transcript always carried the reason: `IfcLoadFile`'s error arm prints `{e.explain()}` and then the trigger on the next line, both to stdout, so `arch_failure_reason` reads the preceding line and the four surfaces now converge on one string, byte for byte. The generic wording survives only as the fallback for a transcript where no reason was printed at all (the command echo is not a reason). **The second half is worse and silent (GH-339).** A failed analysis commit -- `IfcReadSymbols` -> `commit_pending_analysis` -- is an `Execution error:` the console prints before *keeping the session alive*, so `print C` still rendered C, the out-file was non-empty, and `kuna decompile` printed quietly degraded output and **exited 0** while the other three surfaces exited 1 with `read symbols (analysis commit) failed: <reason>`. Degraded how is not visible in the C: the commit is not transactional, so an abort leaves the early arms applied and abandons library/DWARF prototypes, context paints, tracked registers, call-fixups, stack locals and line comments -- and it is unretryable, the stash having been drained. Exit 0 there is indistinguishable from a binary that simply has no symbols, which is the one thing a driving agent must be able to tell apart. `read_symbols_failure` attributes each console diagnostic to the command echo above it and reports a `read symbols` failure in the in-process surfaces' exact wording, with their exit code. The engine defect that produced the reporting binary's particular reason (an `st_size` that overflows the type factory's `int4` domain) is fixed separately; this row is about the driver never being able to say so | 0/675 and 0 stage assertions -- both parity corpora are symbol-less bytechunks driven by `decomp_test_dbg`, so neither can reach `kuna-cli`.
