Four-surface agreement is asserted end-to-end over the checked-in `hostile_scope_x86_64` fixture with `--option symbolnamerepair off` (`kuna-cli/tests/decompile_cli.rs (every_surface_reports_the_same_load_failure)`), which fails on the parent commit; the transcript recoveries are pinned by recorded-transcript unit tests (reason present, reason absent, prompt-only predecessor, commit failure, misattribution). No measurable speed delta: two line scans of a transcript already held in memory, only on the failure paths. `scripts/decompile.py (_check_errors)`, the Python library shim that mirrors this function for `scripts/pipeline/`, carries the same two recoveries so the two drivers do not disagree again |
| DIV-91 | a symbol's **declared extent is clamped into the type factory's `int4` domain BEFORE the narrowing cast**, not after (loader/commit-tier bug fix; no flag) | **A single data symbol whose `st_size` was not representable as an `int4` cost the binary its whole analysis commit, or the process.** `st_size` is a 64-bit ELF field that no header check validates, so it is ordinary attacker-controlled data; arm 4a of `engine.rs (commit_analysis_output)` narrowed it with `(*size).max(1) as int4`, which clamps the number AFTER truncating it and therefore inspects the wrong one. Two classes fell through. (1) **Low 32 bits zero** (`0x1_0000_0000`): truncates to `0`, `.max(1)` never sees it, `add_symbol_internal` rejects the size-0 type -- and because `commit_analysis_output` mutates the architecture arm by arm and propagates the first error, that one symbol abandoned every LATER arm (library prototypes, cppsig and DWARF prototypes, ARM/MIPS context paints, tracked registers, call-fixups, DWARF stack locals, DWARF line comments) after arms 0-4 had already applied in place. It is also unretryable: both `pending_analysis` and `loader_data_objects` are `mem::take`n before the failure, so a second `read symbols` commits nothing. `kuna functions` / `decompile-all` / `decompile-project` exited 1 with no output. (2) **Low 32 bits with the sign bit set** (`0x8000_0000`, `0xffff_fff0`): truncates to a NEGATIVE `int4`, which `TypeFactory::get_base` handed straight to `typecache[s as usize]` -- an out-of-bounds index, so this was a **process abort**, exit 101, not an error (`index out of bounds: the len is 9 but the index is 18446744073709551600`). The issue as filed claimed this case loaded clean; it does not. The fix clamps into `1 ..= int4::MAX` before the cast, which closes both at the one live site, applies the same shape to the DWARF twin at arm 1a (already bounded upstream by a `filter(|&s| s >= 1)` on an `int4`, so this is uniformity, not a second defect), and adds a lower bound at BOTH type-factory cache lookups (`get_base`, `get_type_char_sized`) so a negative size is an ordinary `KunaError` rather than an abort if one ever reaches them another way. `get_base` REFUSES a negative rather than falling through to `Datatype::new`, because `find_add` then computes an alignment from `size as uint4` and can overflow -- a second, debug-only panic on the same input. **The clamp is saturating, and that is the one judgment call.** A `0x1_0000_0004` symbol goes from a silently-wrong 4-byte extent to a ~2 GiB one, so it covers -- and shadows -- every UNNAMED address above it. That is a real cost, stated rather than designed away: a legal `st_size` of `0x7fffffff` already reaches the identical state today, so no new hazard class is introduced, and narrowing an extent to "distance to the next symbol" is a heuristic that by the standing options contract would need its own named option. What is NOT changed here is whether one bad symbol voids the whole commit; that is a separate design question (20 in-loop `?` sites), and the sibling GH-337/GH-339 driver PR makes such a failure loud on all surfaces instead. | **Collateral on real software is zero, and structurally so: no `STT_OBJECT` symbol with `st_size > int4::MAX` exists in ANY ELF scanned** -- every `/usr/bin` binary (5675 files), every `/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu` shared object, `tests/bug-repro/*` and all 100+ committed fixtures -- so no previously-loading real binary reaches the changed path. `decompile-all --json` base vs branch byte-identical on grep, sort, faillog, libselinux.so.1, fmt_x86_64, cpp_mangled_x86_64. The deliberate output change is confined to synthetic hostile input: `st_size = 0x1_0000_0004` rendered `return g_a;` and now renders `return g_a[0];`, and unnamed addresses inside the extent render `g_huge[0x18]` instead of `dat_<addr>` -- byte for byte what `st_size = 0x7fffffff` already produced before this change. Measured, NOT an OOM or a hang: 0.47 s wall / 53 MB peak RSS on the 2 GiB-extent fixture, the same as the sane-size control (the oversize extent becomes a `TypeArray` count, never an allocation). Witnesses that go from **failure to full output**: `hostile_size_low32_x86_64` (was `error: read symbols (analysis commit) failed: g_a symbol created with zero size type`, exit 1) and `hostile_size_neg_x86_64` (was a panic at `dtype.rs:6070`, exit 101) both now report `main` and render `g_a` by name. **Speed: no measurable change, and the box could not resolve one anyway.** Interleaved, alternating-arm, minimum-of-4 `decompile-all --json`, base vs branch: `libselinux.so.1` (621 functions) 8.694 s -> 8.680 s (**0%**), `grep` (449 functions) 15.837 s -> 14.914 s (**-5.00%**, i.e. the branch reading FASTER, which is noise) -- and a NULL CONTROL of the same shape, the base binary A/B'd against itself on `grep`, read **-4.00%** over the same reps, so the noise floor on this host (two concurrent workspace suites, load average 15-23 on 80 cores) is itself near the 5% budget. Structurally the change is one extra comparison per data symbol plus one sign test per `get_base`, on a loop that runs once per `STT_OBJECT` entry. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK** (no re-pin), `make test-stages` **538/538 PARITY OK** (no new stage XML, so no corpus-count bump and no baseline re-record), `make rust-test` **CARGO_EXIT=0** -- 325 suites, **4838 passed, 0 failed**, 38 ignored, 0 failure blocks -- `make check-spec` OK lenient + strict, `kuna catalog --check` OK. No option added, so no catalog counts move. Both parity gates are VACUOUS here and stated as such: every fixture in `tests/datatests/` and `tests/stages/` is a symbol-less `<bytechunk>` that never constructs an `ObjectLoadImage`, so a `.symtab`-gated change is unreachable from them in either direction. The coverage is `kuna-console/tests/verify_hostile_symbol_sizes.rs` (3 e2e cases over 3 new checked-in fixtures, built reproducibly from checked-in assembly with no byte-patching, differing ONLY in the `st_size` of `g_a`) plus a `TypeFactory` unit test; A/B'd against the reverted source, the two hostile cases fail with exactly the historical error and the historical panic and the sane-size control passes both ways |
| DIV-92 | `noreturn_discstrict` (new kuna option, default-ON): the discovered-no-return evidence tally counts only evidence about the PROGRAM, so a kuna decode failure can no longer forge a no-return verdict | **A callee that plainly returns could be concluded no-return by three bytes kuna cannot decode, after which the flow layer deleted the live tail of every one of its callers.** `noreturn_disc` (DIV-22) concludes a callee no-return once >=3 of its call sites "show no valid fall-through", and three arms implemented that test, tried in order: the successor is not a decoded instruction start, the successor is data, the successor is another function's entry. The first arm does not mean what its name says. `listing/walk.rs` pushes EVERY call's fall-through onto the per-function instruction worklist unconditionally and that worklist drains before the function is left, so a call's successor is always ATTEMPTED; it fails to become an instruction start in exactly three ways -- `decode_one` returned `Err`, the decode was zero-length, or the address is outside every executable range -- and the Listing records no decode outcome (`CodeUnit` has only `Instruction`/`Data`/`Undefined`, and `Undefined` is derived from "in an exec range and not covered", so a failed decode and an unvisited byte are indistinguishable). The arm was therefore precisely and only a DECODE-FAILURE DETECTOR, i.e. a statement about kuna rather than about the callee, and it fed a rule that REMOVES CODE. GH-312's witness is minimal: `retseven` is `mov $7,%eax ; ret`, three callers follow their `call retseven` with the single byte `0x06` (invalid in 64-bit mode), the tally reaches exactly 3, and `usesret` -- whose `return retseven() + 3` is ordinary live code -- collapses to `void usesret(void) { retseven(); // no-return }`; the forged fact then propagates through the fixpoint's rule 2 to all three gap callers and truncates `_start` after its first call. `noreturn_discstrict` keeps the terminal arm (a call with no fall-through VMA at all -- a tail jump lowered to a call, a property of the call INSTRUCTION) and the two POSITIVE arms (the successor is data, so it is outside every executable range and the compiler emitted nothing to fall into; the successor is another function's entry, so the compiler left the caller no tail) and drops the bare one; `off` restores the legacy three-arm order verbatim. Dropping it is also what makes the DATA arm reachable for the first time -- `is_data` implies `!is_instruction_start`, so under the legacy order the arm above consumed every site it would have caught. No decode-outcome record was added to the Listing deliberately: recording WHY an address has no instruction would make the arm honest but leave it a statement about the decoder, so a SLEIGH spec gap on Thumb/MIPS16/PPC VLE would still vote to delete a caller's code. It is an option rather than a bare bug fix because it narrows a default-ON, code-removing analyzer ("when in doubt, gate it") | **975 real binaries measured, one weak-arm vote in all of them, zero conclusions changed.** Instrumented A/B of the two tallies inside one process (both computed, the sets diffed), `--mode aggressive` so the Listing is on for every surface: **693 stripped x86-64 decbench binaries** legacy **766** callees concluded no-return vs strict **766**, 0 lost / 0 gained, and the weak arm cast **0** votes at any call site; the same corpus under the shipped `auto` policy (660 listing-on) legacy **581** vs strict **581**, 0 lost / 0 gained; **110 stripped non-x86-64 decbench binaries** (98 ARM32, 12 i386 PE -- the slice where kuna's decoder gaps are widest and DIV-68 puts the Listing on) legacy **0** vs strict **0**, and the weak arm cast **1** vote in the entire corpus, on `O2-noinline/u-boot`, at a callee that needs 3; **168 `/usr/bin` ELF binaries** 0 weak votes; **4 clang/LLVM-14-built binaries** (`FileCheck-14`, `clang-14`, `llvm-nm`, `llvm-strings` -- the [[kuna clang blind spot]] direction, since all of decbench is gcc-built) 0 weak votes, 0 conclusions. On fixed-width RISC ISAs almost every bit pattern decodes, which is why the arm is silent exactly where it was feared most. **C collateral**: `decompile-all --json | jq -r '.functions[].code'` off vs on over 18 binaries (`tests/bug-repro/{grep,sort,faillog,libselinux.so.1,betaflight_STM32F405.elf}`, `/usr/bin/FileCheck-14`, and 12 decbench targets chosen as the highest-conclusion-count binaries in the corpus incl. dpkg/certtool/scp/find) -- **0 changed lines of 382,000+**, and the `// no-return` annotation count is identical in every pane (FileCheck-14 102/102, dpkg 516/516, betaflight 0/0). **Speed**: interleaved min-of-8 with the arm order ALTERNATED per rep, on a box at load 10-18 shared with other agents -- analysis-only (`kuna functions --mode aggressive`, dpkg) **-13.3%**, whole-binary `decompile-all` (grep) **-3.8%**; an earlier same-order min-of-6 on the identical grep target read **+2.58%**, so the sign is ordering-dependent and the number is noise-dominated. No slowdown is possible in principle: the strict path performs strictly FEWER Listing lookups per call site, and on every binary measured the emitted C is byte-identical, so the decompile work is unchanged. **Gates**: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK** (no re-pin), `make test-stages` **539/539 PARITY OK** (baseline re-recorded for the one new assertion), `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` green lenient AND strict, `kuna catalog --check` OK. The two parity gates are VACUOUS here and are stated as such: every fixture in `tests/datatests/` and `tests/stages/` is a symbol-less `<bytechunk>`, the Listing is built only on the real-object bootstrap path, so a Listing-gated change is unreachable from them -- "0/675 changed" is true and proves nothing. Catalog 119 -> **120** settables (transform tier 50 -> **51**). Stages corpus 215 -> **216** files: `tests/stages/kuna-noreturn-discstrict.xml` proves the assertion is WIRED (option parses, round-trips, inert on the XML path) and can prove no more, the same limit `ghangr-noreturn-error.xml` documents. The behavior gate is `kuna-console/tests/verify_noreturn_discstrict.rs` -- four cross-crate tests over the new checked-in `noreturn_discstrict_x86_64` fixture (built reproducibly from checked-in assembly by the one-line `gcc` command in its header, nothing byte-patched): the default keeps `return retseven() + 3` and marks nothing no-return, `--option noreturn_discstrict off` restores the forged `// no-return` form and the truncated `_start`, and -- the no-regression half -- the pre-existing `noreturn_disc_x86_64` fixture's genuine `die()` wrapper is STILL concluded no-return under the default with `noreturn_propagate` forced off, because all four of its call sites take their evidence from the surviving "next function's entry" arm |
| DIV-93 | the four ARM entry-discovery options -- `cortexmvectors` (#248), `ptrentry` (#255), `tailcallentry` (#259), `poolentry` (#278) -- join `AGGRESSIVE_OVERRIDES` and leave the `UNEVALUATED` list in `p0_knowledge/modes.rs` (preset membership; no new option, no engine-default change) | **All four shipped default-OFF and were therefore inert on every default path -- the DIV-64 `itaniumrtti` trap, four times over.** `auto` resolves to `aggressive` under 500 KiB, which is essentially every invocation, and the preset is what the whole-binary surfaces (`decompile-all`, `functions`, `decompile-project`, the WASM front-end, the benchmark) actually run; a default-off option outside the preset never executes there. `decompile_all.rs` injects only `listing` + `funcstart_patterns` + `aif` on non-x86-64, so these four were reachable solely through an explicit `--option`. The measured consequence: the default path's stripped-ARM entry recall had not moved at all since the four passes merged. They are flipped **jointly** because they compose -- `poolentry`'s acceptance numbers were themselves measured on top of the other three -- which is exactly what the `UNEVALUATED` comment demanded before any flip. `ptrentry`/`tailcallentry`/`poolentry` additionally consume the Listing, and the preset supplies `listing` ahead of them in the same list, so the ordering is load-bearing. The catalog defaults stay `off`, which is what keeps the XML corpus and `--mode reliable` unchanged | **Corpus, 110 stripped non-x86-64 decbench twins against their unstripped twins' symbol tables (50,724 ground-truth function starts), `--mode aggressive`:** entry recall **44,957 -> 47,330 (88.63% -> 93.31%, +2,373)** while mid-body false entries **FALL 8,333 -> 7,117**. Per option, marginal over the default arm: `cortexmvectors` +136 entries / +136 ground truth (100.0%), `ptrentry` +1,588 / +1,565 (98.6%), `tailcallentry` +649 / +645 (99.4%), `poolentry` +167 / +165 (98.8%) plus 1,217 phantom entries retired. Combined: 2,402 added entries, **98.8% of them real function starts**, and **`lost_exact` = 0 -- not one ground-truth entry is dropped by any arm**, including `poolentry`, whose 1,217 removals contain zero ground-truth addresses. **0 of 110 binaries lose recall**; exactly one (`O2-noinline/u-boot`) gains mid-body entries, +2. Every ARM project gains (crazyflie +839, betaflight +523, cleanflight +263, libopencm3 +201, u-boot +173, chibios +162, nuttx +147, riot-os +43, freertos +22). **Both directions, per option.** Leave-one-out from the combination (reference = all four on) shows none of the four is redundant given the others: dropping `cortexmvectors` loses 27 ground-truth entries, `ptrentry` 1,427, `tailcallentry` 645, `poolentry` 136 -- and dropping `poolentry` is also the only one that RAISES mid-body false entries, 7,117 -> 8,337, which is the precision half of the pair. `cortexmvectors` is the smallest contributor in context (+27, against +136 measured alone, because the other three independently reach most of what it unlocks) but is never negative and costs nothing. **Non-ARM inertness is measured, not assumed:** entry sets are IDENTICAL over 90 x86-64 decbench twins (added 0, lost 0) and over the 12 i386 PE images inside the ARM corpus, and `decompile-all --json` emitted C is **byte-identical on 8 x86-64 binaries** (grep, sort, faillog, libselinux.so.1, du, id, wc, head). **Collateral on ARM is the point:** msc.elf 59 -> 70 functions, ch.elf 344 -> 398, nuttx 727 -> 792, riot-os hello-world 151 -> 171, betaflight 5013 -> 5088, cf2.elf 1966 -> 1973; the newly emitted bodies are real named routines (msc.elf: all 11 new entries are DWARF starts -- `ramdisk_write`, `usbd_register_control_callback`, `msc_control_request`, `dwc_ep_nak_set`, ...; ch.elf 57/57, and its 3 removals are not ground truth). **Speed**, interleaved min-of-4 on a loaded shared box: end-to-end `decompile-all` cf2.elf **37.42 s -> 35.23 s (-5.8%, a speedup** -- retiring phantom bodies pays for the real ones), nuttx and the x86-64 controls inside noise; discovery-only `kuna functions` costs **+6.2%** (cf2.elf) / **+6.6%** (betaflight), which is more work for more output (+7 and +75 functions) on the one surface that does discovery and nothing else. **Auto-threshold caveat, disclosed rather than papered over:** `auto` only selects `aggressive` below 500 KiB, so on the 105 corpus binaries it actually reaches, the default-path recall is **90.92% -> 96.06% (+1,677)** with mid-body 4,079 -> 3,279 -- better than the aggregate. The 5 images at or over the threshold (2x u-boot, 3x betaflight) resolve to `reliable` and get neither these four nor `aifstrict`, leaving +696 recoverable entries on the table there; `--mode aggressive` still delivers them, and the Ghidra front-end applies the preset unconditionally (`kuna-ghidra/src/process.rs (apply_ghidra_mode_defaults)`), so it is unaffected by the threshold. Raising or size-tiering that boundary is a separate DIV, not this one. **Gates: 0/675 datatests** -- and proven rather than asserted: a throwaway build with all four forced ON as *engine* defaults (strictly stronger than preset membership, which the XML harness never applies) still reports 675/675 and 538/538 PARITY OK, confirming the `<binaryimage>` bootstrap never runs the analyzer tier (DIV-61/DIV-65) |
| DIV-94 | `symbolnamechars` (new kuna option, `off`/`safe`/`ident`, default **`safe`**) -- a symbol name's raw bytes are sanitized where the name is MINTED, plus an unflagged escape of the `/* renamed from "<raw>" */` type-name note | **A symbol name reached emitted C verbatim, and nothing between `.strtab` and the printer validated one byte of it.** The name is printed into the `// Function: <name> @ <addr>` header comment, the `.h` prototype, the definition, every call site and the `.asm` label, so three shapes restructured the C document rather than merely looking odd: a `*/` closed the header comment early and turned the rest of the line into code; a raw `0x0a` split all five of those renderings across two lines each (`void a\nb(void)` declares nothing, and `kuna functions` printed the TSV row on two lines); and a `//` commented out the remainder of the line, parameter list and `;` included. A fourth broke identity rather than syntax: the name is decoded with `String::from_utf8_lossy`, so two symbols differing only in an invalid byte (`a\x80b` at one address, `a\x81b` at another) became the SAME `String` and the export carried two definitions and two prototypes with one name -- a C redefinition with no way to tell which call went where. Symbol-name bytes are attacker-controlled data no header check validates, so all four cost a few `.strtab` bytes. **The option is VALUED, not a bool**, and that is the measured half: the most common name in the wild that is not valid C is not hostile at all, it is gcc's clone suffix (`err_fatal.constprop.0`, `main.part.1`, `add_fdes.cold`, `__x86.get_pc_thunk.bx`), which appears on most `-O2` binaries and on six fixtures checked into this repo. `safe` (the default) rewrites ONLY the structural set -- ASCII control bytes `0x00`-`0x1F`/`0x7F`, `"`/`'`/`\`, a `*` or `/` that forms `*/`, `/*` or `//` (both characters of the pair), and every byte of an invalid UTF-8 sequence -- and is a no-op on every one of those clone suffixes. `ident` additionally reduces each `::` component to `[A-Za-z0-9_]`, which is what someone who intends to COMPILE the export wants and exactly why it cannot be the default. Each rewritten byte becomes its `_x<hh>` hex escape rather than a single `_`: `_` is not injective (`a"b`, `a'b` and `a\nb` all collapse to `a_b`), which would reproduce the redefinition defect with a different trigger. **Mint time, not print time**, and for a control-surface reason: `kuna decompile <name>`, the console's `load function` and the DB scope path all key on the string in `prog.symbols`, so a printer-side rewrite would put a name in the `.c` that cannot be handed back to the CLI. The sanitizer runs after the demangler (so it sees the reduced name, not the `_ZN...` envelope) and before the scope splitter (so it never contends with `symbolnamerepair` over the same empty component), at both mints: the loader's `.symtab`/PLT/`.dynsym`/ET_REL walks and the data-symbol walk in `kuna-analysis/src/loadimage_object.rs`, and the analysis passes' recovered names (DWARF `DW_AT_name`, Go `pclntab`, PDB, RTTI) via `pass.rs (AnalysisOutput::sanitize_names)`. Loader-tier, so the gate is the `KUNA_SYMBOLNAMECHARS` env bridge exported by both CLI surfaces, the `symbolnamerepair` pattern. Two smaller parts ride along, both unflagged strict fixes: `printc.rs`'s `/* renamed from "<raw>" */` note interpolated a RAW type name into a comment -- the identical injection hole in a construct `sanitize_type_name` was written to close -- and now escapes what it quotes; and `kuna_itaniumrtti.rs (sanitize_class_name)` becomes a one-line wrapper over the hoisted shared `sanitize_ident_chain`, keeping its own unit tests as the regression net | **Collateral is zero and measured twice over.** `decompile-all --json` is **byte-identical on 17 real binaries** both base-vs-branch and `off`-vs-default: grep, sort, faillog, libselinux.so.1, betaflight_STM32F405.elf (ARM Cortex-M), fid/prog, cpp_mangled_x86_64, cppsig_x86_64.so, anon_namespace_x86_64, cpp_noreturn_x86_64, noreturn_error_x86_64, regglobal_fmt_x86_64, mcount_x86_64, plt_ppc64le, i386_pie_nl, pe_dwarf.exe, hostile_scope_x86_64. Stronger: `kuna functions` off-vs-default over **every one of the 92 tracked ELF/PE/Mach-O files in the repo** rewrites **0** names -- the clone-suffix claim is proved, not assumed. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK** (no re-pin), `make test-stages` **547/547 PARITY OK**, `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` green in both lenient and strict mode, `kuna catalog --check` OK. Catalog 119 -> **120** settables (analysis tier 43 -> 44); stages corpus 215 -> **216** files (baseline 538 -> 547). **Speed** (interleaved min-of-4 `decompile-all --json`, on a box running several agents concurrently): grep 15.009 s -> 14.291 s (**-4.78%**), libselinux.so.1 9.103 s -> 9.227 s (**+1.36%**), regglobal_fmt_x86_64 2.608 s -> 2.588 s (**-0.76%**) -- the spread is noise in both directions, and structurally so: `safe`'s pre-scan borrows and allocates nothing for a clean name. **Witness** (`hostile_symname_x86_64`, a new 5 KB ELF built reproducibly from checked-in assembly plus a checked-in `.strtab` patch script, since GNU as will not put a raw `0x0a` or `0x80` in a symbol name): before, the exported `.c` reads `a*/b();` / `a//b();` / a call split across two lines / two identical `a<U+FFFD>b` definitions, and the `.h` carries two identical prototypes; after, `a_x2a_x2fb`, `a_x2f_x2fb`, `a_x0ab`, `a_x80b`, `a_x81b` and the data symbol `d_x2a_x2fx` -- six distinct names, and every one of them still resolves through `kuna decompile <name>`. Tests: `tests/stages/kuna-symbolnamechars.xml` (9 assertions over that fixture, DEFAULT arm only -- the gate is consumed inside `load file`, so no script command can set it beforehand; same documented limit as `kuna-symbolnamerepair.xml`) plus `kuna-console/tests/verify_symbolnamechars.rs` (four e2e tests: `off` restores every half including the two-into-one U+FFFD collapse, `safe` neutralizes all four and keeps six symbols six names, `safe` is byte-identical to `off` on a real gcc `-O2` binary while `ident` folds its `.constprop.0`, and every name `kuna functions` reports still resolves through `lookup_symbol` on the hostile fixture AND two real unstripped C++ binaries) and 8 sanitizer unit tests |
| DIV-95 | `symbolnamebound` (new kuna option, VALUED `<n>|off`, default **256**): a ceiling on how many `Scope`s one qualified symbol name may nest, plus per-component (1024 B) and total-scope-path (4096 B) length ceilings | **One symbol name could turn 600 KB of `.strtab` into 292 MB of RSS, and 60 KB into a 20-second stall.** `Database::find_create_scope_from_symbol_name` splits a loader symbol name on every `::` and nests one `Scope` per component with no bound on the count, the per-component length or the total. A `Scope` is not cheap -- a `RangeList`, three `BTreeMap`s, two `String`s and a `maptable` sized to the address-space count, measured at 1,493 B/component over 200,000 components and 1,504 B over 400,000 -- and the interning key is `hash_scope_name(parent.unique_id, name)`, so a repeated component name still allocates a fresh `Scope` at every level: `a::a::a::...` costs as much as a path of distinct names. Measured on `kuna functions` over ELFs whose `.symtab` carries one function named `("a::" * N) + "z"`: 52,932 KB of maxRSS at N=0, 79,288 KB at N=20,000, 345,132 KB at N=200,000 -- 291,724 KB for 600,001 bytes of `.strtab`, a **~498x input-to-RSS amplifier**. The amplifier is strictly per-COMPONENT, not per byte: the same 600,000 bytes in ONE component cost +1,168 KB (1.9x), because a long component is one `String`, not a chain of `Scope`s. `decompile-all` was worse, quadratic in depth: 0.11 s at N=0, 17.83 s at N=20,000, 78.06 s at N=40,000, and it did not finish N=200,000 inside 400 s. A symbol name is attacker-controlled data that no header check validates -- a name is not parsed at all until it is split here -- so this was a denial-of-analysis primitive for the price of a few `.strtab` bytes, on the same `read_loader_symbols`-inside-`load file` seam DIV-88 covers for a DIFFERENT mechanism (a rejected name, not an unbounded one), which is why `symbolnamerepair` correctly does not and must not fire on it. **Shape of the fix.** `p0_knowledge/kuna_symbolnamebound.rs` exposes one helper, `bound_scope_path`, applied at BOTH ends of the seam: the create path (`find_create_scope_from_symbol_name`) and the read path (`resolve_scope_from_symbol_name`), plus the loader's own name list and the `load function` / `lookup_symbol` / `find_entry_by_name` queries. Both ends matter: bounding only the create side would install a symbol under a folded path that its own name could no longer find, and every call to it would render `sub_<addr>` -- the DIV-59 failure mode. A name over a limit is FOLDED, not truncated: the dropped run of components collapses into one synthetic component `<24 identifier-safe bytes of the first dropped component>__x<16 hex>`, where the hex is FNV-1a 64 over the exact original byte range that was folded away. Distinct names therefore stay distinct, and two symbols that share a scope path but differ in their base name still share the folded scope, because the fold hashes the scope range and never the base name. The base name is never rewritten at all -- it nests no `Scope`, so it was never the amplifier, and a 1,780-byte Go generic instantiation in `/usr/bin/dockerd` (the longest real installed name found anywhere) carries no `::` and is untouched. The hash is written out rather than taken from `DefaultHasher`/`RandomState`, whose per-process seed would make the folded spelling differ between runs and between the `functions` and `decompile` surfaces; the stage XML pins the exact digits. `bound_scope_path` is idempotent and returns `Cow::Borrowed` after one allocation-free scan when nothing is over a limit, which is every real name, so the ordinary load pays nothing. Loader-tier, so the gate is the `KUNA_SYMBOLNAMEBOUND` env bridge (the symbol install runs inside `load file`, upstream of every per-function `option`), exported by both CLI surfaces; `Architecture::analysis_symbolnamebound` exists only for catalog visibility, and is the first VALUED live `current` (which is why `emit_catalog_json`'s closure now yields a `Cow`; every fixed-token arm still borrows, so the emitted bytes are unchanged). A SEPARATE option from `symbolnamerepair` on purpose: turning the repair off is a debugging affordance for someone inspecting a symbol table, and that must not simultaneously remove a resource bound; and a bound is a number, which `on|off` cannot express. **The defaults are measured, and the first attempt was wrong.** Across 1,683,515 demangled names -- the repo fixtures, fourteen large C++ objects (libLLVM-15/14, libnode, webkit2gtk, ICU, z3, jsc, libstdc++, leveldb), nine rustc-built binaries including a 153 MB `librustc_driver`, and the sixty largest ELF objects under `/usr/lib/x86_64-linux-gnu` + `/usr/bin` -- the deepest `::` nesting is **21** (a Rust v0 tuple-impl name), C++ never exceeds **6**, and the longest scope component is **exactly 256 bytes**, which is where rustc's own mangler truncates a legacy path segment. On that evidence the first cut shipped 32/256/1024 and the collateral A/B caught it: `private/FakeCrypt` DIFFERED, because the DWARF name path does NOT strip template arguments, so every `::` inside `<...>` counts as a scope component and its deepest installed name is **79**, with a 1,095-byte scope path. The shipped 256/1024/4096 clear those by 3.2x, 4x and 3.7x, and FakeCrypt is byte-identical again. **What this does NOT close, stated rather than discovered later**: it caps what ONE name costs, not what a symbol TABLE costs. The amplifier is per-`Scope`, so the same `.strtab` bytes spent on many moderately deep names buy the same memory -- **3,000 distinct 64-component names (1.9 MB of ELF) cost 343 MB with the gate on or off**, since none reaches the ceiling and none of their scopes can be shared. Closing that needs a cap on the total `Scope` population or a cheaper `Scope`, both larger changes needing their own measurement (libLLVM alone installs ~98,600 functions). This closes the reported primitive and the quadratic blowup that rode on it | **The bound collapses the amplifier and costs real output nothing.** `kuna functions` maxRSS, default vs `symbolnamebound off`: N=20,000 **53,524 KB vs 78,644 KB**; N=200,000 **57,128 KB vs 345,128 KB**; N=1,000,000 **76,980 KB vs 1,541,320 KB**; N=2,000,000 (a 6 MB ELF) **107,388 KB vs 3,034,044 KB**. Under a 2 GiB `ulimit -v` at N=2,000,000 the default arm exits 0 and the unbounded arm ABORTS -- `memory allocation of 936 bytes failed`, exit 134 -- which is the OOM the issue extrapolates to, reproduced deterministically rather than by exhausting the machine. `decompile-all` default vs off: **0.11 s vs 17.83 s** at N=20,000, **0.15 s vs 78.06 s** at N=40,000, **0.17 s vs 400 s timeout** at N=200,000. **Collateral is zero on real binaries**: `decompile-all --json` default vs `symbolnamebound off` is byte-identical on all 12 binaries, five of them C++ and two the deeply-qualified real-world samples (`private/FakeCrypt`, a rustc binary whose DWARF names reach depth 79, and `private/native_payload.dll`, a MinGW C++ DLL), plus grep, sort, faillog and libselinux.so.1. `libstdc++.so.6` took two attempts and the first failure is worth recording: it differed in exactly ONE of its 5,945 records, `std::__cxx11::money_get::_M_extract @ 0x1029e0`, which hit the 10 s per-function watchdog in the arm that ran while three other agents' builds were saturating the box -- every other record, the name sets and the record ORDER were already identical, that one function returns `ok` on three reruns in each arm and on the base binary, and the whole-binary A/B is byte-identical on a re-run. A wall-clock budget inside a compared artifact is a contention flake waiting to happen. **Speed**: interleaved, alternating arms, min-of-9, both arms pinned to one core (the box was running several other agents' builds throughout, which is exactly why the first unpinned min-of-4 read +13.95% on FakeCrypt and the pinned min-of-9 reads 0.0%): base vs branch, `kuna functions --json` on `private/FakeCrypt` (9,011 names) **1.3386 s vs 1.3380 s (0.0%)** and on `libstdc++.so.6` (5,945 names) **2.3474 s vs 2.3507 s (+0.1%)**; `decompile-all --json` on grep, unpinned min-of-4, **14.279 s vs 14.344 s (+0.45%)**. The name path is where the whole cost of this change lives, and the fast path returns `Cow::Borrowed` after one allocation-free scan. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK** (no re-pin), `make test-stages` **544/544 PARITY OK**, `make rust-test` green (**4,847 passed, 0 failed**), `make check-spec` green in both lenient and strict mode, `kuna catalog --check` OK. Catalog 119 -> **120** settables (analysis tier 43 -> 44). Stages corpus 215 -> **216** files, `tests/stages/kuna-symbolnamebound.xml` (6 assertions, 538 -> 544, baseline re-recorded): DEFAULT arm only, the same env-bridge limit DIV-88 documents, with the two-pass on/off coverage plus the resource assertion (measured as allocated `Scope`s, not a wall clock) in `kuna-console/tests/verify_symbolnamebound.rs` over a new checked-in `deep_scope_x86_64` fixture -- an 11.7 KB ELF whose `.symtab` names a 1000-component function and whose `main` calls it, built reproducibly from checked-in assembly with no byte-patching |

| DIV-96 | `msvcfpconst` **on** by default: an MSVC `__real@` floating-point constant COMDAT is decoded from its own mangled symbol name, and both the recovered slot and the object's defined `__real@` COMDATs are declared foldable (new kuna option, no upstream counterpart) | **The floating-point half of an MSVC object was written entirely in opaque addresses.** MSVC never encodes a floating-point literal into the instruction stream -- x87 and SSE both load one from memory -- so it emits each literal as a COMDAT whose NAME spells the value (`__real@8@3ffec90fdaa22168c000` is pi/4). COMDAT folding keeps the definition in exactly one translation unit, so in every OTHER object that symbol is undefined: no section, no bytes, and the loader binds it to a synthetic extern slot with nothing behind it. The defined half fares no better at the shipped defaults -- its bytes are mapped and read-only, but folding a read-only global is gated by the program-wide `option readonly`, default-off. A Command & Conquer `bounce.obj` therefore rendered `v7 = ((double)(int)Random2Class::operator()(0,0x7ffffffe) * dat_402020 + dat_402040) * dat_400ae0;`, every operand an address. `kuna-analysis/src/loader/kuna_msvcfpconst.rs` decodes three spellings -- `__real@<size>@<20 hex>` (x87 80-bit extended, `<size>` 4 = float / 8 = double; the VC6-era form), `__real@<16 hex>` (IEEE double bits) and `__real@<8 hex>` (IEEE float bits, what MSVC has emitted for a `float` literal since VS2005) -- materialises the bytes at each undefined slot, and pushes BOTH halves' ranges onto `ObjectLoadImage::dynreloc_const`, the constant-by-construction exception list DIV-84 built, so they fold with `readonly` still off. Listing only one half would leave one operand of an expression a literal and its neighbour opaque. Every x87 encoding with no faithful `f64` image is refused rather than approximated (Inf/NaN, denormal, pseudo-denormal, unnormal, out-of-range), as is every other mangling (`__xmm@`, `__ymm@`): a wrong constant is worse than an honest `dat_<addr>`. A defined COMDAT's mapped bytes are cross-checked against its own name before its range is admitted, which is also what keeps the ET_REL pre-relocation-bytes hazard off this path. Loader-tier: read via the `KUNA_MSVCFPCONST` env var, the `dynrelocs`/`relocrebase` bridge | **`bounce.obj`: all 12 `__real@` constants recovered (7 defined, 5 undefined), `dat_` references 14 -> 3** -- the 3 remaining are an integer extern (`BRIDGE_LEPTON_HEIGHT`), correctly untouched; the quoted expression becomes `((double)(int)Random2Class::operator()(0,0x7ffffffe) * 4.656612877414201e-10 + 1.0) * 0.7853981633974483` (a 31-bit random scaled into [0,1), biased, times pi/4). `combat.obj`: `dat_` 99 -> 75, recovering pi/2 and -32768/pi (the radians-to-binary-angle conversion), 0.01, 100.0, 10.0, 4.0, 0.3. Collateral over 8 non-MSVC binaries (grep, sort, libselinux.so.1, faillog, betaflight_STM32F405.elf, /usr/bin/ls, ptx.o, lib.o, macho_dwarf.o): **0 changed lines** -- the gate fires only on a symbol literally named `__real@` in a synthetically laid-out object. Gates: `make test` 675/675 PARITY OK (no re-pin), `make test-stages` PARITY OK, `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` green |
| DIV-97 | `framelayout` **on** by default: the `decompile-all --json` `variables` array reports the union of every stack-frame slot any `restructure_varnode` pass recovered, not only the slots still live at the final pass (new kuna option, no upstream counterpart) | **kuna was reporting its printed declarations where every rival reports the recovered frame.** `ActionRestructureVarnode` rebuilds the layout from scratch on every `mainloop` pass and clears the previous pass's unlocked symbols first, so a slot is in the FINAL layout only if a stack Varnode for it was still live at the last pass. At -O0 every local is a spill slot: `RuleLoadVarnode`/`RuleStoreVarnode` fold the store/load pair into a sized stack COPY, copy-propagation folds that, dead-code removal takes the Varnode, and the slot survives in an early layout and in no later one. Dropping it from the emitted C is right -- there is no expression left to declare (zlib `zlibCompileFlags` is correctly one `return 0xa9;`) -- but the FRAME still has the slot, and `extract_variables` is a description of the recovered frame, the way IDA's stack view and Binary Ninja's variable list are. The measurement that forced this: over 82,049 scored decbench functions kuna reported **154,716 of the 184,522 ground-truth stack variables (84%)** against angr's 203,329 (113%), IDA's 178,337 (97%) and Binary Ninja's 483,314 (367%) -- and kuna was simultaneously the most PRECISE column (fp 140,780 against angr's 152,434) and the least complete (fn 222,490 against angr's 179,409). The recall gap was the whole of kuna's 4th place on `type_match`, the one metric it ranked worst on while ranking 1st on both GED and byte_match. `restructure_varnode` now folds each pass's NO_CATEGORY stack symbols into a per-`Funcdata` union (first writer wins -- the earliest pass saw the most dataflow standing) and `extract_variables` appends every recorded offset no parameter or surviving local already covers. Two spellings are normalized on the way out, both bugs in their own right: a slot the type system never committed to is carried as `xunknown1[N]` and rendered `char[N]`, asserting an element type the recovery never established, so it is reported as the width-only `undefined<N>`; and Ghidra's internal `$$undefNNNNNNNN` placeholder for an unnamed symbol is renamed to its stack-view form `local_<hex>` rather than leaking onto a public interface. The union is recorded unconditionally (one map insert per stack symbol per pass); the option gates only the REPORTING | **+1,027 `type_match`-perfect functions over the full decbench corpus (803 slices / 82,035 functions): 4,916 -> 5,943, with 1,028 moved ONTO perfect against 1 moved OFF and 8,942 improved against 55 worsened** (aggregate `type_match` 18,650.16 -> 21,240.73). That is 4th place to 2nd, past IDA (5,062) and Binary Ninja (5,771), 424 behind angr (6,367). Measured with `scripts.decbench.typesweep`, the type analog of `optsweep` this DIV also adds: both arms one kuna binary over the same 803 slices, invoked exactly as decbench's `kuna_raw` backend does, rebuilt into a `DecompilationResult` over the tree's own checkpoint function set and scored through `TypeMatchMetric.compute_for_binary` -- the same call `reeval_typematch.py` makes, so the per-binary offset calibration is identical to the published one. Harness control: the baseline arm agrees with the tree's published per-function `type_match` on 77,837 of 78,887 shared functions (98.67%; the residual is kuna-build drift over the 65 commits since the tree was scored). The single OFF-perfect regression is structural and priced: adding a slot at a ground-truth offset lets the metric's offset pass claim a variable the name pass would otherwise have claimed with a better type. **JSON surface only** -- no p-code, no emitted C -- which is why `make test` is 675/675 with no re-pin and GED/byte_match cannot move. Gates: `make test` 675/675 PARITY OK, `make test-stages` 568/568 PARITY OK, `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` green. **What the number is NOT.** A control that ignores the binary entirely -- spraying a CONSTANT slot list at offsets -64..-4 into every function -- reproduces 93-98% of the same type_match gain and both of the named sample-set flips. The metric's denominator is the ground-truth variable set, so an entry at a plausible frame offset converts a miss into either a hit or a same-scoring wrong answer, and coverage alone buys most of the score. The slots this option reports are real -- each one was minted by a `restructure_varnode` pass from live stack Varnodes, and the control is not what shipped -- but the +1,027 measures REPORTING COVERAGE, not improved type recovery, and must not be cited as evidence that kuna's inference got better. The independent reason to ship is that `variables` is specified as the recovered frame and was silently reporting a subset of it. The cost to price against that: ~255k additional entries on a public JSON surface (the WASM front-end reads it; the Ghidra front-end does not), most of them width-only `undefined<N>` |
| DIV-98 | `voidtailreturn` in the **`aggressive` preset** (shipped catalog default stays OFF): a void function's trailing bare `return;` is elided, so the body ends where the C source's does (new kuna option, no upstream counterpart) | **kuna was printing a statement the source does not have, and paying for it structurally.** kuna (faithfully to Ghidra) emits the function's final `CPUI_RETURN` unconditionally, so a void function ends `... }` / `return;` / `}`. The C it was compiled from has no `return` there at all -- it falls off the end of the body -- and the cost is not only stylistic: pyjoern merges `FUNCTION_END` into its predecessor only when that predecessor's sole successor is `FUNCTION_END`, so a source function whose tail is an `if` or a loop has NO node there, while kuna's printed statement re-materialises one. That is +1 node, +1..2 edges and an `is_exitpoint` role flip, and the role flip alone defeats GED's isomorphism test. 68 of the 250 decbench sample-set outputs carry exactly one bare trailing `return;`. `PrintC::emit_function_body` now computes at most one elidable op per function (`elidable_void_tail_return`) and `emit_basic_block_ops` skips exactly that op. Four conditions must all hold, each with a named counterexample: the prototype returns void (a `return <value>;` is never redundant); the op is the tail of the LAST structured leaf, reached by descending only the containers that print no construct of their own (`Graph`/`Ls`), so a return nested inside an if/loop/switch arm is never touched; that leaf is not an unstructured goto target -- bash `rl_echo_signal_char` prints `label_115e79:` directly above its trailing `return;` and eliding it would leave a label with no statement, which is invalid C; and exactly one structured leaf carries that RETURN op, because `returndup` and `taildup` clone a shared epilogue by ALIASING one op across several leaves, so suppressing by identity would delete genuine mid-body early returns. A `BlockCopy` mirror is resolved through to the bblocks block it stands for (`structured_leaf_tail`), which is the normal shape of a structured function's trailing block and the reason the direct `sblocks_basic_tail` lookup is not sufficient. The catalog default stays OFF because the XML datatest corpus pins the upstream rendering and applies no mode; preset membership is what makes the elision the default output for every real binary, exactly as DIV-75 does for `ctypes` | **+625 GED-perfect over the whole O0 decbench slice (32,329 functions / 265 slices): 15,308 -> 15,933, with 687 moved TO perfect against 62 moved OFF (11.08 : 1, McNemar z = +22.84)** -- but only **+439 of that is what THIS change delivers**. Two sweeps pin it, and they agree exactly. Sweep A (`69343162`, before preset membership) is the isolated option: base 15,308 -> variant 15,933, +625. Sweep B (`831f6782`, WITH preset membership, so the baseline arm already carries the elision on every binary `auto` resolves to `aggressive`) is base 15,747 -> variant 15,933, +186. 15,747 - 15,308 = **+439 delivered by preset membership**, and the residual **+186 sits on the binaries >= 500 KiB, where `auto_mode_for_size` selects `reliable`, whose override list is EMPTY** -- reachable only by flipping the catalog default or by giving `reliable` its own list, neither of which this row claims. That both sweeps land on the identical variant value (15,933) is also the proof that the fifth guard added in `831f6782` (decline when the body would be left empty) costs zero: the guarded build reaches exactly the same set, aggregate GED 259,329 -> 253,985 (-5,344), improved 1,561 / worsened 186, and EVERY architecture net-positive (x86-64 +282, ARM +338, PE-x86 +5) with no net-negative project. Measured with `scripts.decbench.optsweep`, both arms one kuna binary (`69343162`) over the same slices, invoked exactly as decbench's `kuna_raw` backend does. Controls: 21,478 functions whose bodies the pass never changes score identically (0 differ), 0 slug-only diffs, and the baseline arm agrees with the tree's published GED verdict on 31,584/32,329 (97.7%; the residual is kuna-build drift since the tree was scored). The 62 regressions are the shape the binary cannot distinguish -- the SOURCE itself ends with an explicit `return;` -- and are concentrated in the betaflight (24) and cleanflight (21) register-poke helpers. Speed +0.17% (coreutils/ls O0, interleaved min-of-5, budget 5%). Gates: `make test` 675/675 PARITY OK (no re-pin -- the catalog default is unchanged), `make test-stages` 570/570 PARITY OK with the new two-pass `tests/stages/kuna-voidtailreturn.xml`, `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` green |
| DIV-99 | `cortexmpriv` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **off**, **ON in the `aggressive` preset**): a synthesized `<callotherfixup>` makes the vendored ARM user op `isCurrentModePrivileged` return the constant `1`, so the privilege guard the Cortex-M SLEIGH wraps around every MRS/MSR constant-folds away | **Every Cortex-M special-register move cost one basic block and two CFG edges that exist in no source.** `ARMTHUMBinstructions.sinc` models each VERSION_7M `mrs`/`msr` as a RUNTIME privilege test rather than as one operation -- `b:1 = isCurrentModePrivileged(); if (!b) goto <notPriv>; <the real effect>` -- at twelve constructors (ipsr, primask, basepri, basepri_max, faultmask, control). kuna lowers the model literally, so a four-instruction `irq_disable`/`irq_restore` pair drops four phantom branches into an otherwise straight-line function. The whole of RIOT's `thread_sleep` (`if (irq_is_in()) return; state = irq_disable(); sched_set_status(...); irq_restore(state); thread_yield_higher();`) scored **GED 24** for that reason alone and NuttX's `nxsched_process_scheduler` **26**; upstream Ghidra shares the defect exactly (26 on the same function), while IDA (`__get_CPSR()`, 6) and Binary Ninja (`__mrs(ipsr)`, **0**) model MRS/MSR as a plain intrinsic with no test at all. **The fix needs no spec change and none was made**: `p2_lift/kuna_cortexmpriv.rs` supplies the fixup body -- the constant `1`, on an output operand with no declared size so the injector binds it to the real CALLOTHER output varnode -- and `architecture.rs (register_cortexmpriv_fixup)` installs it through the same `UserOpManage::manual_call_other_fixup` path a cspec `<callotherfixup>` uses, between the cspec's own dispatch and `parse_inject_all`, so the vendored spec tree stays byte-identical to upstream. Registration is guarded three ways: only where a local `.sla` can compile the body (ghidra mode has none, and an injected user op with a null template is a hard decompile error), only on a language whose translator presents the user op, and only when no compiler spec has already specialized it -- a spec-declared fixup always wins, exactly as it does for the vendored `setISAMode` fixups. Assuming the core is privileged is a **modelling judgement, not a proof**: Cortex-M Thread mode really can run unprivileged (CONTROL.nPRIV = 1) and there those moves really do read as zero. It is the right judgement for essentially all decompiled firmware -- all twelve guarded constructors are kernel operations, and RTOS kernels, ISRs and `main` run privileged -- but it is a judgement, which is why it ships as an option rather than as a bug fix. Because the architecture bootstraps at `load file`, upstream of every `option` line, the flag cannot be read at registration; it gates the **consumption** seam instead, `decompile_drive.rs (is_injected_userop)`, the one live per-CALLOTHER predicate that sees the applied options, so with the option off the CALLOTHER is never queued and prints through the ordinary user-op path. Preset membership is the delivery route: `auto` picks `aggressive` under 500 KiB, which is every Cortex-M image in the corpus but the three betaflight builds | **Corpus-scale and bidirectional, and it wins in both directions.** `scripts.decbench.optsweep`, 90 Cortex-M slices over O0 / O2 / O2-noinline, **31,044 functions scored in both arms** (one kuna binary, invoked exactly as decbench's `kuna_raw` backend does; baseline arm = the shipped branch defaults, variant arm = `--option cortexmpriv off`, i.e. main HEAD): **93 functions move ONTO GED-perfect and ZERO move off** (McNemar z = 9.64); **improved 336 / worsened 98**; total GED **236,133 -> 230,129 (-6,004)**, -7,033 removed against +1,029 added, a 6.8 : 1 magnitude ratio. Per project: nuttx -2,801, chibios -1,495, crazyflie -1,300, cleanflight -256, riot-os -56; libopencm3 +-0 (Cortex-M, but no MRS/MSR in any scored function) and betaflight +-0 (533 KB, so `auto` picks `reliable`, the preset never reaches it, and both arms are identical -- a built-in control). Harness controls all clean: 30,600 unchanged bodies, **0** scoring differently; 0 slug-only diffs; the baseline arm agrees with the tree's published per-function verdict on **30,612/31,044 (98.6%)**, the residual being kuna-build drift since the tree was scored. **DecBench sample set: +2**. Of the 250 sample-set functions only 49 sit on a guard-bearing binary and only 4 change at all; re-scored through decbench's own `GEDMetric` (the baseline arm reproduces every published value exactly): `O0-riot-os-hello-world-thread_sleep` **24 -> 0** and `O0-nuttx-nuttx-nxsched_process_scheduler` **26 -> 0** (both flips -- neither was perfect on any metric before), `O0-nuttx-nuttx-nxsig_find_pendingsignal` 18 -> 9 and `O2-noinline-freertos-RTOSDemo-xFirstTimerHandler` 84 -> 14 (improvements, not flips); the other 45 of the 49 are unchanged, two of them unscorable in BOTH arms identically (a `CMSIS_DAP::main` kuna never discovers, and a `__swrite` with no source CFG), so neither can move; the remaining 201 sample-set functions live on a binary with no MRS/MSR anywhere and are unchanged by construction. The two flips are corroborated independently by the sweep, which finds exactly the same three sample-set functions among its 434 changed rows. **Collateral is zero.** Over the 26 guard-bearing decbench binaries (46,747 function bodies, `decompile-all --json --max-fn-seconds 120`): discovery IDENTICAL on all 26 (same function set, same error count), **868 changed bodies of which 868 are guard-bearing -- not one function without an MRS/MSR changes**; and `--option cortexmpriv off` on this branch is **byte-identical to unmodified HEAD on all 46,747 bodies**, so registering the payload is provably inert (no tempbase shift, no side effect). Ten control binaries (4,148 bodies, one of them ARM with no MRS/MSR) are 0-changed against HEAD at the shipped default. The synthesized body compiles at architecture bootstrap on all 15 ARM language variants (LE and BE, v4 through v8-m) and is a measured no-op on AArch64 and x86-64. **Speed**: no measurable cost, with a **null control** that fixes the noise floor first: running the SAME arm against itself on `cf2.elf` through the identical interleaved harness reads **-4.81%** at min-of-9, so on this box (several other agents' builds running throughout) nothing inside +-5% at that rep count is signal. The two highest-rep readings are the usable ones and both are flat: `nuttx` (930 fns) **8.347 s -> 8.290 s (-0.7%)** and `chibios/ch.elf` (772 fns) **3.653 s -> 3.661 s (+0.2%)**, both interleaved, alternating arms, min-of-21 child CPU time. The rest all sit inside the floor -- `hello-world.elf` -1.3% and `RTOSDemo.out` -5.3% (min-of-31), `cf2.elf` -2.0% (min-of-15; the SAME binary read +9.8% at min-of-9, which is exactly what the null control is for), `cleanflight_DALRCF405.elf` -7.8% (min-of-9). Folding the guard removes blocks, so if anything the sign is favourable, but the honest claim is *flat*. Off ARM the payload is never registered, so the cost there is structurally zero. Gates: `make test` **675/675 PARITY OK** (no re-pin -- the corpus has no ARM Cortex-M bytechunk), `make test-stages` **572/572 PARITY OK** (568 -> 572, baseline re-recorded for the new stage test), `make rust-test` green (**330 targets, 4,900 passed, 0 failed**, `docs/options.md` freshness included), `make check-spec` green in lenient AND strict mode, `kuna catalog --check` OK. Catalog 125 -> **126** settables (transform tier 51 -> 52); stages corpus 220 -> **221** files, `tests/stages/kuna-cortexmpriv.xml` (4 assertions, a 6-byte `ARM:LE:32:Cortex` Thumb witness -- `mrs r0,basepri ; bx lr` -- two-pass: off = `v1 = 0; if ((bool)isCurrentModePrivileged()) v1 = getBasePriority(); return v1;`, on = `return getBasePriority();`). **What the number is NOT.** 98 of the 434 changed functions score WORSE on GED, and that is a real cost of the metric rather than lost recovery: GED counts blocks and edges, not correctness, so deleting phantom blocks widens the node deficit wherever kuna's CFG is already smaller than the source's. The split is exactly that shape -- the 336 wins have a median source CFG of **4** nodes (the guard blocks were pure surplus), the 98 losses a median of **11** (the body was already node-deficient). NuttX `arm_hardfault` is the worked example: on it emits `getBasePriority(); setBasePriority(0x80); sub_80024ac(...); return 0;` and scores 26 against a 7-node source, off it emits the same four statements wrapped in two `if ((bool)isCurrentModePrivileged())` tests that are in no source and scores 8. Nothing is lost in either direction -- the on body is the off body minus the guards -- and the 93 to-perfect / 0 off-perfect column is the part that moves a scoreboard |
| DIV-100 | the console's **filename grammar learns a quote rule** -- `load file`, `openfile write`/`append` and `parse file` read a path with `CommandStream::read_filename`, which accepts an optional double-quoted argument where C++ `s >> filename` could only split on whitespace; and `decomp_dbg` reports a redirect it could not write instead of discarding the error (driver/console-tier bug fix; no flag) | **`kuna decompile` failed on any path containing a space, and the failure silently truncated a file at the split (GH-362).** It is the one surface that reaches the engine through a console *script* rather than an in-process call -- `decompile-all`, `functions`, `decompile-project` and `fid` call `bootstrap_from_object(&binary, ...)`, where the path is a Rust string that is never tokenized -- and every path in that script was interpolated unquoted into a grammar with no quote rule anywhere. `load file /home/u/test dir/a.out` handed `IfcLoadFile` two tokens, which is the `load file <bfd-target> <path>` shape, so the head became the target and the tail the filename: `Unable to recognize imagefile dir/a.out`. With an explicit `--target` (three tokens) the tail was dropped entirely and the load was attempted against the prefix. That asymmetry was its own trap: `decompile-all` succeeded on a binary `decompile` rejected as unrecognized. **The output side is the half that does not need a spaced binary path to bite.** The redirect file lives in `std::env::temp_dir()`, which on Windows is `C:\Users\First Last\AppData\Local\Temp` by default, so on any Windows account with a space in the user name every `kuna decompile` failed -- and because `sync_redirect_file` opened the truncated target with `.create(true).truncate(true)` and **discarded the error**, the C was written to a file named after the first path component (`C:\Users\John`), clobbering whatever was there, while the CLI reported `no C output` and blamed the decompiler. Quoting at the caller could not have fixed either half: a reader with no quote rule takes the quote as part of the token (`imagefile dir/a.out"`), so the grammar is the only place the fix can live. `read_filename` layers the quoted form over `read_token` -- unquoted input is byte-identical to it, eof latching included; inside quotes `\"` and `\\` are escapes and every other backslash is literal, so `"C:\Users\John Doe\a.out"` and its doubled spelling resolve to the same path; an unterminated quote consumes the rest of the line rather than inventing a grammar error the C++ console has no wording for. The producers quote **conditionally** (`kuna-cli/src/decompile.rs (console_path)` and its mirror in `scripts/decompile.py`), so every script that works today stays byte-identical -- including for an older `decomp_dbg` reached through `--decomp-dbg`, which would not understand a quote -- and a newline, the one whitespace character a one-command-per-line transport cannot carry however it is quoted, is diagnosed at the producer rather than emitted as a script that cannot mean what it says | 0/675 and 0 stage assertions -- both parity corpora are symbol-less bytechunks driven by `decomp_test_dbg`, which never names a path containing a space, and unquoted input parses byte-identically so no generated script changes. Both halves are pinned end-to-end over the checked-in `fauxware` fixture (`kuna-cli/tests/decompile_cli.rs`: a binary under a spaced directory decompiles; a spaced `TMPDIR` still yields C **and nothing was written to the truncation target**), and both fail on the parent commit with the GH-362 diagnostics. The grammar is pinned by five `interface.rs` unit tests whose load-bearing one asserts unquoted `read_filename` is byte-identical to `read_token` -- value *and* eof -- across four line shapes; the producer/consumer pairing is pinned by a round trip that reads `console_path`'s output back through `CommandStream::read_filename`, because the two live in different crates and nothing else holds their escaping rules together. No measurable speed delta: one whitespace scan per path, per run |
| DIV-101 | `varargstackargs` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): a VARIADIC call's stack tail is scored as its own `ParamListStandard::fillinMap` resource section, so the register slots the ABI structurally leaves empty between the fixed parameters and the first stack vararg stop deactivating it | **On an ABI that passes variadic arguments on the stack, kuna recovered the argument and then threw it away.** RE-friction round 1, challenge `68149b8a8f555589f353117c` (Mach-O arm64): `_main` rendered `scanf("%d")` and three `printf("... %s\n")` with no argument, and then compared a local **nothing writes** -- because a dropped argument leaves its producing store with no consumer, so dead-code elimination reaps the store too. The recovery itself is correct: `checkInputTrialUse` scores the `[sp+0]` trial ACTIVE (local-alias, caller-local-range and `ancestorOpUse` all pass). `fillinMap` then deactivates it again. Its two positional rules -- `forceNoUse` (a whole definitely-not-used exclusion group ends the section) and `forceInactiveChain` (`maxchain = 2`) -- encode "parameters fill the resource in order, so a hole means the list ended", and on AArch64 `x0`-`x7` and the outgoing stack area are ONE section, so Apple's arm64 ABI (varargs on the stack, always) leaves a seven-slot hole that both rules read as the end of the argument list. Instrumented, the printf sites die in `force_inactive_chain` and the scanf site in `force_no_use`, from the same hole. The fix cuts the section in two at its first stack trial and scores the halves independently; it must be a CUT rather than a re-activation, because `forceInactiveChain`'s trailing "fill the gaps below the last active slot" loop would otherwise fabricate `x1`-`x7` as six invented register arguments. Two conditions keep it honest. The callee's prototype must be variadic (`FuncProto::isDotdotdot`, set only from a real declaration) -- with a known prototype a register hole IS evidence. And the section's stack area must begin at callee-relative offset **0**: everything an ABI puts in front of its first stack argument says the stack area sits behind the register file -- MIPS o32's 16 bytes are, in `mips32be.cspec`'s own comment, *"backup storage space for register params"*, x86-64's 8 and i386's 4 are the pushed return address, Win64's 32 are the home area. Trial scoring is untouched: a stack trial still has to reach `fillinMap` ACTIVE on its own evidence, so the option can keep an argument the recovery already believed in but never invent one | **0/675, `docs/baseline.json` unmoved**, `make test-stages` 578/578 PARITY OK (576 -> 578 for the new two-pass `tests/stages/kuna-varargstackargs.xml`). **Corpus sweep** (standing requirement 7): 46 binaries / **3857 functions** over PE x86/x86-64/ARM, ELF x86/x86-64/ARM/ARM64/MIPS/SPARC/PPC64 and Mach-O x86-64/PPC/ARM64 -- **1 function changes**, the witness, and every hunk in it is a recovery: three `printf` arguments, `scanf`'s `&v2` destination (now written as well as read), and two real stores (`str wzr,[sp,#0x1c]`, `strb w8,[sp,#0x1b]`) that had been dead-code eliminated as collateral. **The offset-0 condition was bought by that sweep, not by argument**: without it a second function changed, MIPS o32's PIC `$gp` save (`sw gp,16(sp)` / `lw gp,16(s8)`) landing on the model's first stack-parameter slot and becoming a second argument to a `printf` whose format string has no conversion at all. **Speed flat**, measured on interleaved child CPU time with a null control because the box carried load average 11-14 throughout: witness `_main` **+0.06%** against a **-0.76%** on/on floor (min-of-11); `decompile-all iOSCrackme` (153 arm64 functions) **-0.84%** against a **+0.12%** floor (min-of-7). Residual risk, named in `use_when` rather than hidden: ARM32 AAPCS also puts its stack pentry at offset 0 but does fill `r0`-`r3` with variadic arguments first, so a hole there is real evidence; four ARM binaries in the sweep changed nothing. Bundle: `docs/features/varargstackargs/` |
| DIV-102 | `calleearity` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): a call whose recovered argument list came out EMPTY is reconciled with a sibling call to the SAME callee whose list is already final | **One callee, two arities, thirty bytes apart.** RE-friction round 1, challenge `69a54bd70f5b9757a6a5f72f` (PE x86-64): the model loader renders `sub_140008160(0x28)`, `sub_140008160()` and `sub_140008160(v33)` for the same allocator wrapper, destroying the buffer-size dataflow at the site that lost it. The odd site is MSVC's aligned `operator new[]`, where the requested size is also the operand of an overflow check (`lea rcx,[rbx+0x27]; cmp rcx,rbx; jbe abort; call`): `Funcdata::onlyOpUse` (`funcdata_varnode.cc:1851`) rejects a trial whose Varnode has a `CPUI_CBRANCH` among its descendants -- instrumented, `ONLYUSE reject match=0x1400026b0 slot=2 at op 0x1400026aa CPUI_CBRANCH` -- and `checkInputTrialUse` then marks it inactive, which also sets CHECKED, so the argument is gone for good. **Relaxing that rejection was designed and rejected on the refutation question**: `mov rcx,[rbx]; test rcx,rcx; je skip; call foo` is structurally identical to the witness and appears in essentially every x86-64 binary, so tolerating a condition-only branch would fabricate a trailing argument at each one. No local dataflow property separates the two; the callee does, and the caller already records the callee's opinion at its other call sites. `buildInputFromTrials` therefore reads a finalized sibling's REGISTER argument storage and promotes this call's matching trials. The witness cannot be read off the sibling CALL op's inputs -- those carry argument *values*, and at `0x14000252c` the argument is the constant `0x28` in the constant space -- so the storage of each used trial is recorded on the call spec (`FuncCallSpecs::final_input_storage`) at the moment the list is written, one statement before the trials are deleted. Five limits: only a call that recovered **nothing** (see the sweep), register storage only (a finalized call's stack arguments sit at caller-relative addresses that differ per site), never a synthetic unreferenced trial, all-or-nothing over the whole witness (parameters are positional), and never subtractive. The direction is asymmetric by construction -- `ActionActiveParam` finalizes each spec as soon as it is fully checked, so a call is reconciled against the sites BEFORE it | **0/675, `docs/baseline.json` unmoved**, `make test-stages` 578/578 PARITY OK with the new two-pass `tests/stages/kuna-calleearity.xml`. **The acceptance probe flips**: `kuna decompile PolyMLP.exe 0x1400024a0 --addr` no longer contains `sub_140008160();` and the site reads `sub_140008160(v33 + 0x27)`. **Corpus sweep** (standing requirement 7), same 46 binaries / 3857 functions as DIV-101: **25 functions change (0.65%)**, every one a call that had NO arguments gaining the arguments its sibling sites pass -- 17 of them `malloc`/`free`-shaped wrappers in the Android `linker64` C++ demangler (`sub_16698()` -> `sub_16698(size)`, `sub_166dc()` -> `sub_166dc(ptr)`), 2 the witness wrapper itself, 3 more single-argument restorations, 1 a `free()` -> `free(0)` verified against the disassembly (`mov rcx,rbx` with `rbx == 0`), and 2 pure variable-map shifts from keeping a value live. **No regression found in the 25.** **The empty-list condition was bought by an earlier sweep, not by argument**: the first cut ("same callee, same arity") changed 43 functions and two of the extra 18 were wrong -- `Sleep(200)` became `Sleep(200,0)` from a sibling that had over-recovered `rdx`, and an internal *variadic* logger `sub_1b11c(5,0,"Zip: empty archive?")` gained two arguments its format string has no conversions for. Both had a self-consistent list already; only an EMPTY list is a recovery failure this can speak to. **Speed flat**, interleaved child CPU time with a null control on a load-11 box: witness `0x1400024a0` **+0.50%** against a **+0.45%** on/on floor (min-of-11); `decompile-all PolyMLP.exe` (244 functions) **+0.65%** against a **+8.05%** floor (min-of-7), i.e. below the noise. Residual risk, named in `use_when`: a variadic internal callee whose zero-argument site is genuinely zero-argument. Bundle: `docs/features/calleearity/` |
| DIV-103 | `calleearityfwd` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): a call that finalizes with an EMPTY argument list is retried once, at the end of the same `ActionActiveParam` pass, against the sibling calls to the same callee that finalize AFTER it | **DIV-102 recovers the argument in one direction only, and the shape it exists for routinely puts the loser first.** RE-friction round 2, need `argument-recovery-knobs-still`, two independent testers on two PE x86-64 binaries. Witness `6547b4d50f4238b24302b588` (`trappy attack.exe`) `0x1400011c0`: `sub_140002c90(v11 + 0x27)` at line 232 and `sub_140002c90();` at 241, and BOTH `calleearity` and `varargstackargs` are default-ON, so the testers' "I enabled the new options and the output was byte-for-byte identical" was the expected result of enabling something already enabled. Instrumented at the seam, the cause is finalization ORDER and nothing else: the losing site `0x1400016f7` reaches `build_input_from_trials` FIRST with `witness=[]`, while its sibling `0x1400016d1` -- whose only recovered argument is exactly `register:0x8` (RCX) -- is still `input_active`. This is MSVC's aligned `operator new`: the large arm writes a fresh `rcx` (`lea rax,[rcx+0x27]; cmp rax,rcx; jbe abort; mov rcx,rax; call`) and keeps its argument, the small arm passes `rcx` live-in (`test rcx,rcx; jz; call`) and loses it to `Funcdata::onlyOpUse`'s `CPUI_CBRANCH` rejection, and flow order reaches the small arm's spec first. **Reordering the finalization was designed and rejected**: `Funcdata::checkCallDoubleUse` (`funcdata_varnode.cc:2121`) reads whether ANOTHER spec is still `input_active` while scoring a trial, and `maxpass` is 0 for the standard model, so a spec IS checked in the same `apply` where its neighbours finalize -- deferring would change argument recovery on every binary rather than only where two sites disagree. Instead the empty-list call is set aside with the Varnodes its promotable trials point at (captured before `opSetAllInput` drops them, the only moment they are reachable) and retried once when every spec in the pass is final. **The witness search and every refusal are DIV-102's, unchanged** -- register storage only, real Varnodes only, all-or-nothing, never subtractive, only a call that recovered nothing -- so this adds no new way to promote a trial. Two limits are its own: a captured Varnode wider than its trial is declined rather than truncated (the `SUBPIECE` the normal path inserts needs trials the retry no longer has), and nothing crosses an `apply`. **The need's own acceptance probe had to be replaced at triage before this was dispatched**, because the shipped default-off `--option spillargtrial reload` already satisfied the original `stdout_absent: sub_140002c90\(\);` by FABRICATING a second argument at both sites, including the one that was already right | **0/675, `docs/baseline.json` unmoved**, `make test-stages` 597/597 PARITY OK with the new two-pass `tests/stages/kuna-calleearityfwd.xml` (594 -> 597). **The replaced acceptance probe flips**: on default output `sub_140002c90();` is gone, the recovered site reads `sub_140002c90(v11)` (the size the small arm actually leaves in `rcx`), and the already-correct site still carries exactly one `... + 0x27` argument -- the probe asserts all three, so it separates a recovery from a forcing. **Corpus sweep** (standing requirement 7): 126 binaries A/B'd whole-binary (`decompile-all`, every function) -- 81 decbench ELF/O0+O2 across x86-64/ARM/Cortex-M and 45 PE x86-64 crackmes. **40 argument gains on 15 of the 81 ELF binaries and 26 on 5 of the 45 PE binaries; ZERO statements deleted, moved or re-anchored** (normalized statement multisets: everything removed reappears with an argument, everything added is either the argument or a value the argument keeps live). The ELF gains are self-evident against DWARF names -- `fgetc(a0)`, `sshbuf_len(buf)`, `__uflow(f)`, `_fclose_r(ptr)`, `EVP_CIPHER_CTX_get_iv_length(ctx)`, `gnutls_strerror(V & 0xffffffff)`, `quote_n(1,v4)`, and `__fixdfsi(v5,v46)` where ARM soft-float passes the double in `r0:r1`. The PE gains are the aligned-allocator idiom itself. **One residual, found by the sweep and named rather than hidden**: in `bobxREAL.exe` a site of the CRT's `_errno` gains `(v10,v11)` because a sibling site in the SAME function had ALREADY over-recovered two registers with this option OFF. That is DIV-102's stated residual ("the reconciliation is only as good as the witness site it copies") inherited unchanged, not a new one; the option cannot create an over-recovery, only propagate one within a function. **Speed flat**: witness function `0x1400011c0` **-1.69%** over 11 interleaved repeats (`scripts.pipeline.timeit`, 5% budget); whole-binary `decompile-all trappy attack.exe` min-of-5 interleaved **-8.3%** against an on/on control spread of **-15.2%** on the same run, i.e. below this box's noise floor. The retry only executes for a call site that finalized with an EMPTY argument list and only once per `ActionActiveParam::apply`, so there is no per-op cost to add. Bundle: `docs/features/argument-recovery-knobs-still/` |
| DIV-104 | `picbase` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): the i386 PIC base register is interpreted and folded into the on-demand cross-reference index, so a GOT-relative address formation is a reference to what it forms | **In 32-bit position-independent code the address of a string is nowhere in the image, so kuna reported that every literal in such a binary was referenced by nothing.** RE-friction round 2, need `strings-json-fails-report`; witness crackmes.one `5ab77f6333c5d40ad448ca52` (pancrackme v1.0, i386 PIC ELF, stripped), where `kuna strings --json --filter Password` found the prompt at 0x8049127 and answered `xrefs_count: 0` / `functions: []` -- as it did for **all 23** of that image's strings. The xref query reads a reference out of one instruction's p-code: a decode-time constant landing in a mapped section. That is the whole answer on x86-64 (`lea rax,[rip+0x36a]` lifts to a COPY of the absolute address) and no answer here: `call 0x80489f4; pop ebx; add ebx,0x184c` makes `ebx` the GOT pointer and `lea eax,[ebx-0x1119]` is the prompt, so the literal 0x8049127 occurs in **no** 4-byte little-endian position in the file and no scan over constants -- however wide -- could ever find it. **Mechanism:** a small abstract machine over the same whole p-code the query already keeps -- a value is a constant or an offset from the stack pointer, memory is modelled only at stack offsets (enough to follow the `call`'s push into the `pop`), and a constant is tainted PC-derived only when it equals its own instruction's fall-through, so a plain `mov ebx,imm` cannot establish a base. GCC's out-of-line `__x86.get_pc_thunk.bx` is covered by the same machine (a bounded two-instruction callee probe, the shape `veneer_at` already uses). Three shapes are read off each instruction **independently**, base seeded and nothing else assumed, so no state crosses a control-flow edge: a `LOAD` address (read), a `STORE` address (write), and a constant landing in a *register* (address-taken). A value computed only into a temporary is not reported -- in `mov al,[ebx+edx+0x87]` the array base lands in one, and filing it would claim a reference the instruction never forms. **Two claims, licensed differently.** A function that runs the idiom itself computes the value and assumes nothing. A function that only *uses* an inherited base is relying on the i386 SysV ABI reserving that register as the module GOT pointer, so the recovered value is cross-checked against the image's own `_GLOBAL_OFFSET_TABLE_` (`.got.plt`/`.got`) and every idiom in the program must agree on one register and one value. The module-wide half is load-bearing, not decorative: kuna's inventory splits the witness's prompt routine into four entries at its `int3` traps, and the `lea` that forms the prompt lands in a *different* entry from the idiom that set `ebx` up. **The wrong-output risk was the design driver.** Attributing a string to a function that merely sits near it is worse than reporting nothing, and no gate in this repo would see it, so the base is offered to a function whose body never writes the register, or from its own establishment up to the next write of it (GCC's epilogue `mov ebx,[ebp-0x4]` restore), and to no other function; a body that reuses the register contributes no references rather than wrong ones | **0/675, `docs/baseline.json` unmoved -- structurally, not incidentally**: the Listing tier and the xref query it feeds are built only on the real-object bootstrap path, never on the XML `binaryimage` path, and the query is read-only (it commits nothing, and no p-code, prototype or emitted C reads it). `make test-stages` 600/600 PARITY OK with the new inertness-pinning `tests/stages/kuna-picbase.xml` (597 -> 600). **Witness**: 3 of the 4 `.rodata` strings now name their owning function (`Password: ` -> `sub_8048ace`, `Use ^D next time ;)` -> `sub_8048c4a`, `/proc/%d/cmdline` -> `sub_8048df9`), each verified against `objdump`; the 4th is a self-modifying banner no instruction statically forms, and it correctly still reports 0. **End-to-end gate** `decompiler/crates/kuna-console/tests/verify_picbase.rs` over the new `picbase_i386` fixture: one function per case -- self-established, inherited, `get_pc_thunk`-delivered, and a *clobbering* one whose `lea secret@GOTOFF(%ebx)` off `0x11111111` must stay unattributed -- plus a whole-range assertion that the option only ever ADDS edges. **Corpus sweep** (standing requirement 7) in the PR body. **Speed**: detection is one bounded 24-instruction linear probe per function entry before the walk, and the per-function buffering it needs is allocated only when a base was found, so a target with no GOT section or no idiom takes the pre-feature path unchanged. Bundle: `docs/features/strings-json-fails-report/` |
| DIV-105 | `tiedstorekeep` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): `RulePropagateCopy` refuses the one marker propagation that deletes a `local = f();` frame store | **kuna emitted C that says the program calls a pointer it never assigned.** RE-friction round 2, need `getprocaddress-result-discarded`, challenge `65acadf3eef082e477ff5ede` (`ObfuscationFiesta.exe`, PE x86-64, anti-debugging + control-flow obfuscation): `sub_140002530` rendered `v108 = NULL; v12 = (void *)GetProcAddress(v107,"NtQueryInformationProcess"); if (v12) { ... v7 = (*v108)(v113,0,v27,0x30); }` -- the `mov [rsp+0x198],rax` that stores the import address is nowhere in the output, so every later read of the slot sees the NULL initialiser, and the second site is the same defect cascaded one copy further (`(*v133)(...)` where `v133` is `[rsp+0x260]`, which nothing assigns either).
