The dataflow underneath is correct -- the CALLIND target really is the `GetProcAddress` result -- which is what makes it worse than a wrong-looking expression: an analyst reads a null-pointer call and concludes the import is never used, and the anti-debugging logic the whole challenge turns on is unreadable. **Mechanism** (P3, `RulePropagateCopy`, `ruleaction.cc:3943`): the store lowers to an address-tied `stackvn = COPY(RAX)`, and the rule rewrites each reader of `stackvn` to read `RAX` instead. Ordinary readers are pure gain. A MARKER reader is not -- an `INDIRECT` across the following `GetCurrentProcess` call, or a `MULTIEQUAL` at a join, never prints -- so once the marker has swallowed the last remaining reader the `COPY` has no descendants and dies to dead-code elimination. `Merge` normally hides this by merging RAX's HighVariable into the tied location's; here that merge is DECLINED (intersecting covers, because RAX carries a dozen other live call returns in this function) and nothing repairs it. Upstream already declines the propagation for an `addrforce` output ("don't propagate if we are keeping the COPY anyway") but `addrforce` is never set on an ordinary frame store. ON widens that refusal by exactly one case: a marker is about to consume the LAST reader of a non-`persist` address-tied `COPY` whose input is not itself address-tied and whose value comes from a call (`CALL`/`CALLIND`/`CALLOTHER`, or the `INDIRECT` that carries the return register before `ActionActiveReturn` promotes it). The tester's filed diagnosis ("stack-variable recovery split one logical local") and the captain's alternative ("the second site's `v133` is a mis-attributed stack offset") were both **refuted**: `v133` is `[rsp+0x260]`, a real slot the disassembly writes at `0x140002bab`, dropped by the same mechanism one link down the copy chain, and one predicate fixes both sites | **0/675, `docs/baseline.json` unmoved**; `make test-stages` PARITY OK, unchanged. **The acceptance probe `a-f2df446f39d5` flips**: both sites now read `v104 = (void *)GetProcAddress(...)` / `v124 = v104;` / `v95 = (*v104)(v109,0,v22,0x30)` and `v94 = (void *)GetProcAddress(...)` / `v129 = v94;` / `(*v129)(...)`. **Both narrowing clauses were bought by measurement, not by argument.** Without the from-a-call clause the brake also keeps ordinary arithmetic stores alive, which turns the if/else diamonds of `union_datatype` and `condconst` into `?:` ternaries and degrades union field resolution (`res = ptr->b->bIntField` becomes `(int4)ptr->a->aFloatField`): **7/675 assertions move**. Letting it fire on `persist` globals -- which already have heritage's RETURN-COPY keeping their last store printed, and whose propagation `copytrim` exists to pin -- made `copytrim` run for **minutes instead of 0.16 s**. **Corpus sweep** (standing requirement 7): 7 binaries A/B'd whole-binary (`decompile-all`) over ELF x86-64/i386/ARM and PE x86-64. 3 byte-identical (`fauxware`, `i386/all`, `armel/aes`); `libgcc_s.so.1` statement-multiset identical (5 hunks, pure reordering); `mv_-O2` 4 of 390 functions change -- declaration order, 7 copy-shadow `vA = vB;` statements collapsing where the kept store makes them redundant, one dereference hoisted into a temp, and a net **+6 DWARF source names** (`rpl_fts_open`'s `p` appears at 7 more sites while `backupfile_internal`'s `name` moves from a register to a stack local); `ALLSTAR_9base_dd` 1 function, adding `(long)`/`(unsigned long)`/`(double)` casts. **One degradation, named rather than hidden**: in that `ALLSTAR_9base_dd` function (`sub_406290`, a dtoa-shaped slot type-punned between `double` and `unsigned long`) the constant `v32 = 0xfffffff;` re-renders as `v32 = 1.32624736441264e-315;` -- the same bit pattern spelled as a float literal. **Speed -0.13%** (`scripts.pipeline.timeit`, witness function, median of 5, 5% budget). **No `tests/stages/` testcase**: the defect is import-table-gated and the XML `binaryimage` format has no PE import table -- three fixtures were built and measured (a synthetic SysV construct, a 1 KB carve of the witness at its real addresses with `.rdata` mapped, and the whole 6 KB function likewise) and NONE reproduce it, because with no prototypes the calls do not build the RAX cover that makes `Merge` decline. The acceptance probe is likewise not vendorable into `tests/cli/` (`binary_source: dataset`; CI has no dataset). Bundle: `docs/features/getprocaddress-result-discarded/` |
| DIV-106 | `overlapbranch` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): a conditional branch whose own target lies strictly inside its fall-through instruction truncates that fall-through instead of letting it swallow the target | **kuna handed back an anti-disassembly binary's own lie.** RE-friction round 2, need `overlapping-anti-disassembly-sequence`, challenge `5ab77f6333c5d40ad448ca52` (`pcme0`, i386 ELF, stripped). The recursive password checker at `0x804881c` rendered as `v2 = (char *)sub_b10cdc7(); *v4 = *v4 | (unsigned char)v3; dat_d98ddd00 += (char)v2;` with two mangled arguments (`unsigned int a0, unsigned long long a1`) and an unreadable comparison -- a call to an address that is not in the image, writes through pointers nothing assigns, and a global that does not exist. Every one of those is an artefact of operand bytes read as opcodes. The binary carries the classic overlap at `0x8048835`: `JNZ 0x8048838` immediately followed, at `0x8048837`, by a junk `e8` lead byte, so the fall-through decodes as `CALL 0xb10cdc7` (5 bytes) and swallows the branch target that sits one byte inside it. Decoding from `0x8048838` instead gives the real stream, `MOV EAX,[EBP+0xc]; ADD EAX,[EBP+0x8]; MOVSX EDX,[EAX]`, which carries the input-byte XOR and the table comparison the whole challenge turns on. **Mechanism** (P2, `flow.rs (FlowInfo::process_instruction)`): a conditional branch pushes its target and then its fall-through, and `addrlist` is a stack, so the fall-through is always decoded FIRST and wins by arrival order. `set_fallthru_bound` does detect the clash -- it calls `reinterpreted` -- but only on the next visit, when the losing stream and everything downstream of it are already built, which is why the upstream detection has never been able to do anything but record the fact. ON adds the check at the one point where it is still cheap: right after the fall-through instruction is decoded, against a target that is still pending. **The ownership policy, stated:** the branch target wins, because a branch target is an address the program *encodes* while a fall-through is only ever inferred from the previous instruction's length; and because two real instruction starts cannot sit at `next` and strictly inside `next`, so whenever the rule fires at least one of the two decodes is already wrong. The loser is truncated in place and never re-anchored: the ops that decode just emitted are dropped, an artificial RETURN marked `badinstruction` is planted at the loser's own address with recorded size 1, and an `overlapbranch` warning makes the truncation attributable. The conditional stays a conditional and its fall-through EDGE stays in the graph -- it simply ends in a halt -- and the target is then decoded on its own boundary by the ordinary walk. `badinstruction` and not `noreturn`: a `noreturn` halt is folded by `kuna_ifnoexit` into an empty `if (cond) { }`, the one rendering that is actually wrong about where control goes. Both ends of the interval are strict -- `target == curaddr` is a branch to its own fall-through, `target == curaddr + step` is a branch over one instruction, and both are ordinary compiler output. **One legitimate overlap is excluded first** (`kuna_streams_reconverge`): glibc's compiler-generated conditional-`LOCK` idiom is the SAME instruction with its prefix taken or skipped, so its two decodes end at the same address and both streams are real; the branch target's own length is decoded and the rule declines whenever `target + target_len == curaddr + step`, or whenever the target does not decode at all. The test is architecture-neutral -- no prefix-byte table -- and it is the one clause that stands between this option and deleting an atomic store on a live path | **0/675, `docs/baseline.json` unmoved**; `make test-stages` PARITY OK (603/603, +3 for the new `overlapbranch` two-pass testcase). **The acceptance probe `a-52c2ad89b522` flips**: the checker now reads `int sub_804881c(char *a0, int a1)` with its two ordinary arguments, `if (*a0 == '\xff') return;` for the truncated overlap, and the real `((int)a0[a1] ^ dat_804a2e8 + 0x3aU) != (int)*(char *)(*(char *)(a1 + 0x804a2c6) + 0x804a2bc)` comparison over the recursive descent -- no `sub_b10cdc7`, no `dat_d98ddd00`. **Corpus sweep** (standing requirement 7): **428 binaries A/B'd whole-binary (`decompile-all`, every function)** -- 142 compiler-built decbench ELFs (x86-64 O0+O2, ARM, Cortex-M firmware) and 286 crackmes-corpus executables (ELF32/64, PE32/PE32+, Mach-O, MS-DOS). **14 differ, and all 14 are hand-written or obfuscated crackmes; 0 of the 142 compiler-built binaries change a byte.** 78 functions move and the emitted function COUNT is identical on both arms (7,799), so nothing leaves the inventory. Classified: 4 functions go from a hard `(error: ...)` to real code, 2 lose only two never-read variable declarations, 1 is rendering-only, and 71 are mixed add+remove inside decodes that are garbage on both arms (33 of them in `crypto4.exe`'s IAT thunk region, 29 in `CrackMe.obf.exe`); aggregate errors fall 387 -> 383. Two gains are concrete and independent of the witness: in `nano` (an anti-debugging ptrace crackme) the always-taken `jz +3 / jnz +1 / e8` pair stops swallowing the argument setup and `main`'s ptrace calls recover their request codes (`func_00101189(0,...)`, `(7,...)`, `sub_...(0xd,...)`) where the off arm printed them with no arguments at all; in `poof.exe` a bogus `*v4 = *v4 ^ (unsigned int)v4;` store through a junk pointer disappears and the following test reads the real `dat_40311f`. **The wrong-output risk was ablated, not argued.** The exclusion is REACHED on ordinary compiler output -- instrumented, a statically linked glibc's `je +1` over an `f0` `LOCK` prefix hits the strict-interior test (`curaddr=0x401004 step=5 target=0x401005 -> true`) and is declined by reconvergence (`tlen=4 -> true`). With `kuna_streams_reconverge` forced to `false` and nothing else changed, `_int_free` moves 487 lines and a minimal `je +1`/`LOCK OR` fixture renders as `if (a1) return;` -- **the atomic store deleted on a live path**. As shipped both are byte-identical. **Speed -0.22%** on a target where the option is provably inert (`mv` -O2 `main`, output byte-identical on both arms) and **-1.71%** on the witness (`scripts.pipeline.timeit`, median of 11, 5% budget); an interleaved min-of-9 A/B/A on the neutral target puts ON vs OFF at **+0.00%** on the minimum, and its on-vs-on control arm shows the identical median shift, i.e. the difference is noise. The hook is an `Option::take` and two integer comparisons per instruction; the extra decode of the branch target happens only once the strict-interior test has already matched. **Regression cover**: a two-pass `tests/stages/overlapbranch.xml` (default recovers the real stream; `option overlapbranch off` reproduces both artefacts) plus the promoted `tests/cli/overlapping-anti-disassembly-sequence.json`, whose acceptance clauses are verbatim and whose target is re-pointed at a vendored 2 KiB ELF32 fixture (`kuna-analysis/tests/fixtures/overlapbranch_i386`, generator beside it) because the need's own target is a dataset crackme and CI has no dataset. Bundle: `docs/features/overlapping-anti-disassembly-sequence/` |
| DIV-107 | `ppclocalentry` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): the Listing walk refuses a function entry at a PPC64 ELFv2 **local entry point** -- an address a defined `STT_FUNC` symbol declares, through the `st_other` local-entry field, to be a point inside itself | **every function in a PPC64 ELFv2 image came back as two.** RE-friction round 2, need `ppc64-localentry-splits-function`, witnessed on this repo's own `plt_ppc64le` fixture. The OpenPOWER ELFv2 ABI gives a function a *global* entry (the symbol's `st_value`, whose first two instructions materialise the TOC pointer `r2` from `r12`) and a *local* entry a few bytes later, where a caller that already holds the right `r2` -- anything in the same module -- branches instead; the distance is recorded per symbol in `st_other` and `readelf -sW` prints it as `[<localentry>: 8]`. Nothing in kuna read that field, so the recursive-descent walk saw an intra-module `bl` land eight bytes past a function symbol and minted a function there like any other CALL target, which `funcdisc_recursive` / `fast_funcdisc` then committed. `kuna functions` reported `__do_global_ctors_aux` at size 8 followed by `sub_948` at size 112, and the same 8-byte husk / anonymous-body pairing for `deregister_tm_clones`, `__do_global_dtors_aux` and `frame_dummy` -- every routine in the image with a distinct local entry. The husk is what an agent actually got back: S2's `funcboundflow` truncates a fall-through that reaches a known function entry, and the entry it stopped at was eight bytes into the function being decompiled, so `kuna decompile __do_global_ctors_aux` emitted an empty body with a `funcboundflow: fall-through reached the next function entry` warning while the constructor loop was reachable only under the generated name. Upstream has no analog because the C++ tree has no analyzer-tier discovery walk at all. Default-**on** because the fold is a strict correction rather than a judgement -- the two entries are the same routine by the ABI's construction -- and because it is provably non-destructive: it applies only when the global entry is itself a walk seed with no other seed in between, so the walk's instruction closure is invariant and the bytes at the local entry are reached as the global entry's fall-through either way. Three further guards (a real `st_other` offset, a sized symbol containing its own local entry, no other defined text symbol at the folded address) bound it. PPC64-only and inert on an image whose symbols carry no local-entry annotation, so 0/675 datatest and 0 stage assertions move; the repo's single PowerPC fixture is the whole in-tree blast radius, where the four husks disappear and the four bodies re-attach to their names. `--option ppclocalentry off` restores the previous, husk-producing discovery set byte for byte. |
| DIV-108 | `ptrdepthcap` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **off** in the catalog, **on** in the `aggressive` preset -- the preset `auto` selects under 500 KiB, so the cap is the default rendering of `kuna decompile`, `decompile-all`, `decompile-project` and the web front-end): the type propagation refuses to adopt a candidate that deepens an already unsatisfiable pointer equation | **A C++ string object was declared `unsigned long long *****`, and the false types dominated the listing.** RE-friction round 2, need `c-string-objects-become`, challenge `6547b4d50f4238b24302b588` (`trappy attack.exe`, PE x86-64, MSVC C++): the key checker at `0x1400011c0` carried 33 five-star declarations in 350 lines, and the tester could only use it after hand-tracking fields through `unsigned long long *****` and `char *****`. **The filed hypothesis ("MSVC STL object layouts and constructor prototypes are not propagated into stack locals") is REFUTED**: nothing about the defect is C++, MSVC, STL, layout or prototype specific, and it reproduces on a 0x131-byte hand-built x86-64 ELF bytechunk with three unnamed extern stubs and no C++ at all. **Mechanism** (P5, `coreaction_infertypes.rs (propagate_type_edge)`): a small-string-optimized object keeps EITHER the characters OR a pointer to them in the same first 8 bytes, chosen on a capacity field, so the compiler emits one MULTIEQUAL whose inputs are `PTRSUB(spacebase,-0xN)` -- typed pointer-to-the-mapped-local by the spacebase arm of `propagate_add_in2_out` -- and a LOAD from that very address, typed as the local itself. That is `T = ptr(T)`, which no finite type satisfies, so `propagate_one_type` adopts a type exactly ONE pointer level deeper per pass and only stops at the empirical seven-pass settle ceiling. Measured, not argued: making that ceiling settable and sweeping it 1..12 on the witness gives max pointer depth 2,2,3,4,5,5,5,5 -- one level per pass -- and tracing every depth-&gt;=3 candidate the funnel adopted names the producer (1,368 INDIRECT, 958 MULTIEQUAL, **84 PTRSUB**, 44 COPY, 42 PTRADD, 4 STORE, 4 INT_EQUAL; every PTRSUB `in0 -&gt; out-1` off `register:0x20`, i.e. `&amp;stack_local`). The markers are carriers; the spacebase PTRSUB is the producer. **The fix is upstream's own rule, called where it was missing.** `TypeFactory::getTypePointerNoDepth` (`type.cc:1509`) refuses a pointer whose target is a pointer-to-pointer, substituting `ptr(undefined&lt;N&gt;)`, and returns an existing `ptr(undefined&lt;N&gt;)` unchanged -- a fixed point. Ghidra calls it from `TypeOpLoad`/`TypeOpStore::propagateType` only and kuna's port of that (`propagate_to_pointer`) is faithful; this escalation does not go through LOAD/STORE, so the rule never ran. ON applies it at the single funnel every adopted type passes through, in `p5_types/kuna_ptrdepth.rs`. The collapsed type is LESS specific than the concrete pointer already held, so the `0 &gt; type_order` adoption test rejects it and the lattice settles rather than escalating; depth 1 and depth 2 over a concrete base are untouched, so `char **argv` keeps its spelling | **0/675, `docs/baseline.json` unmoved** (catalog default off, and the XML corpus applies no mode); `make test-stages` **PARITY OK 605/605** with the new two-pass `tests/stages/kuna-ptrdepthcap.xml` (602 -&gt; 605). **The acceptance probe `a-4ac515df8701` flips** -- and was STRENGTHENED before it was relied on, because as filed it had no `exit_code` clause and a crash would have passed it: `+ exit_code eq 0`, `+ stdout_absent "\*\*\*"` (no three-deep pointer anywhere), and `+ stdout_matches` on all four literals the tester had to hand-track. **Corpus sweep** (standing requirement 7): 90 binaries / **22,307 functions** A/B'd whole-binary. **103 change (0.46%); three-or-more-star occurrences 1,617 -&gt; 28 and the functions carrying one 80 -&gt; 17; ZERO call sites lost or gained** across all 103 (call-name multiset per function). 98 are type-only; the 5 with statement-level differences were read line by line and every residual is an addressing SPELLING (`*(char *)((long long)v + N)` becomes `v[N]`, `v = (T ****)((long long)v + N)` becomes `v = &amp;v[N]`, several of them plainly better -- `if (v[N] != 'L')` for `if (*(char *)((long long)v + N) != 'L')`). **One degradation, named rather than hidden**: where the object really is an array of 8-byte pointers, a single 8-byte zero store re-renders as eight one-byte stores -- 12 such lines in 2 functions out of 22,307. **Speed is paid only where the option fires**: on a 1,027-function binary whose output is byte-identical on both arms, `decompile-all` measures **-0.12% min / +0.05% median** against an on-vs-on control of -0.10%/-0.04%, so the per-edge hook is free; on the witness binary (1 of 144 functions changes) whole-binary is **+5.3%** and the witness function alone **+8.5%** on the minimum -- ~40 ms, all of it in the one function whose lattice now settles instead of freezing at the ceiling. Randomized-interleaved throughout: a fixed A/B/A order on this box reported **-29% between two IDENTICAL arms**, so ordering had to be randomized before any number meant anything. `--option ptrdepthcap off` restores upstream's unbounded escalation byte for byte. Bundle: `docs/features/c-string-objects-become/` |
| DIV-109 | `tailcallframe` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): a direct `jmp` whose target is NOT a known function is recovered as a tail call when the instructions ending at it tear down EXACTLY the frame the entry block built | **A keyboard callback decompiled to 1,555 lines because its tail jump annexed the renderer.** RE-friction round 2, need `direct-address-keyboard-handler`, challenge `6989ca5da15272fa37a80c43` (`lugosiii`, stripped x86-64 Wayland/xkb PIE): `kuna decompile 0x6500 --addr` emitted the keyboard handling and then ran on through the renderer at `0x4610`, UI title and drawing calls included, from the tail `jmp 0x4610` at `0x66e2` -- while the SAME function already rendered `sub_4610(a0)` correctly at `0x6cc3`, where the compiler used a real `call`. **The filed hypothesis is HALF right and its half is the wrong half**: it named `0x6500`'s own absence from the function map, but `0x6500` being undiscovered is why the agent had to ask by address at all -- the annexation is caused by `0x4610` being undiscovered. **Mechanism**: `tailcalljump` (DIV-13, default on) resolves its callee through `query_call(dest).is_some()`, so it only fires on a target some oracle already found. Every kuna oracle reaches a function from a symbol, an unwind record, or a direct `call` the recursive-descent walk arrived at; `0x6500` is only a `wl_keyboard_listener` code pointer in `.data.rel.ro`, so the walk never enters it, never sees its `call 0x4610`, and neither address is a function (`kuna functions` reports 34 entries, 30 of them PLT stubs, and neither `0x4610` nor `0x6500` is among them under `auto`, `reliable`, `aggressive`, `fast`, `--option listing on`, `aif on` or `eh_frame_full on`). Proved rather than argued by `map function 0x4610 renderer` in `decomp_dbg`, which alone takes the output 1,555 -> 440 lines. **The signal is the frame teardown.** A tail jump is the caller's last instruction, so the compiler restores the stack pointer first and the `jmp` executes exactly where `ret` would find it; an intraprocedural jump never does, because the code it jumps to still needs the frame. `p2_lift/kuna_tailcallframe.rs` measures two constant stack-pointer deltas over the already-decoded raw p-code -- forward from the entry address (`push rbx; sub rsp,0x10` = `-0x18`) and backward from the branch (`add rsp,0x10; pop rbx` = `+0x18`) -- and fires when the second is a strictly positive teardown of exactly the first. Both scans are per-INSTRUCTION, not per-op, which is load-bearing: an x86 `call` lowers to `SP = SP - 8` and a return-address store BEFORE its `CPUI_CALL`, so an op-at-a-time scan charges the run a phantom push and silently declines every function whose entry block contains a call (it cost 2 of the 4 witnesses in this binary until it was fixed). Both stop at the first control-flow instruction (so neither leaves its block), at a stack-pointer write that is not `SP = SP +/- <const>` (a `leave`-style `SP = FP` restore declines), at a neighbour more than 16 bytes away, and after 24 instructions. A frameless leaf can NEVER match -- with no frame torn down there is no evidence and an ordinary unconditional jump would be indistinguishable -- nor can the function's own entry, nor an address this function has already decoded | **0/675 PARITY OK, `docs/baseline.json` unmoved**; `make test-stages` **PARITY OK 609/609** with the new two-pass `tests/stages/tailcallframe.xml` (605 -> 609). **The acceptance probe `a-f13adb91d5d4` flips**: `kuna decompile lugosiii 0x6500 --addr` goes 1,555 -> **427** lines, both `LUGOSI'S II` occurrences and all 12 `sub_5e30(` calls gone, `sub_6500(` still emitted. **Blast radius, measured on the surface it changes and the surface it must not.** Whole-binary `decompile-all` A/B over **62 binaries** (decbench O0/O2/O2-noinline x86-64 PIE, i386 PE, ARM Cortex-M, u-boot A32): **0 firings, 0 output differences**. **The reason is the exact-cancellation clause, not discovery** -- an earlier draft of this row claimed `tailcalljump` already owns the jump wherever discovery works, and that is WRONG: this rule is asked for every direct BRANCH whose target is not a known function *entry*, which includes every ordinary intraprocedural label. What actually rejects compiler output is that a shared return sequence has to be shared *including* its teardown, so the jump is emitted part-way through the epilogue and the epilogue delta is a PARTIAL teardown. Verified independently in attempt 2 by re-implementing the predicate statically over disassembly and running it across **60 unstripped x86-64 ELFs / 26,110 sized `FUNC` symbols**, which carry **65,361** sites of the raw shape (an intraprocedural `jmp` immediately preceded by a stack teardown) and **0** that satisfy the exact cancellation; gcc and clang at `-O1/-O2/-O3/-Os` all emit `add rsp,0x68; jmp <shared tail>` against a `-0x70` prologue, and the one full six-register teardown found in a real LLVM `-O2` function declines because its prologue is shrink-wrapped out of the entry block (`prologue == 0`). **The residual is named rather than hidden**: because a kuna `FunctionSymbol` has no extent, the rule cannot ask whether `dest` is still inside the caller, so a hand-built function that tears the frame down COMPLETELY and then jumps to a shared `ret` in its own body is recovered as a tail call and renders a fabricated `sub_<addr>()` call -- demonstrated on a 27-byte fixture, and not reproducible from any compiler tested. The sound fix is to defer the decision until the flow work-stack drains and then ask whether the function decoded `dest` by another path; that is a change to the flow walk's ordering rather than to this predicate, and is left as follow-up work. Address-level A/B over **377 candidate entries** in 8 stripped crackmes (first instruction after each padding run): **0 firings**. On the witness binary itself, all 32 candidate entries A/B'd: **4 fire, 4 differ**, and all four were read line by line -- `0x6500` 1,555 -> 427, `0x63d0` 1,143 -> 10, `0x6d40` 1,145 -> 11, `0x6db0` 1,156 -> 24, every one of them a callback whose renderer call is now `sub_4610(a0)` and whose own body is intact. `0x6e30` correctly does NOT fire: its transfer to `0x4610` is a CONDITIONAL `jne` at `0x6e89`, which is not a tail call. **Speed**: -92.95% on the witness function (7 repeats -- it stops decompiling 1,100 lines it should never have had); on a byte-identical binary (coreutils `dd`, `decompile-all`, 5 interleaved pairs) **-0.9% min / +3.5% mean**, i.e. inside noise, since the predicate runs only on a direct BRANCH whose target `query_call` already failed to resolve. `--option tailcallframe off` restores the flow-into-the-callee decode byte for byte. Also vendored: `decompiler/crates/kuna-analysis/tests/fixtures/tailcallframe_x86_64` (14,488 bytes, source beside it) plus `kuna-console/tests/verify_tailcallframe.rs`, because neither parity corpus can carry the DISCOVERY half -- both are symbol-less bytechunks with no function map for `query_call` to miss. Bundle: `docs/features/direct-address-keyboard-handler/` |
| DIV-110 | `widestrings` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): the string-literal analysis pass also scans at 2-byte (UTF-16LE) width and plants a typelocked `wchar2[N]` at each wide literal — Ghidra `StringsAnalyzer`'s `allCharWidths` arm, which kuna's 1-byte port left as a documented seam | **A wide Windows-API argument rendered as its own first character.** RE-friction round 2, need `wide-api-string-args`, challenge `65acadf3eef082e477ff5ede` (`ObfuscationFiesta.exe`, PE32+): `sub_140002530`'s anti-debugging checks emitted `LoadLibraryW("n")` and `FindWindowW("O",0)` where the image says `L"ntdll.dll"` and `L"OllyDbg - [CPU]"` — the DLL and window-class names ARE the content of such a check, and `kuna strings --encoding utf16` had been printing them in full all along while the C did not. Read at 1-byte width a UTF-16LE literal is a ONE-CHARACTER string: the NUL behind the first unit closes the run, and a 1-char run is below the pass's minimum length, so nothing was marked up at all. What then produced the `"n"` was NOT the 1-byte pass (the filed hypothesis) — it was `operand_refs`, a `--mode aggressive` member and so the default for anything under 500 KiB, whose run test accepts a SINGLE visible character and read the first unit plus its high-byte NUL as a complete `char[2]`. The fix is the widened matcher (same printable-ASCII recognizer over each unit's low byte, same require-NUL-end rule, same minimum length 5, same section set, even addresses only) plus the ordering that makes it stick: the wide facts commit BEFORE the 1-byte stream, so the width that read the whole literal wins the commit's occupied guard. The character type's size 2 is what already made the printer emit the `L` prefix. Sweep over 88 RE-dataset binaries (`decompile-all` both arms): the 40 carrying no UTF-16 literal are byte-identical, and 28 of the 48 that carry one change. Normalising the variable RENUMBERING an added declaration causes, the whole delta is 434 added / 429 removed lines: 259 wide literals recovered, 121 `char *` -> `wchar_t *` declarations, 16 bare constants that became named data symbols (a wide buffer in a writable section is named, not quoted), and 38 consequential lines, all read — the largest is `LOL`'s MSVC charset walk going from `*(unsigned short *)&v[2]` / `&v[4]` to `v[1]` / `&v[2]` over a `wchar_t *`, the same program spelled correctly. No call site is lost or gained and no arm exits differently from its partner. Speed +0.11% on the witness (7 interleaved repeats). Every parity gate byte-identical (the two XML corpora are symbol-less bytechunks, which no analysis pass reaches) |
| DIV-111 | `machomain` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): the Mach-O `LC_MAIN` entry routine is named `main` through the existing `entry_names` overlay and declared `int main(int argc, char **argv)` | **A stripped macOS binary would not say where the program starts.** RE-friction round 2, need `mach-o-entry-function`, challenge `653d88600f4238b24302b0ec` (`crackme0x04`, Mach-O 64-bit x86_64 PIE, stripped): `kuna functions` listed 24 functions, every one of them a `sub_<addr>`, and the program routine only as `sub_100003a80`, which `kuna decompile` rendered `unsigned long sub_100003a80(void)` — so finding `main` meant reading bodies until one looked like a prompt loop. The image had been saying which one it was the whole time: `LC_MAIN` is a load command whose `entryoff` field is documented as the file offset of `main()`, it survives `strip` (kuna's Mach-O entry oracle 1 already READ it, purely as an address), and `dyld` calls `__TEXT.vmaddr + entryoff` as `main(argc, argv, envp, apple)`. The filed hypothesis — that the LC_MAIN loader path simply does not label the entry — stood. The prototype half is the Mach-O counterpart of the PE `entrymainproto` pass, typed differently on purpose: that pass reports the widths a recovered call site establishes and refuses to assert the C library's declaration, whereas `LC_MAIN` IS the POSIX `main` by definition, so `int`/`char **` is the honest spelling (and lets a literal render through `argv[i]`); `envp` is left undeclared. Sweep over the 23 Mach-O images of the RE dataset (`decompile-all` both arms): 8 fire and 15 are refused, and the refusals are fully accounted for — 12 already carry a `_main` symbol at that address (a named entry is never overwritten) and 3 are `LC_UNIXTHREAD`-only pre-10.8 images whose entry is the crt's `start`, not `main`. Of the 8, 6 change only the declaration line, 1 also narrows a local from `unsigned int` to `int`, and 2 gain one spurious argument at an unprototyped callee (`___chkstk_darwin(CONCAT44(v7,argc))`) — the same standing live-argument-register behaviour that pass measures at 4 of its 30. No statement is moved, deleted or re-anchored anywhere. Every parity gate byte-identical, and that is NOT evidence here: both XML corpora are symbol-less ELF bytechunks, so no Mach-O loader change can move them in either direction. The closing evidence is the acceptance probe plus `tests/cli/mach-o-entry-function.json`, re-pointed onto a new in-repo stripped Mach-O fixture (`macho_stripped_main`, with its generator beside it) |
| DIV-112 | `calleedeadarg` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): at an unlocked call site, a bounded decode of the CALLEE's own body may veto a register argument the callee provably never reads | **kuna's output contradicted itself inside one file: it declared `int _secret_function(void);` and then called `_secret_function(v3);`.** RE-friction round 2, need `void-callee-spurious-arg`, challenge `68149b8a8f555589f353117c` (Mach-O arm64 `practice_2_rengr`): `_main` renders `v3 = scanf("%d",&v2); ... _secret_function(v3);` while `_secret_function` decompiled on its own is `int _secret_function(void)` — the export does not recompile and the reader cannot tell whether the callee consumes the value. The filed hypothesis ("the live return register from scanf was treated as an argument to the subsequent no-argument call") STANDS. `ActionActiveParam` recovers an unlocked callee's argument list from the CALLER's data flow alone (`FuncCallSpecs::checkInputTrialUse`, fspec.cc:5592) and `AncestorRealistic::enterNode` answers `pop_solid` for a Varnode defined by a CALL; where the ABI's return register and first argument register coincide (`x0` on AArch64, `r0` on ARM) the previous call's result IS the next call's argument as far as the caller can see. `calleearity`/`calleearityfwd` cannot help — they reconcile against a SIBLING call, they are only ever additive, and there is one call site. **Mechanism:** two independent conditions, and the pass is only as safe as their conjunction. (1) A bounded decode from the callee's entry proves every path WRITES the register before reading it; each path carries the bytes already written, a read of an unwritten byte vetoes the range, and every path terminator — RETURN, a nested call, an unresolved BRANCHIND, a LOAD/STORE naming the register space, an undecodable instruction — demands the write have happened already, which is what lets a body that overwrites `x0` and then calls `printf` prove `x0` dead while a body whose first act is a call proves nothing. (2) The value in the register must be an earlier call's leftover result, not something the caller loaded, computed or received as its own parameter. **Both conditions were forced by measurement, not design.** Requiring the WRITE rather than the absence of a read came from the `stackreturn` datatest, whose three callees are one `c3` byte each: a bare `ret` reads nothing, so a "never read" rule declared every register dead and deleted the arguments of every stub in the image (3/675 assertions). Requiring the leftover-result shape came from a u-boot ARM sweep, where callee evidence alone emptied `do_bootm(ctx->cmdtp,0,v2,bootm_argv)` and `ubifs_scan_a_node(a0,v9,v11,a1,v1,1)` — dropping a LEADING register argument punches a hole that `ParamListStandard::fillinMap`'s positional rules read as the end of the list, taking every later argument with it. The probe is taken from the driver right after the flow build (the per-function handle carries the load image but no translator — the seam `rustabi`'s call-OUTPUT probe already uses), cached per callee entry, and skipped for a function with fewer than two calls. **Evidence for default-on:** 675/675 datatests and 613/613 stage assertions unchanged; collateral sweep over 61 binaries / 24,306 functions (`decompile-all`, both arms, every changed line read) changes 5 functions (0.021%) — 3 genuine argument-list corrections verified against the callee disassembly (`env_get(name,leftover)` -> `env_get(name)`, `tstc(v4,v3)` -> `tstc()`, and a glibc `__libc_fatal`-shaped `sub_4108b0("...",v3,v5,0x405992)` -> `sub_4108b0("...")` whose body forwards only `rdi`), 2 pure variable-renumberings with identical semantics, plus two calls whose result is now bound instead of discarded; ZERO arguments lost that the callee reads and ZERO statements deleted. Cost: interleaved min-of-N, +0.44% on the witness and +0.78% on a whole-binary `decompile-all`; in ghidra mode the callee decodes are host round trips, so `ghidra_sim_e2e`'s session pins move 1314 -> 1613 getPcode and 801 -> 1044 distinct decoded instructions (re-pinned with that reason) |
| DIV-113 | `rodatastring` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): a run of wide COPYs out of read-only memory into one stack buffer is collapsed into a single `builtin_strncpy(dest, "...", n)` | **kuna emitted C that does not compile, and hid a string it had already recovered.** RE-friction round 2, need `stack-string-invalid-cast`, challenge `68149b8a8f555589f353117c` (Mach-O arm64 `practice_2_rengr`): `_main` rendered its message buffer as `v1[0] = (char[8])s_100003f1d._0_8_;`, `v5 = s_100003f1d[8];`, `v6[0] = (char[7])s_100003f1d._9_7_;`, `v8._0_9_ = s_100003f1d._16_9_;` -- C has no array cast and `._0_9_` is member syntax on an array object, so the export does not recompile, and the four lines spell nothing the reader can use while `strings` reports `Hello, Reverse Engineer!` at that very address. The filed hypothesis (piecewise constant-to-stack stores typed as array-valued casts) STANDS in symptom but is wrong in mechanism: the stores are not piecewise constants at all. `RuleStringCopy` (`constseq.cc`) gathers a stack string only from COPYs whose inputs are p-code CONSTANTS -- the character-at-a-time form a compiler emits at `-O0`. A compiler that already has the literal in `.rodata`/`__cstring` instead emits a BLOCK copy: wide loads out of read-only memory re-stored into the frame. Those survive heritage as free read-only memory varnodes, never constants, so the constant-input guard declines and the run falls through to the printer as partial-symbol slices. **Mechanism:** a new S5 constsequence rule gathers the same-block run and claims it only when every step is a fact: each COPY source is `Varnode::isReadOnly` free memory (image bytes ARE run-time bytes); all sources lie in ONE covering data symbol the string-literal analysis planted, typed as a char-printable array; source and destination advance in lockstep (block copy, not a shuffle); the COPYs tile the destination EXACTLY -- no gap, no overlap -- across the symbol's whole length, so nothing is invented and nothing is dropped; the image bytes really are one NUL-terminated string of that length; and no LOAD/STORE/CALL interferes between members (the same `ArraySequence::interfereBetween` window the string driver demands). A foreign write into the region between the first and last member also vetoes the run, because the CALLOTHER is inserted at the FIRST member and would be re-ordered against it. A single-COPY run is deliberately left alone -- the defect is the SPLIT copy, and a whole-string single COPY already renders as one assignment. Build and teardown are the string path's own `constructTypedPointer` + `removeCopyOps`, reached through `from_rodata_run`/`transform_rodata`; only the source byte count is supplied by the caller, since `RuleStringCopy` derives it as one character per op and this run moves blocks. **Evidence for default-on:** 675/675 datatests and 615/615 stage assertions unchanged -- and that is NOT evidence by itself, because both parity corpora are symbol-less bytechunks, so the covering-string-symbol guard can never fire there. The real evidence is the collateral sweep over the 135-binary RE dataset (`decompile-all` both arms, 62,131 functions, every changed line read): 4 functions change (0.0064%), and all 4 are the intended recovery -- `crackme0x00::_main` 8+4+1 -> `builtin_strncpy(v2,"NoxIsTheBest",0xd)`, `cursed::sub_4311c0` 7+7+1 -> `builtin_strncpy(v11,"/usr/lib/gconv",0xf)`, `practice_2_rengr::_main` 8+1+7+9 -> the witness, and `crackme1.exe::sub_1400012f0` 8+8+4 -> `builtin_strncpy(v2,"rCC_ZPRGZ] UZ]Z@[VW",0x14)`. In every one the byte count equals the tiled width and the quoted text is the image content; no statement is moved or lost beyond the slices the CALLOTHER replaces, no call site changes, and the destination DECLARATIONS are byte-identical between arms (the only declaration lines in the whole sweep diff are a renumbering in `crackme1.exe`). 12 binaries are excluded from both arms alike by a pre-existing whole-binary load failure. Residual, deliberately out of scope: where the frame carving split the buffer, the slices the run used to write survive as unread declarations (`char v5;`, `char v6[7]`, `char v8[24]` beside `v1` in the witness) -- the local variable map still sees the frame the original wide stores carved, which is a P6 concern and is identical with the option off. Speed: -21.4% on the witness (9 repeats), i.e. no measurable cost -- the sign is favourable and the magnitude is inside this machine's known single-target noise floor (`returncopysplit` measured -20.23% on byte-identical output). Restore the slice assignments with `option rodatastring off` |
| DIV-114 | `inputparamgap` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): in the FUNCTION'S OWN input recovery, `ParamListStandard::forceInactiveChain` may no longer demote an ACTIVE **register** trial because a run of unused argument registers precedes it | **kuna emitted a handler whose first statement branches on a variable nothing assigns.** RE-friction round 2, need `keyboard-callback-uses-undefined`, challenge `6989ca5da15272fa37a80c43` (x86-64 ELF `lugosiii`): the Wayland `wl_keyboard_listener` key callback at `0x6500` rendered as `void sub_6500(long a0)` with `int v7; // r8d` and `int v8; // r9d` declared and then read, `if (v8 != 1 || ...)` and `xkb_state_key_get_one_sym(..., v7 + 8)`. The ABI signature is `key(void *data, struct wl_keyboard *, uint32_t serial, uint32_t time, uint32_t key, uint32_t state)`, so `key` is in `r8d` and `state` in `r9d`. **The filed diagnosis -- 'no prototype evidence, so live-in registers are not promoted' -- was overturned by a 30-line fixture**: `ActionInputPrototype` DOES promote gap-separated live-in registers with no prototype anywhere, filler parameters and all, and a control callback with a TWO-register hole (`rdi`/`rcx` read, `rsi`/`rdx` dead) recovers `void on_two(long a0,unsigned long a1,unsigned long a2,int a3)` today. The discriminating variable is the WIDTH of the hole: `forceInactiveChain` (`fspec.cc:1519`) is called with `maxchain = 2`, the witness's `rsi`/`rdx`/`rcx` hole is three registers, and once the run passes two the rule marks EVERY remaining trial inactive -- the active ones included. That rule's premise is a call site's: there a trial is scored from the CALLER's data flow, where a live argument register is genuinely ambiguous. For a function's own inputs an ACTIVE trial means the body reads that caller-saved register before defining it, and an untouched argument register is just an ignored parameter -- which a callback whose signature is fixed by the API it is registered with has as a matter of course. | 0/675 datatest assertions and 0 stage assertions changed; `make test`/`make test-stages` PARITY OK. Speed, interleaved min-of-15 on the witness: **+0.07% min / +0.33% median** -- no measurable cost (`scripts.pipeline.timeit`'s sequential -30.52% is the known sequential-measurement artifact). Corpus sweep, 83 real-world binaries / 36,589 decompiled functions: **196 changed (0.54%), every one of them a SIGNATURE gain of +4 to +6 parameters, and 0/196 with any statement change** after normalizing `vN`/`aN` identifiers -- the pass only ever adds register parameters and renames the undefined locals they replace. **Three narrowing clauses, one of them measured**: the flag is carried on the `ParamActive` `ActionInputPrototype` builds so no call site is touched; only an ACTIVE **exclusion (register)** trial is protected, which is what bounds the gain to the ABI's register file -- an earlier draft that exempted the whole section instead let one Win64 `sub_140010a57` span its four-register hole and promote eleven scratch slots of the caller's argument area into a fifteen-parameter signature (343 changed functions, 12 with >20 residual diff lines); and it never makes a trial active that was not already active. `option inputparamgap off` restores upstream's veto. |
| DIV-115 | `simdlane` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): a ONE-BYTE lane read of a constant-mask `pshufb` byte shuffle resolves to the source lane the mask selects | **kuna asked the reader to simulate SSE byte lanes to find out that thirty-two variables were one value.** RE-friction round 2, need `simd-constant-string-initializer`, challenge `653d88600f4238b24302b0ec` (macOS x86-64 `Crackme0x04`): the 752-byte initializer `sub_100003790` rendered `v7 = pshufb(ZEXT116(dat_100008040),0);` followed by thirty-two `SUB161(v7,k)` / `SUB161(v9,k)` temporaries, thirty of them declared only to be read once, every one naming the SAME byte. The filed hypothesis (vector lane extraction and constant propagation do not recognize the byte-sequence construction idiom) STANDS, and the mechanism is exact: `pshufb` has no p-code semantics, the x86 SLEIGH spec models it as an opaque CALLOTHER over a 16-byte value, `ActionLaneDivide` then splits the vector consumers into byte lanes, and no simplification rule can see through a CALLOTHER, so `RuleSubExtComm` / `RuleSubZext` and copy propagation never fire even though the shuffle mask is an immediate zero sitting in the operand. The instruction is a pure permutation with an exact per-lane definition once the mask is known, `dst[i] = (m[i] & 0x80) ? 0 : src[m[i] & (N-1)]`, so the rewrite is an IDENTITY and not a heuristic; the standard broadcast idiom `pxor xmm2,xmm2; pshufb xmm0,xmm2` resolves all sixteen lanes to `SUBPIECE(src, 0)` and the existing rules collapse them into one value. | 0/675 datatest assertions and 0 pre-existing stage assertions changed; `make test` PARITY OK 675/675, `make test-stages` PARITY OK 628/628. Bounded by construction: only a user op the architecture registered as `pshufb`/`vpshufb` (ids resolved once per program into the ArchSeam), only the three-input form at vector width 8 or 16, only a ONE-BYTE lane read (a wider SUBPIECE of a shuffle is a concatenation of lanes, so it is declined rather than approximated), and a mask wider than eight bytes only at value 0, where the `uintb` offset IS the whole value. Corpus sweep over 90 RE-dataset binaries (70 loaded under both arms, 15,365 functions, `decompile-all`): `simdlane` changes 0 of them -- a constant-mask `pshufb` is rare outside a vectorised byte loop, which is what makes the rule inert rather than risky. Witness: 15,532 -> 15,810 bytes (the lane temporaries go, the broadcast source is spelled at each use) with 32 `SUB161(` -> 0 and 30 declarations dropped; together with DIV-116 the function is 11,264 bytes. `option simdlane off` restores the opaque rendering. |
| DIV-116 | `retsplitglobal` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): a shared RETURN block carrying more than eight observable global stores is no longer a *bare epilogue*, so neither return splitter clones it into every predecessor | **46% of one function's text was three copies of one block.** Same need and challenge as DIV-115. `sub_100003790` ends in a block that fills four message buffers, seventy-two byte stores, reached three ways (the `n < 0` guard, the vectorised loop's exact-fit exit, and the scalar tail), and kuna emitted it three times: 216 of the function's 469 lines. `ActionReturnSplit::isSplittable` (`blockaction.cc:2242`) admits only MULTIEQUAL, COPY and RETURN and, for COPY, checks only that no INPUT is free -- it never looks at the COPY's OUTPUT. Upstream that is harmless because the epilogue COPYs it was written for move registers, which die at the return; a COPY into a PERSISTENT Varnode is a store to a global and is exactly what P9 prints as `dat_ADDR = ...`. Both splitters share the one predicate, which is why `option returndup off` did not change the output by a single statement. **A bound, not a veto**, and that was settled by measurement: a plain `is_persist()` veto also declined the split for GNU grep's `nlscan` (two global stores) and moved the `gh180-implied-cover-scarry` stage assertion, and a small global-writing epilogue genuinely reads better cloned, because the merged form needs a phi per global and can print a global's own value back into it on a path where the binary stores nothing. The bound of 8 is this phase's house range (`crossjumprevert` and `dedupitetail` cap a duplicated leaf run at 8 printed ops, `taildup` at 12). `Heritage::guardReturns`' synthetic `glob = glob` liveness self-copies are excluded two ways, either sufficient -- the `return_copy` flag and input and output naming the same storage -- because there is one per persistent global before every RETURN in every function, so counting them would price a bare epilogue by how many globals the whole PROGRAM has; with them counted the `ghdec-returncopysplit` stage assertions moved. | 0/675 datatest assertions and 0 pre-existing stage assertions changed; `make test` PARITY OK 675/675, `make test-stages` PARITY OK 628/628 with NO per-test opt-out. One-directional: it can only make a split not happen, so it cannot create a duplication or change what the surviving merged block does. Corpus sweep over the same 70 binaries / 15,365 functions: **2 changed (0.013%), both a pure de-duplication** -- `KeyVal2.exe sub_417ca0` 1,474 -> 1,021 bytes (17 duplicate statement copies removed, DISTINCT statement set identical) and `libmodplug ModPlug_1SetPatternLoopRange` 997 -> 987 (2 duplicate `return;`); zero statements added anywhere, zero distinct statements lost, function sets identical on every binary. Witness: 15,810 -> 11,264 bytes, the epilogue emitted once instead of three times. `option retsplitglobal off` restores the upstream predicate byte-for-byte. |
| DIV-117 | `pdatachained` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): a PE `.pdata` `RUNTIME_FUNCTION` whose `UNWIND_INFO` carries `UNW_FLAG_CHAININFO` no longer seeds a function entry; and, ungated, the exception-directory record stride follows `FileHeader.Machine` (8 bytes on ARM/ARM64/ARM64EC/ARM64X) instead of a fixed 12 | **An MSVC function was cut off a third of the way through and finished with `} while ;`, which is not C.** GH-403 (`dobin/redtest`): `sub_140002650` stops at `14000270c JZ 0x140002d6b` carrying the `funcboundflow: fall-through reached the next function entry` warning, while Ghidra decompiles the same function whole. The entry it fell through into was kuna's own invention. MSVC splits one function across several `RUNTIME_FUNCTION` records whenever it shrink-wraps a prologue or moves a cold block out of line; the first record is the function and every later one points at an `UNWIND_INFO` with `UNW_FLAG_CHAININFO` set plus a trailing chained record naming the primary, so its `BeginAddress` is a point INSIDE the primary. kuna's `.pdata` oracle read only `BeginAddress` and pushed every non-zero one, so each chunk became a `sub_<addr>` — and P2's `funcboundflow` truncates a fall-through that reaches a known function entry. The filter has to sit in `pdata_begins` itself rather than at a commit-time suppression, because the candidate reaches the inventory by two independent routes (`entry::collect_entries` and `passes::listing_seeds`) and a commit-only skip would still leave the recursive-descent walk seeded there and the primary's size wrong. On, the third dword is resolved against the loaded sections and a record whose flags set bit `0x4` contributes no entry — Ghidra gates `markAsFunction` on the same predicate. The read is total: a null `UnwindInfoAddress`, an RVA no section covers, or an empty slice all read as *not chained*, so the rule can only ever subtract a record it positively identified. Loader-tier, so the gate is the `KUNA_PDATACHAINED` env bridge (the entry oracles run inside `load file`, upstream of every per-function `option`); `--option pdatachained off` restores the previous x86/x64 discovery set exactly, and only that one — the stride below is not part of the gate. **The ARM stride is a separate, ungated bug fix**: `RUNTIME_FUNCTION` is the 12-byte `{BeginAddress, EndAddress, UnwindInfoAddress}` only on x86/x64, and ARM images use an 8-byte `{BeginAddress, UnwindData}` record whose `BeginAddress` carries the Thumb bit and whose second dword is an `.xdata` RVA only when its low two bits are clear. Reading an ARM64 table at 12 is simply the wrong parse, not a judgement call; the second dword is never dereferenced there, and chained ARM fragments are not decoded (Ghidra does not decode them either). A machine that is neither an x86 nor an ARM variant — IA64, MIPS, SH, PowerPC, each with its own record layout — now parses no exception directory at all rather than reading it at the x64 stride and dereferencing dword[2] as an `UNWIND_INFO` RVA, which is Ghidra's "Exception Data unsupported architecture" arm (`ExceptionDataDirectory.java:59-68`). | **0/675 datatest assertions and 0 stage assertions changed** — and that is by construction, not measurement: both parity corpora are symbol-less bytechunks that never construct an `ObjectLoadImage`, so no `.pdata` change can reach them, and `kuna functions` over every committed file with an `MZ` magic (10 pre-existing PEs, 251 `.pdata` records between them, zero chained) is **byte-identical on both arms**, so no fixture moves either. The real evidence is a sweep over the 53 PEs of the RE dataset and probe corpus that DO carry chained records (7,213 of them), `kuna functions` both arms: **7,434 entries removed, 27 added**, and the union of every function's extent goes 47,016,664 -> 47,015,674 bytes, i.e. **52 of 53 binaries keep every byte inside some function's extent**. The 990-byte exception is one image's run of unreferenced 5-byte incremental-link `JMP` thunks, each of which MSVC gives its own chained record and none of which anything in the image references (`kuna xrefs --to` reports 0); Ghidra drops the same thunks. The 27 additions are real functions the walk reaches once the primary stops being truncated (12 to 1,820 bytes each). Witness binary (`nikos_crack_me.exe`, 193 records / 32 chained): inventory 461 -> 416 (45 removed — the 32 chunks plus 13 zero-xref phantoms they had seeded — 0 added), `sub_1400013a0` **237 -> 784 bytes** (its true `.pdata` extent), whole-binary `funcboundflow` truncation warnings **32 -> 9**, occurrences of the invalid `} while ;` **7 -> 0**, and the union of extents byte-identical at 196,943. Whole-binary `decompile-all` over that image, both arms: of the 277 functions present in both, **263 bodies are byte-identical, 13 grew, and 0 lost a line** — the only C that disappears is the 45 dropped entries' own truncated stubs. **The residual, measured on emitted C rather than on extents**, because the extent union is blind to it — a fragment the primary's flow never reaches stays inside the primary's extent and simply stops being decompiled. On a second 240 KB MSVC image (716 records, 93 chained), `decompile-all --json` both arms compared through `line_mappings`: the option removes 99 entries, **97 keep their decompiled coverage inside the primary and 2 lose all of it** — `0x140007498` and `0x140015dc8`, 24 bytes each, **48 bytes**. Neither is a `.pdata` record: both sit in holes in the exception directory and appear in the inventory only while the chunk entries around them do, `kuna xrefs --to` reports 0 references to either on both arms, and both entries were mis-started to begin with — `0x140007498` is 8 bytes into a virtual-call thunk that starts at `0x140007490`, and `0x140015dc8` is one byte into a `mov [rip+…],rax`, so its body read an undefined `v1`. That is also why the filter is not narrowed to keep such a fragment: the decision is taken in `pdata_begins`, inside `load file` and before any instruction is decoded, so the oracle cannot ask whether the primary's flow reaches an address — and on this image no chained record loses coverage, so a narrower predicate would recover nothing. The named shape, an `__except` funclet entered only through the exception dispatcher, is what the 990 bytes above are; Ghidra has the same residual. Speed, interleaved min-of-9 on the witness binary: load + discovery (`kuna functions`) **+0.66% min / +0.78% median**, i.e. the `UNWIND_INFO` read is free. Decompiling `sub_1400013a0` itself is **+24.9%**, which is the price of the fix rather than a cost of the pass — the function is 3.3x larger now that it is whole; whole-binary `decompile-all` over the same image is **-1.33%** (interleaved min-of-3), the 45 fewer functions paying for the larger bodies. Gates: `make test` 675/675 PARITY OK, `make test-stages` PARITY OK, `make rust-test` green, `make check-spec` OK (lenient + strict), `make test-cli` 30/30, `kuna catalog --check` OK. Counters re-derived from the built artifact: settables 149, tiers (35, 60, 54), `phase_catalog.json` 149, `docs/options.md` 149 |
| DIV-118 | **`noreturnretuse`** (P4 output-prototype; default **on**) | `Funcdata::only_op_use` (C++ `Funcdata::onlyOpUse`, funcdata_varnode.cc:1851) answers whether a Varnode reaching `opmatch` is used ONLY there. Any other CALL consuming the same Varnode rejects the trial, and `ActionOutputPrototype` then types the function `void`. On an ABI whose return register is also the first argument register -- ARM/AArch64 `r0`/`w0` -- a status helper trips that for free: the status is returned on the normal path and handed to a failure handler on the error path, so `int guarded_status(int)` recovers as `void guarded_status(int)` with a bare `return;`. kuna now skips a CALL/CALLIND use when the matched op is a RETURN, the call is the second-to-last op of its block, and the block's last op is an artificial halt flagged no-return -- a block from which no RETURN is reachable, so the two uses never compete. A call that may return, a halt further down the block, and every non-RETURN match keep the upstream rejection | 0/675 on the datatest corpus and 0 stage assertions. 0 changed lines of `decompile-all` output across 23 linked binaries (20 repo fixtures spanning x86-64/ARM/AArch64/RISC-V/MIPS/PPC64/SPARC/Mach-O, plus `/bin/ls` 14,532 lines, `gzip` 9,185, `cmp` 4,058) -- the shape needs the return register and the first argument register to be the same storage, which x86-64 does not have. It fires on ARM/AArch64 objects built with `-fstack-protector-strong`: over six `clang -O0/-O1/-O2` objects, 4 change and every change is `void ibuf(void)` -> `char ibuf(void)` with the value-bearing `return`, matching the `int ibuf(int)` the source declares, while the genuinely-void `vbuf`/`handle` in the same object stay `void` under both settings. Two-pass gate in `kuna-console/tests/verify_et_rel_status_return.rs`; restore upstream with `option noreturnretuse off` |
| DIV-119 | `fastfailnoreturn` (new kuna option, `on|off`, default **on**): a Windows `int 0x29` (`__fastfail`) ends the flow, so the call's unbalanced stack pointer never reaches a join | **Eleven interrupts cost a checker 120 lines, 63 bogus stores and every one of its Win32 call arguments.** RE-friction round 3, need `large-function-malformed-output`, challenge `6a3822888a86e4c2c55254fe` (`selam.exe`, 240 KB MSVC PE32+, no PDB): `kuna decompile 0x140002d50 --addr` emitted 1,267 lines and 159 declarations, its stack locals written as offsets off a `char *`, every call preceded by an explicit store of its own return address (`*(unsigned long long *)&v66[-8] = 0x140003e16;`), and `BCryptGenerateSymmetricKey(*(unsigned long long *)&v66[0x50],&v53,v60,v51)` taking stack blobs where it takes values. **The filed hypothesis -- C++ small-string type recovery plus stack-variable splitting -- is overturned**; the function constructs no `std::string` at all. **Mechanism**: x86 SLEIGH lifts `INT imm8` to `intloc = swi(imm8); call [intloc]` (`ia.sinc:3671`) -- a `call` with NO matching push, unlike every other x86 `CALL`, which lifts as `RSP = RSP - 8; push &next; call target`. Nothing downstream knows that, so the interrupt is an ordinary modelled call and `x86-64-win.cspec`'s default `__fastcall` (`extrapop="8" stackshift="8"`) hands eight bytes back after it: kuna prints `(*(void *)swi(0x29))(5);` followed by `v62 = &v61[8];`. Once two paths join carrying stack-pointer values eight bytes apart the frame stops being a constant offset from the spacebase, so stack locals degenerate into offsets off a pointer and each `CALL`'s return-address push -- normally a dead store into an unmapped slot -- survives. This witness holds ELEVEN `int 0x29`, the MSVC `/GS` + STL `_STL_VERIFY` idiom, which is in essentially every modern MSVC C++ image. **The fix**: `__fastfail` never returns, so the call spec is marked no-return at the P2 CALLIND seam and `flow.rs` plants the artificial halt it already plants for a named no-return callee. **MEASURED**: the witness goes 1,267 -> 1,147 lines, 63 -> 0 return-address stores, 160 -> 149 declarations, and the BCrypt call becomes `BCryptGenerateSymmetricKey(v52,&v55,v62,v54)`; over the whole image 22 of 858 functions change, all of them shrinking, gotos 456 -> 390, and not one named call is lost. Across four further Windows PEs: `selamv2` 210 -> 43 return-address stores and 92 fewer gotos, `phantomgate` 7 -> 3, `doncris_mirror_vault` byte-identical (it has no `int 0x29`), and on the obfuscated x86-32 `illusion` an 80-line garbage block that used the interrupt's *return value* collapses to six honest lines. **Default-on evidence**: byte-identical on the 675-assertion datatest corpus (0/675) -- it is gated on the Windows compiler spec (`windows`/`clangwindows`) and on the exact lifted shape, so it is structurally inert everywhere else -- and 20.5% FASTER on the witness (1,323.65 -> 1,052.49 ms, 7 samples), since cutting flow decodes strictly less. Restore the unbalanced fall-through with `option fastfailnoreturn off`. |
| DIV-120 | the CLI's decompiling surfaces (`kuna decompile`, `decompile-all`, `decompile-project`, `decompile-graph`) inject `errortoomanyinstructions off` (driver-tier default, no new flag); and, ungated, the truncation that flag selects actually truncates | **A function past the instruction budget decompiled to nothing, and the error named no way out.** RE-friction round 3, need `checker-exceeds-instruction-ceiling`, challenge `69d6affb110488a3205426e2` (crackmes.one "i love mbas", 7,131,136-byte PE32+ `crackme_shroud.exe`): `kuna decompile-all --functions sub_140001000 --json` returns `code: null` and `error: "Flow exceeded maximum allowable instructions"`, and the tester found no budget override in `kuna catalog` or `docs/cli.md` and gave up. The function is not mis-bounded -- an independent capstone recursive-descent walk from `0x140001000`, following branches and not calls, reaches **1,804,480 instructions** spanning `0x140001000..0x1406a983f` with 4 direct call targets, so the MBA obfuscator really did emit one 6,981,712-byte function and the 100000-instruction budget is doing its job. What was wrong is what happens when it fires. `max_instructions` has always had two policies (`flow.cc:396`), fatal or truncating, and the truncating one was unusable: upstream plants a `badinstruction` artificial halt at the address that overran and then **decodes it anyway**, so the next address is queued and the next call truncates again -- the halt halts nothing and the walk still runs to the end of the reachable body, now carrying one halt per instruction. On this function that is an out-of-memory abort, and the tell is that the budget does not change it: `--option errortoomanyinstructions off` died at a failed 11.5 GB allocation after 38 s under a 16 GB address-space cap at `maxinstruction` 2000 and at 5,000,000 alike. `FlowInfo::process_instruction` now returns no-fall-through at the budget after planting the halt, registers that halt in `visited` as the instruction at the address (so a branch arriving there resolves to it instead of raising "Could not find op at target address") and marks it a basic-block and instruction start -- the lesson DIV-67's `funcboundflow` truncation learned in #410. Each address still queued is halted the same way when popped, so the decode stops at the budget. With a truncation that truncates, the CLI can afford the truncating policy, and takes it: the surfaces that decompile clear the flag, an inventory or query load never follows flow and does not, and the engine/console/datatest default is still upstream's throw. The warning header names both knobs (`--option maxinstruction N` to raise the budget, `--option errortoomanyinstructions on` to make it fatal again), because an error that names no remedy is what sent the tester looking through the catalog for an option that was never there | **0/675 datatest assertions and 0 stage assertions changed, by construction rather than by luck**: neither harness takes the CLI's driver defaults, so both corpora keep upstream's throw, and no bytechunk in either comes within three orders of magnitude of the budget. `make test` 675/675 PARITY OK, `make test-stages` PARITY OK, `make rust-test` green (5,615 passed / 0 failed), `make check-spec` OK, `make test-cli` 34/34, `kuna catalog --check` OK -- no new option, no catalog counter, no stages XML. Inertness measured as well as argued: `decompile-all --json` over all 156 binary fixtures under `kuna-analysis/tests/fixtures`, both arms, is **156/156 byte-identical documents**. The changed set is exactly the functions that used to report `Flow exceeded maximum allowable instructions`, and for those it is `code: null` -> a truncated body. Witness (`sub_140001000`): before, 18.5 s to fail with nothing; after, 37.3 s / 3.76 GB RSS for **10,164 lines of C** with `error: null` -- the price of decoding the 100000 instructions the budget allows and running P3-P9 over them, paid only by a function that used to produce nothing, and bounded by `--max-fn-seconds` (the acceptance exercises it at 30). Speed elsewhere is unchanged because the flag is read at one site: interleaved min-of-3 `decompile-all` over the 1.0 MB stripped `tests/hang-repro/ssh-sk-helper`, 8.00 s vs 8.38 s (-4.5%, noise). Covered by the promoted `tests/cli/checker-exceeds-instruction-ceiling.json`, a two-pass `kuna-cli` integration test (`--option errortoomanyinstructions on` must still give `code: null`), and a strengthened `decompile_e2e::maxinstruction_option_bounds_flow_following` that now asserts the bounded run emits FEWER statements than the unbounded one -- it did not before, which is how the port bug survived having a test |
| DIV-121 | under a caller-declared function extent (`--define-function START-END` / console `function bounds`), a branch target outside the extent resolves to the artificial halt already planted for it, and the last in-body instruction is decoded rather than reported out of bounds; both ungated, both structurally inert without a declared extent | **A declared end that any branch crossed produced no C at all, and a declared end that was CORRECT deleted the function's last instruction.** RE-friction round 3, need `explicit-function-boundary-aborts`, two challenges and two testers (`69d6affb110488a3205426e2` `crackme_shroud.exe`, `6a0b84982b3df128c1df5c0d` `frz_crackme_rage_v7.exe`). `kuna decompile <bin> 0x140001000 --addr --define-function 0x140001000-0x1400010cd=entry_prefix` exited 1 with `Could not find op at target address: (ram,0x0001400010cd)` -- the declared exclusive end, reached by the `JZ` at `0x140001054` -- and `--mode reliable` and `--mode fast` failed identically. `FlowInfo::new_address` records an out-of-extent branch target on `unprocessed` and refuses to decode it; `fillin_branch_stubs` plants a `missing` artificial halt there, but nothing registered that halt in `visited`, and `FlowInfo::target` resolves through `visited` alone, so `collect_edges` threw on the very first cut edge -- the same shape DIV-120 fixed for the instruction budget and explicitly left to this need. The halts for out-of-extent addresses are now registered as the instruction at their address; the registration is restricted to the addresses `handle_out_of_bounds` recorded, because an unprocessed address INSIDE the extent means an op that should exist does not, and clipping there would shorten a correct function instead of reporting the defect (the separate open need `default-decompilation-fails-despite`, whose missing target is an in-extent NOP, still fails deliberately). **The second half was found by the collateral sweep, not by the need**: `FlowInfo::fallthru` tests `bound <= addrlist.back()` with `bound == eaddr`, which upstream can only hit at the top of memory because its `eaddr` is the space's highest address -- under a declared extent it is every function's last instruction, so the closing `ret` was never decoded and the body was reported out of bounds. `eaddr` is the last IN-BODY byte (`new_address` admits `to == eaddr`), so that instruction is decoded and the fall-through past it is caught on the next lap | **Inert without a declared extent, measured not argued**: `decompile-all --json` over all 112 binary fixtures under `kuna-analysis/tests/fixtures`, both arms, is **112/112 byte-identical documents** -- `set_range` is the only narrowing of the flow range and only a declared extent calls it. The changed population is exactly the declared-extent one, and there the oracle is kuna's own undeclared output: declaring **every** discovered function at its derived extent across those 112 binaries (2,765 functions), bodies differing from the undeclared decompile go **403 -> 138** and functions that hard-error under the declaration go **142 -> 0**. Witness (`aif_gap_x86_64` `sub_1129`, 24 bytes ending `POP RBP; RET`): declared, before, an empty `void sub_1129(void)` under a bogus out-of-bounds warning; after, the `int sub_1129(int a0) { return (a0 + 10) * 2; }` the undeclared run gives, byte for byte. Dataset witness `entry_prefix`: exit 1 with no output -> 35 lines of C under `// warn: Function flows out of bounds` naming each cut edge. Gates: `make test` 675/675 PARITY OK, `make test-stages` 635/635 PARITY OK, `make rust-test` green (5,618 passed / 0 failed), `make check-spec` OK lenient + strict, `make test-cli` 35/35, `kuna catalog --check` OK -- no new option, no catalog counter, no stages XML. Covered by the promoted `tests/cli/explicit-function-boundary-aborts.json` and three cargo tests that each fail without the change |

### Convergences (upstream behavior restored — not divergences)

A port defect whose fix moves kuna *back onto* upstream's default earns no DIV row, but a
corpus-wide output shift still needs a record for anyone bisecting. Those go here.

| Area | What changed | Evidence/effect |
|---|---|---|
| `callsitestackargs` (P4 active-input trial scoring) | kuna dropped every stack-passed call argument at call sites whose callee prototype is unlocked — x86-64 everything past the 6th register argument, i386 every argument. `check_input_trial_use` handed the trial's **callee**-relative address to `FuncProto::get_local_range().in_range()` where upstream `fspec.cc:5618` hands the argument Varnode's **caller**-relative address; on a downward-growing stack the callee-relative offsets are always positive and the caller's `localrange` negative, so every stack trial was scored no-use — and a definitely-unused trial has its CALL input replaced with constant 0, handing the argument-producing computation to dead-code elimination. Not an unported feature: `guard_calls` registers the trials and appends the argument Varnodes correctly; they were mis-scored one pass later. Introduced in `5cd4d518 rport/w10-callsite-args`. The new option is default-ON (= upstream); `off` reproduces the pre-fix output for bisection | 0/675 — `docs/baseline.json` unmoved; stages 316/316, and the one assertion whose text changed (`gh6882-sparcstructret` #2, `return v1 + a0` → `return v1[0] + a0`) changed because the OLD text was wrong: `f` is a struct-return function whose hidden return-buffer pointer kuna had also been dropping, so `f()` became `f(v2)` and the buffer typed correctly as `int4 v1 [2]`. 30-binary stratified decbench sweep (x86-64 / ARM Cortex-M / PE-i386, all three optimisation levels): calls rendered with ≥7 arguments 7 → 250, i386 empty-argument-list fraction 42.5% → 2.5%, short calls −36%; 72 runs, zero timeouts, stderr byte-identical off vs on. Speed unchanged (`decompile-all` ssh-add 10.80 → 10.79 s median, mydoom 2.03 → 1.98 s). Decbench cases closed: `O0-mydoom-mydoom-msg_b64enc` (a deleted `for(;;)` loop restored, 3 → 22 blocks) and `O2-noinline-openssh-portable-ssh-add-parse_dest_constraint` (2 → 6 `if`s, full 16-argument call) |
| `funcptrencoding` (P3 rule pool, the ISA-mode / alignment mask on an indirect-call target) | On processors that steal the low bits of a function pointer the *instruction* clears them before branching, so SLEIGH lifts the clear as a real `INT_AND` feeding the `CALLIND`: Thumb `blx` through `BXWritePC` (`ARM.sinc:221`, `local tmp = addr & 0xfffffffe`), MIPS `jalr` through `JXWritePC` (`mips.sinc:925`, `tmp = -2 & addr`). Upstream erases it in `RuleFuncPtrEncoding` (`ruleaction.cc:9928`) whenever the compiler spec declares `<funcptr align="N"/>`. kuna's port of that rule is a line-for-line transcription and has been registered in the pool (`universalaction.rs:515`) since W8, but its **only input** was a hard-coded stub — `fn funcptr_align(_data: &Funcdata) -> int4 { 0 }` in `p3_dataflow/ruleaction_8.rs`, whose first two lines are `let align = funcptr_align(data); if align == 0 { return 0; }` — so **the rule could never fire on any architecture**. The stub's premise ("the W4 `Architecture` skeleton does not carry `funcptr_align`") was stale: `architecture.rs:2105 decode_funcptr_align()` has parsed the cspec element into the field since W4, `:3902` calls it during `parse_compiler_config`, `:1869` copies it into the ArchSeam, and two other consumers (`jumptable.rs:3661`, `ruleaction_6.rs:609`'s `preserve_thumb_funcptr`, GH-8471/DIV-2) already read it live. The accessor now reads `data.get_arch().funcptr_align` like they do; nothing else changes. Cost of the stub, on every ARM/Thumb and MIPS16-capable target: the emitted C asserted a bit-clear the program never performs, *and* the mask stood between the `CALLIND` and its pointer operand so the pointer-to-code type never back-propagated onto the LOAD — `(*(code *)(*(uint4 *)(a0 + 0x44) & 0xfffffffe))(a0)` where ghidra prints `(**(code **)(param_1 + 0x44))(param_1)` and the source (`libdw1000.i`, `dwHandleInterrupt`) writes `(*dev->handleError)(dev)` with no mask at all. The width is **not** a kuna policy — the vendored cspecs declare `align="2"` for the four ARM, nine MIPS and four Loongarch cspecs plus 8051, and `align="4"` for the five AARCH64 cspecs | **675/675 PARITY OK and stages PARITY OK**, no re-pin and no per-test opt-out (the stages delta is purely the 2 new keys); the corpus's two `ARM:LE:32:v8` and six `MIPS:*:32:default` datatests contain no masked indirect call. Whole-binary `decompile-all`, occurrences of `& 0xfffffffe` / `(code *)` / `(code **)`: crazyflie `cf2.elf` 264 → 15 / 255 → 46 / **0 → 205**, nuttx 459 → 40 / 435 → 42 / **0 → 397**, libopencm3 `msc.elf` 56 → 6 / 50 → 10 / **0 → 41**, chibios `ch.elf` 38 → 13 / 40 → 30 / **0 → 16** — every ARM project in the corpus had **zero** `(code **)` before, because a mask sat on every indirect-call target; the triage measured the class at 4,027 masked indirect calls in 2,496 function-instances across 97 binary-instances in 9 ARM projects. x86-64/i386 are **byte-identical** (their cspecs declare no `<funcptr>`, so `funcptr_align == 0` and the rule stays inert): whole-binary diffs with the watchdog disabled change 0 lines on zlib `minigzip` (8,923), gzip (9,465), bash (120,695), coreutils `head` (4,403) and the i386 PE `x0r-usb.exe` (2,205). **AARCH64 `align=4` was verified rather than assumed**: upstream's `applyOp` is a single code path (`slide = ~0 << align`) with no `align == 1` branch and an exact comparison, `blr` itself emits no mask (`AARCH64base.sinc:887`), and stock **Ghidra 12.1 headless** on a purpose-built probe (`clang --target=aarch64-linux-gnu`, one plain `blr x8`, one real `and x8, x8, #0xfffffffffffffffc; blr x8`) prints `(**(code **)(param_1 + 2))(*param_1)` — byte-for-byte what kuna now prints, where kuna previously printed `(*(code *)(*(uint8 *)&a0[2] & 0xfffffffffffffffc))(*a0)`. Interleaved `decompile-all` medians, two builds of the same tree on a box running three concurrent agents: libopencm3 `msc.elf` 592 → 597 ms (+0.84%, 5 pairs), coreutils `head` 5,368 → 5,443 ms (+1.40%, 5 pairs — that binary's output is byte-identical, so it is noise, not cost), crazyflie `cf2.elf` 36,838 → 32,052 ms (**-12.99%**, 3 pairs: one op leaves the graph at every indirect call). `tests/stages/ghdec-funcptralign.xml` (0 of 2 assertions pass pre-fix, 2/2 post-fix); rule-level unit tests pin all three alignments (`funcptrencoding_noops_without_cspec_funcptr`, `..._strips_thumb_mode_bit`, `..._strips_aarch64_word_alignment`, the last also asserting that `& ~1` is declined at align=2) |
| `subright` (P3 cleanup pool, non-least-significant truncation) | `RuleSubRight::apply_op` (`ruleaction.cc:7271`) was a **port stub**: only the leading `doesSpecialPrinting`/`isPieceStructured` half was transcribed and the body then returned 0 under a `// Remainder transcribed for the next wave ... W6` comment, so upstream's `sub(V,c) => sub(V>>c*8,0)` rewrite never ran. Every SUBPIECE with a **nonzero byte offset** therefore reached the printer, which renders one as the raw p-code operator — `*v2 = SUB81(a1,7);` in openssh `ssh` `sshbuf_put_u64` @ `0x4cd60`, where Ghidra and the source both have a shift (`*local_18 = (char)((ulong)param_2 >> 0x38);` for `((u_char *)(p))[0] = (__v >> 56) & 0xff;`). `SUB81`/`SUB84`/`SUB41` are **undeclared identifiers** — kuna emits no header defining them — so those function bodies were not compilable C. kuna's own offset-0 SUBPIECE already rendered `(char)a1`, which is what proves the printer was never at fault. The rule was already registered and scheduled in upstream's position (`rrow!("subright", "cleanup", ...)`, `universalaction.rs:552`); only the ~45-line tail was missing, and it is now transcribed with every guard upstream applies — the `c == 0` bail, the address-tied `overlap == c` bail that leaves `ActionCopyMarker`'s storage markers alone, and the lone-`INT_RIGHT`/`INT_SRIGHT`-descendant lumping (declining an `INT_RIGHT` whose combined amount would evaluate to zero, clamping `INT_SRIGHT` to the sign bit). No option: restoring upstream's own rendering of ordinary arithmetic is not a judgment call, and `subright` is already a rule-group name, so `option togglerule subright off` reproduces the pre-fix output for bisection | **0/675 PARITY OK** with no re-pin (restoring upstream behavior moves no upstream assertion) and stages 364/364 with no per-test opt-out — the stages delta is purely the 8 new keys. Across 7 whole-binary `decompile-all --mode reliable` runs (x86-64 -O0/-O2 and ARM Cortex-M: openssh `ssh`, iproute2 `ip`, e2fsprogs `e2fsck`, `gzip`, `bzip2`, betaflight STM32F405, cleanflight DALRCF405 — 14,720 functions common to both panes) nonzero-offset raw `SUBnn` operators go **1,912 → 95** (95.0%), the functions carrying one go 733 → 20, and the blast radius is 728 functions (4.95%) with emitted lines 443,737 → 443,785 (+0.011%). Every one of the 95 residuals is a case upstream's own guards decline: 42 address-tied global-array stores (round 1's separately-filed `rodata-phantom-store`), 47 address-tied local partial-symbol writes (the P6 thread that renders `sym._2_2_` upstream) and 6 piece-structured field extractions on the special-print branch.
