Measured cost: exactly two functions in 14,720 gain a dead partial-register shift statement — `(undefined7)((uint8)a2 >> 8)` in bzip2 `sub_da00` and one `(undefined3)((uint4)v11 >> 8)` on ARM — the `SUB87`/`SUB43` form upstream lowers the same way (corpus-wide count of that shape 0 → 2). GED is unchanged by construction (`O2-noinline-iproute2-ip-netns_add` 8.0 → 8.0): the rewrite changes expressions inside blocks and moves no block. Interleaved medians, `decompile-all --mode reliable`: openssh `ssh` @0x4cd60 2,307 → 2,165 ms (-6.16%, 9 pairs), gzip -O0 3,387 → 3,325 ms (-1.84%, 7), bzip2 -O2 12,016 → 11,512 ms (-4.20%, 7), e2fsck -O2 21,378 → 21,430 ms (+0.24%, 5) — worst case +0.24%, inside the +5% budget. `tests/stages/ghdec-subright.xml` (1 of 8 assertions passes pre-fix, 8/8 post-fix) |
| `cspecprotos` (P4 prototype-model registry, the cspec's named models) | `Architecture::build_default_proto` read exactly one cspec element, `<default_proto><prototype>`, where the C++ `parseCompilerConfig` (`architecture.cc:1253/1280-1284/1310`) also dispatches `<prototype>`, `<resolveprototype>` and `<modelalias>` (`ProtoModel::decode`, the marshaling-decoder entry point, is a hard `Err` stub). The per-program model registry therefore held **exactly one** model, so `getModel("__thiscall")` could never resolve, `option defaultprototype __thiscall` failed with "Unknown prototype model" on every target, and kuna's fully-ported `this`-pointer machinery (`ProtoModel::hasThisPointer`, `FuncProto::hasThisPointer`/`updateThisPointer`/`getThisPointerStorage`, `ParameterPieces::isThisPointer` — driven from `ActionInputPrototype`, consumed by P5 type inference) was permanently dead: nothing could set `hasThis`. `ProtoModelMerged` was likewise ported and unit-tested with no live producer, because its only producer is `<resolveprototype>`. The named models are now decoded through the *same* body as the default one (so they carry identical `<pentry>` storage and `<unaffected>`/`<killedbycall>`/`<returnaddress>`/`<internal_storage>` fidelity) plus the `hasthis`/`constructor` attributes and upstream's `if (name == "__thiscall") hasThis = true` override (`fspec.cc:2595`); a `<resolveprototype>` folds its constituents into a `ProtoModelMerged` and finalizes the merged input list, and a `<modelalias>` registers an `isCompatible` named copy. Upstream's post-parse invariant "we must have a `__thiscall` calling convention" (architecture.cc:1342) is honored too, cloning one off the default on the many languages whose spec declares none, so `getModel("__thiscall")` resolves everywhere. Deviation from upstream, deliberate: a named model that fails to decode is **skipped** rather than aborting the spec, because kuna's vendored cspec corpus spans every processor it ships and one undecodable named model must not cost the architecture its default one. **Registration only** — `<eval_current_prototype>`/`<eval_called_prototype>` are still ignored (honoring them changes which model every function on x86win/x86gcc is evaluated with, which is a default change and not part of registration), and nothing assigns `__thiscall` automatically | **No output shift at all**, which is the point: 675/675 PARITY OK with no re-pin, stages 400/400 (delta is purely the 6 new keys), and 12 whole-binary `decompile-all` panes (73,173 lines) byte-identical before vs after across four compiler specs, both arms captured on the same rebased tree (x86-64-gcc ELF `ls`/`grep`/`gzip`/`du`/`xxd`/a g++ vtable demo/the DIV-60 `cppproto_x86_64` fixture; x86win PE32 `msvc_rtti_x86.exe`/`pe_imports.exe`/`pe_dwarf.exe`/`pdb_prog.exe`; x86-64-win `msvc_rtti_x64.exe`). Registry census over every `language:compiler` pair kuna ships: 294/294 bootstrap, pairs holding more than one model 0 -> 294, pairs where `getModel("__thiscall")` resolves 0 -> 294, models registered 294 -> 790 (110 cspecs declare 186 `<prototype>` elements; only the 110 inside `<default_proto>` were ever decoded, so 76 named models + 6 `<resolveprototype>` + 3 `<modelalias>` were dead spec text). Load time, the metric that matters since the extra elements are parsed once per program load: interleaved `kuna functions --json` minima at n=31 move +0.42% / +0.55% / +0.15% / -0.05% (`msvc_rtti_x86.exe`, `msvc_rtti_x64.exe`, `pe_imports.exe`, gzip) after the three separate cspec parses were folded into one; `decompile-all` worst case +0.89%. What changed is what is now *reachable*: on `x86:LE:32:default:windows` the registry holds `__stdcall` + `__cdecl` + `__fastcall` + `__thiscall` + the merged `__fastcall/__thiscall/__stdcall`, and `option defaultprototype __thiscall` turns the MSVC member function `int4 Box::vftable_0(void) { int4 v1; // ecx ... }` (a read-before-write of ECX — not valid C) into `int4 Box::vftable_0(int4 a0) { return *(int4 *)(a0 + 4) * *(int4 *)(a0 + 4); }` on the real `msvc_rtti_x86.exe` fixture. `tests/stages/ghdec-cspecprotos.xml` (two-pass, 6 assertions) + `decompiler/crates/kuna-decomp/tests/verify_cspecprotos.rs` (5 registry assertions over four real vendored cspecs) |
| `maxlennop` (instruction decode, the parser's 16-byte instruction window) | `ParserContext::getInstructionBytes`/`getInstructionBits` guard only the **start** byte of a read against `MAX_INSTRUCTION_LEN` (16) and then pull a fixed-width run from it, so a read can legitimately run off the end of the buffer: C++ `PatternBlock::isInstructionMatch` walks a candidate pattern in `sizeof(uintm)` chunks whatever the pattern's real width, so matching an instruction **at** the 15-byte x86 maximum issues a 4-byte read at offset 13. Upstream reads whatever `ParserContext` memory follows `buf` (UB) and the caller's mask discards it; the kuna port turned the same read into a hard `BadData` (`"Instruction byte read past buffer (C++ UB)"`), so `FlowInfo` planted an artificial halt at that address and truncated the function. The witness is clang's 15-byte loop-alignment NOP `66 66 66 66 66 66 2e 0f 1f 84 00 00 00 00 00` (six operand-size prefixes + a CS override + `0f 1f /4` with SIB and disp32), routine `-O2` padding ahead of a loop head: everything past it — the loop, the stores, and any parameter only the loop read — vanished, and on a shrink-wrapped function the truncated CFG restructured into an inverted empty guard (GH-271). The tail bytes are outside the matcher's mask and cannot affect the verdict, so they now read as 0, the deterministic equivalent of the garbage C++ reads. The start-byte guard is untouched, so a genuinely over-length encoding still raises the instruction-length error | **Invisible to every existing gate and to decbench**: 675/675 PARITY OK with no re-pin, `make rust-test` green, and all 791 ELFs in the decbench corpus are gcc-built and contain **zero** 15-byte NOPs (which is why the bug survived the whole campaign). Measured on a real clang-built binary, `/usr/bin/FileCheck-14` (528 KB, 1,155 functions, 122 instances of the padding): 46 functions (4.0%) change, **all 46 grow, none shrink**, emitted lines 66,420 -> 70,243 (+5.8%), function list byte-identical (discovery is unaffected). 26 of the 46 (+1,497 lines) are straight recovery of code that was silently missing — e.g. `sub_40e6e0` 58 -> 214 lines, regaining the whole `operator new` reallocation branch of a `std::vector` growth path. The other 20 (+2,326 lines) are a **pre-existing gap this fix un-masks**, not a new defect: `noreturn_disc`'s evidence tally counts a call site whose fall-through "is not a valid instruction start", and a *failed decode* satisfied that test, so clang's post-call alignment padding was silently voting `std::__throw_length_error` no-return. With the padding decoding, the tally drops below its 3-site floor and the dead code after that genuinely-non-returning libstdc++ helper reappears; whole-binary `// no-return` annotations 94 -> 83, and **every one of the 20 hangs on that single callee**. The real fix is the vendored `ElfFunctionsThatDoNotReturn` list (which is Ghidra's own, and shares the gap) not covering the `std::__throw_*` family — filed as GH-273, deliberately not folded in here. Speed: interleaved min-of-5 `decompile-all` on gzip 14.45s -> 14.65s (+1.4%, inside run-to-run spread; means 15.26s -> 14.91s). `tests/stages/gh271-x86-maxlen-nop.xml` (5 assertions) + `decompiler/crates/kuna-sleigh/tests/x86_maxlen_nop_regression.rs` (the 15-byte decode and the surviving over-length guard) |
| `arraysubfield` (P9 emission, the partial-symbol walk's routing gate) | A partial access into a plain **array**-typed symbol never entered `PrintC::pushPartialSymbol`. Upstream `pushSymbolDetail` (`printlanguage.cc:256-258`) sends every partial cover of a mapped Symbol into that walk and lets the walk's per-type arms choose the token; the port routed STRUCT and UNION in but split ARRAY off into a dedicated branch (`printc.rs:7241`) that computes `index = symboloff / elementAlignSize` and emits `name[index]` **without reading the access size**. An eight-byte write at offset 0 of an `undefined1[16]` therefore rendered `v1[0] = a0` -- a statement that names one `char` and so claims a one-byte store -- and at offset 8, `v1[8] = 0`, the ninth byte. The walk already had the correct behavior on both sides: its ARRAY arm carries upstream's `TypeArray::getSubEntry` guard (`printc.rs:6620`, `newoff + size <= elementAlignSize`), so a genuine in-element access keeps its subscript, and anything spanning elements falls to the artificial `unnamedField` member (`printc.rs:6720`) that renders `v1._0_8_` -- Ghidra's own notation, and one kuna emits constantly for struct and scalar symbols (141 sites in one interp-bee run) but had never once emitted on an array. Fix: admit plain `TYPE_ARRAY` to the routing gate. | Reported as the 128-bit register-pair return (`interp-bee` `eval_result_noun` @ `0xa3e0`, PR #288): P5 types a `CONCAT88` return `undefined1[16]` (no integer primitive past `max_basetype_size`, upstream) and P6 merges both halves into it, so the two eight-byte half-writes were the size-blind case. Measured over **14,080 functions** in 12 binaries (`tests/bug-repro/*`, four `/usr/bin`, two b-chall): **308 functions / 4,953 lines** change, and every one is accounted for -- 4,937 lines are a subscript that spanned elements becoming the sized member, 13 are a within-element truncation gaining the explicit `(char)v7[0]` cast the walk's `finalcast` arm emits, 3 are a bare array name used as a scalar operand becoming `v7[0]._2_2_`. **Zero** genuine `arr[N]` in-element renders move, here or in the 675 datatest assertions (unchanged, `docs/baseline.json` untouched). The `._<off>_<size>_` token went from **0** renders on a declared array local across all 14,080 functions to 4,509 in 220 of them. Four existing stage assertions and two `kuna-console` integration-test pins moved, every one of them a pin on the defective spelling (`ghdec-returncopysplit` #1/#5/#6, `kuna-retinputhalf` #2, `kuna-rustabi` #5, `verify_return_uncomputed`, `verify_rustabi_pair`) -- the min=0 arm of `verify_return_uncomputed` was widened to reject BOTH spellings so the repair cannot make it vacuous; `kuna-rustabi` is the clearest witness, where a one-byte discriminant write stays `v2[0] = a0 < 0xb;` in the same function whose 4- and 7-byte writes become `v2._8_4_` / `v2._1_7_`. NOT fixed: `return v1 << 0x40;` -- shifting the whole container is upstream-faithful and still not valid C. `tests/stages/kuna-arraysubfield.xml`; corpus file count 216 -> 217. |
| `jtsharepartial` (P2 jump-table recovery, the partial sub-decompilation's scope) | Recovering a BRANCHIND means cloning the function's raw p-code into a partial `Funcdata`, building its blocks and running the reduced `"jumptable"` action set over it so the switch's index calculation simplifies into something the emulator can walk. C++ `FlowInfo::recoverJumpTables` (`flow.cc:1429`) builds **one** `partial` per call and `Funcdata::stageJumpTable` (`funcdata_block.cc:512`) guards the clone plus the reduced pipeline behind `if (!partial.isJumptableRecoveryOn())`, so upstream pays for that sub-decompilation once per function however many BRANCHINDs it holds; kuna paid it **once per table**. The cost is linear in the number of switches: on the RE-friction round-2 witness `nikos_crack_me.exe` `sub_140023350` (two tables) it was 3,077 ms of action time plus 464 ms of cloning inside an 11 s decompile. The correctness half is the bigger one. Because kuna's later table cloned the jumpvec *after* an earlier sibling had recovered, that clone's `collect_edges` iterated the sibling's case targets and called `target()` on a case body decoded only into the PARENT flow, threw `"Could not find op at target address"`, and truncated a perfectly recoverable dispatch to a computed `(code *)()` call. `option unrolledguard` (DIV-13, default off) exists **only** to tolerate that self-inflicted condition; upstream never meets it because its one shared partial collects edges while every sibling table is still empty. Restoring the upstream shape removes the cause instead of the symptom. Shipped as a settable (`on|off`, default **on**) rather than a bare fix because `unrolledguard`'s per-table re-clone is the thing being removed, so that option needs `jtsharepartial off` to keep working | **0/675 PARITY OK, `docs/baseline.json` unmoved**; `make test-stages` **PARITY OK 610/610** (609 -> 610; `tests/stages/ghangr-optimized-memcpy-6301a9.xml` grows a third pass proving the shipped default recovers the same 16 interleaved dispatches with `unrolledguard` OFF: `code **` stays 7 across all three passes, `switch(` 17 -> 33). **Speed**: the witness `kuna decompile nikos_crack_me.exe sub_140023350` measured by interleaved paired A/B against `--option jtsharepartial off`, 8 pairs, 10,869 ms -> **9,138 ms median, -15.92%** (paired mean -15.96% +/- 2.03, 8/8 pairs a win, ~7.8 sigma), which takes RE-need `decompiling-3396-byte-main` under its 10,000 ms acceptance bar for the first time in five attempts (71.5 -> 19.4 -> 14.4 -> 11.8 -> 10.8 -> 9.1 s). **Output blast radius, whole-surface `decompile-all` A/B over 100 binaries / 21,458 functions** (the 6 round-2 probe binaries, 9 system ELFs, the 83 `kuna-analysis` fixtures spanning ELF/PE/Mach-O/COFF and x86-64/i386/ARM/Cortex-M, plus `betaflight_STM32F405.elf`): **62 functions differ, 61 are recovery GAINED, 1 is neither, and 0 are losses.** Aggregate over the differing set: **68 truncated `(code *)()` jump-as-call dispatches removed, +237 `switch` statements, +14,770 `case` arms**, with `unrolledguard` OFF throughout. Largest movers: glibc's `__memcpy_ssse3_back` (1 -> 36 switches, 144 -> 4,384 cases), `__vfprintf_internal` (6 -> 16), `_nl_load_domain` (13 truncated dispatches -> 0), `KeyVal2.exe` `sub_442390` (1 -> 15) -- the MSVC optimized-memcpy shape in the wild. Every one of the 62 was classified; the classifier keys functions by NAME, so `mcount_x86_64` was re-run keyed by ADDRESS and reports 19 differing rather than 18 (two distinct `printf_positional` bodies collapse under one name), also all gains. Bundle: `docs/features/decompiling-3396-byte-main/` |

## Upstream provenance & sync

- Upstream: https://github.com/NationalSecurityAgency/ghidra.git — pinned port anchor:
  GHIDRA_REV=cef869af04c4740a71ad31a55704045b1b0d1644 (2026-06-01). Updated automatically
  by `tools/sync_upstream.py` on a successful sync; keep the `GHIDRA_REV=<hex>` token on
  one line — the script greps for it.
- Still vendored (the only paths the sync script maps):
  `Ghidra/Features/Decompiler/src/decompile/datatests/` → `tests/datatests/` and
  `Ghidra/Processors/<P>/data/languages/` → `specs/Ghidra/Processors/<P>/data/languages/`.
  The C++ source (`decompiler/cpp/`, `unittests/`) was removed after the port was proven.
- Sync: `python3 tools/sync_upstream.py --ghidra <checkout> --to origin/master [--dry-run]`,
  then `make test` (675/675 PARITY OK), review, commit. Plain `git apply` first, `--3way`
  fallback; hard-fails on renames/binary diffs (handle manually). The DIV-2 re-pinned
  datatest files (15 files / 22 assertions) no longer clean-apply — expect `--3way` there.
  If a sync intentionally changes the corpus, re-pin with
  `kuna test --datatests --save-baseline docs/baseline.json` and say so in the commit;
  never re-pin to absorb an unexplained regression.
- Local-modification policy: byte-identical to upstream until 2026-06-06 (GH-558); since
  then kuna intentionally diverges — it is its own decompiler, not a tracking mirror. New
  logic goes in `kuna_*` modules; edits to ported-core files stay minimal and carry a
  `// (kuna)` marker. The per-file divergence table for the retired C++ tree (71 rows) is
  in git history of `docs/UPSTREAM.md`.

## Retired campaign notes (what the deleted docs recorded)

- **Stage-model study** (`docs/history/stage-model.md` + `evidence/`): the P0+S1–S9 model
  was synthesized from 10 structured readers over Ghidra/angr/Reko (~168 pipeline steps,
  ~80 feedback loops, ~98 decision points), 3 competing taxonomies, and 2 adversarial
  critiques; it killed 4 "phantom stages" of the older 19-stage guess. Empirical critique
  over the first 10 issue fixes: symptom→sub-stage navigation 7/10 clean; ablations sized
  each option's blast radius and justified the DIV-2/DIV-3 default flips.
- **Issue campaign** (`fixed-public-bugs.md`, `issue-coverage.md`): 58 public Ghidra
  issues reproduced, 37 fixed (17 bug-fixes / 20 decision points); 5 options deliberately
  left opt-in as destructive-by-default (`returnpair`, `v850indirectbranch`,
  `switchmodbound`, `stackalias`, `sparcstructret`).
- **Analysis-tier port** (`analysis-port-*.md`): plan generated 2026-06-22 by a 13-agent
  workflow, executed as 62 logged increments; Ghidra ships 142 analyzers, ~57
  decompiler-relevant — every feasible one ported, plus the Listing tier, multi-format
  loaders (6 ELF chokepoints → `ObjectFormat` trait), kuna-native FID (`.fid`, not
  Ghidra's non-redistributable `.fidb`), and RTTI/Obj-C/PDB metadata (Swift deferred:
  no Rust demangler existed).
- **Region structurer** (`region-structurer-roadmap.md`, `regions.md`): the angr
  Phoenix/SAILR structurer roadmap that became DIV-12/DIV-14; deep goto-win schemas
  remain deferred.
